Search power is the limit: cut real tablets are not found either. | Jigsaw by arithmetic: NOT SUPPORTED at k>=2 (saturated, no power). Next: (2) the summary-line form with multi-item donors only and a global annealed assembly; (3) attribute test (scribe, findspot, words) that the shuffle cannot fake, and a within-tablet bootstrap null. |
| la12:LA-12.2a | Summary-line jigsaw. Targets = all 889 HT quantities (entries and totals). Donors = 274 MULTI-item sums of other sides (section or whole side, per tag or all); single-item blocks excluded, so two equal single numbers never count. Hit = target equals one donor (k1) or two donors from distinct sides (k2). Vector arithmetic (fraction signs counted, not valued). | 1,497 k1 hits on 435 targets; 60 targets have exactly one k1 donor; 17,482 k2 hits. | k2 saturated again; only unique k1 hits carry information. |
| la12:LA-12.2b | Global assembly: simulated annealing (8 restarts x 60k moves) chooses hits so that each side donates at most once and each line is used once (a list goes to one ledger). Weight = 1/(number of alternative hits for that line), so unique closures count 1. Nulls: N1 entry numbers shuffled across sides within tag; N3 (new) each entry redrawn from its OWN side's entries (same tag, with replacement: keeps every tablet's magnitude, roundness and fraction habits). 40 runs each, identical annealing. | Real: score 42.9, 79 joins (26 unique). vs N1: score 43.9+-3.1 (P=0.68); vs N3: 36.5+-2.8 (P=0.049). Raw hit counts disagree between nulls (real >> N1 but << N3), so only the weighted assembly score is used. | Nulls disagree: at N1 level, borderline vs N3. Not significant overall. |
| la12:LA-12.2c | Positive control PL: 10 random HT sides have their whole sum written as a new line on another random HT side; 2 seeds, 20 null runs each. Recall = planted pair chosen by the annealer. | Recall 5/10 and 10/10 (all unique planted lines chosen). Score +5 to +6 over the unplanted corpus: vs N3 P=0.048 (both seeds, floor of 20 runs); vs N1 P=0.10-0.14. | PASS for recall when the planted value is unique; aggregate power is marginal (10 plants = about 2 null SD). |
| la12:LA-12.2d | Positive control LB: 205 random Pylos + Knossos documents, unplanted and with 10 planted summary lines; 2 seeds. | Unplanted LB already shows huge raw excess over N1 (401 vs 35 k1 hits: repeated values in D-series sheep records). Planted recall 5/10 and 5/10; score vs N1 P=0.048 and 0.29; vs N3 P=0.19 and 0.76. | Mixed: the assembly finds half the planted LB joins, but 10 joins do not reliably lift the aggregate score above null. |
| la12:LA-12.2e | Unique real joins (w = 1) worth a look, read on the transliteration. | HT 27a 'VIR+[?] 90 / 50' (140) = sum of ALL numbers on HT 27b (7+1+14+4+70+4+40 = 140, same scribe 11, same tablet), but side b mixes VIN, SA and RE counts, so the sum has no economic meaning. HT 116a GRA lines sum to 109 (its own KU-RO on side b says 100) = HT 1 KU-PA3-NU 109; HT 116a's first GRA line is KU-PA-JA. HT 122b KU-RO 65 = whole of HT 118; HT 94a KU-RO 110 = whole of HT 86a; HT 9a KU-RO 31 = HT 93a GRA. | Each is grade C: lines with a unique donor number 60 in real vs 61.9+-18.9 under N3 (P=0.54), so unique joins as a class are at chance. Would support: a photo or GORILA reading that ties HT 1 and HT 116 (findspot, hand, or the KU-PA- name); would kill: the HT 116a GRA sum is 100 once damaged signs are read. |
| la12:LA-12.2 | Cycle verdict | Assembly recovers planted unique summaries, but real HT does not beat both nulls. | Summary-line jigsaw NOT SUPPORTED (P 0.05-0.68 depending on null). Cycle 3: test the joins with attributes the number shuffle cannot fake. |
| la12:LA-12.3a | Cycle-1 statistics against N3 (each entry redrawn from its own side's entries, same tag, with replacement; 100 runs). Planted cuts (2 seeds) re-tested against N3. | Real: L_k2 216.4 vs 215.7+-2.0 (P=0.36), L_k3 400.7 vs 404.6 (P=1.0), H_k1 P=0.20, H_c1 P=0.74. Per target: 0 of 139 at P<=0.01 (1.4 expected), 3 at P<=0.05 (7.0 expected). Planted targets vs N3: P 0.61-1.0 on every statistic. | The cycle-1 L_k2/L_k3 excess over N1 was tablet magnitude, not joins: GONE. N1 is anti-conservative and N3 conservative for raw counts (bootstrap repeats values), so the truth is bracketed and real HT sits inside the bracket. |
| la12:LA-12.3b | Attribute test. All joins found in real HT (J1 total = block, 152 pairs; JC completion, 63; JS summary line = multi-item sum, 1,089) vs joins found by the same search in N1 and N3 corpora (100 runs each). Shuffling keeps scribe, findspot and words, so an excess of same-scribe, same-findspot, same-tablet (two sides), GORILA-number neighbours (+-5) or shared words would mean real numbers link related tablets. | vs N3: same scribe 0.078 (null 0.080, P=0.63); same findspot 0.306 (0.295, P=0.10); same tablet 0.004 (0.003, P=0.15); near number 0.096 (0.084, P=0.02); shared word 0.029 (0.032, P=0.82). vs N1: same scribe P=0.05, near number P=0.02, others P>=0.36. 40 tests in all; the two P=0.02 are expected by chance (2 at 0.05). | Joined tablets are NOT more often same hand, same room or same vocabulary than chance joins. No join set is supported. |
| la12:LA-12.3c | Word roles from joins (la12_c3b.py). For each word: its count as the label of a summary line (value = multi-item sum of another tablet) and as the heading of a donor tablet, as a share of all JS joins, vs N3 (200 runs); Holm over 195 words. Planted control: 10 random lines relabelled TEST-PLANT and set to the whole sum of another random tablet. | Real: no word survives (min Holm P = 1.0). Tops: TA-RI-NA line label 8 vs 2.7 (share P=0.005), QE-TI as donor heading 50 vs 18 (share P=0.01): both single-tablet effects. Planted: TEST-PLANT 23 vs 18.2 (share P=0.01, Holm 1.0), so the test could not find even a planted role; one control word (PA-DA-SU-TI, 3 vs 0.5) passed Holm by small-count noise. (An earlier N1 comparison made SA-RA2, MA-DI, KU-RO look enriched; that was the overall join-count difference and is withdrawn.) | Word roles from joins: NOT FOUND, and the method has no power for them (planted control fails). No word gets a 'total', 'deficit' or 'transfer' role from this strategy. |
| la12:LA-12.3d | Other sites. Khania (KH, tablets) and Zakros (ZA) with the cycle-1 (N1, 60 runs) and cycle-2 (N1 and N3, 30 runs) statistics. | KH: no KU-RO/KI-RO targets; summary-line assembly score 8.5 vs N1 8.6 (P=0.52), N3 8.6 (P=0.55). ZA: 1 target; assembly score 5.75 vs 5.3 (P=0.39) and 6.4 (P=0.84). | No cross-tablet ledger structure at KH or ZA either (small samples, low power). |
| la12:LA-12.3 | Cycle verdict | Nothing the shuffle cannot fake: same hand, room, tablet or words are at chance among joined tablets. | The jigsaw hypothesis gives no reading. |

| la10:LA-10.final | Breed the language: GA over ~110,000 rule sets of artificial languages (syllable shape, harmony, OCP, prefixing/suffixing, root length, stems), spelled by Linear B rules, scored by synthetic likelihood on the la5 fingerprint + length/affix/Kober/inventory statistics; length-preserving ablation. Controls: 3 planted languages, Linear B (must be Greek-like), shuffled LA (must be rule-less), non-HT hold-out, spelling variants (loops/la10_cycle1-3.txt, la10_final.txt). | Planted rules recovered; LB recovered as suffix-dominant without harmony (v2; v1 failed), but not its cluster spelling; shuffled LA rule-less in v2. LA: prefixes and suffixes both required at similar rates (swap +4.5 to +31 vs LB +53/+73), no clusters (7/7), no harmony (7/7), o-poor a-i-u(-e) vowels; OCP sub-threshold; vowel dissimilation killed by the shuffle control; spelling convention undecidable (LB control fails). | B: balanced prefix+suffix, no clusters, no harmony, small o-poor vowel set. C: OCP, consonant stems. Typology (WALS, Grambank; data only): closer to prefix+suffix languages (Sumerian, Akkadian, NW Caucasian, Basque) than to suffix-only Hurrian/Luwian/Greek, weakly. Not cracked. |
| la10:LA-10.1a | Planted control PLANT1 (harmonic copy 0.5, identity-OCP 0.8, suffixing 0.5, no prefixes, no clusters, open syllables). GA from random start, same budget as LA. | Best 72.9 vs truth genome 102.5 (GA beat its own generator on this lexicon). Recovered: ocp_id 0.78 (truth 0.80), pre_p 0.01 (0), cluster 0.01 (0), onset0_med 0.04 (0.05), suf_p 0.38 (0.5). Required by ablation: harmony +47.8, ocp_id +15.8, suffixing +173.8, swap prefix/suffix +206.8, reuse +25.5; not required: prefixing, clusters, final vowel. One false positive: medial codas 0.34 (truth 0.10), ablation +24.8 (echo from obstruent codas mimics harmony). Unrecoverable gene coda_fin wanders (0.13, truth 0.5), as designed. | PASS (harmony, OCP, suffixing, no clusters recovered; codas over-called). |
| la10:LA-10.1b | Planted control PLANT2 (prefixing 0.45, obstruent+sonorant clusters 0.3, place-OCP 0.6, final-vowel restriction 0.6, no harmony). | Best 68.9 vs truth 72.6. Recovered cluster 0.34 (0.30), pre_p 0.48 (0.45), root_len 2.45 (2.5), harm_copy 0.05 (0). Required: clusters +293, prefixing +123, swap +86.8, hiatus +24, reuse +21; harmony and OCP not required (correct for harmony; place-OCP at 0.6 is invisible also in the truth's own ablation, -5.9). False positive: suffixing +20.4 (suf_p 0.18, truth 0.05). Missed: final-vowel restriction (+9.7; truth +33.9). | PASS on clusters, prefixing vs suffixing, no harmony. Place-OCP of 0.6 is below the method's power. |
| la10:LA-10.1c | Positive control LB (Greek). Should recover suffixing, clusters / codas (dead-vowel echo), no harmony. | FAIL. Best 139.3 (poor fit: vinit 0.075 vs 0.348, pre/suf extension 0.08/0.11 vs 0.017/0.029, cecho 0.79 vs 0.41). Degenerate solution: 1-syllable roots (root_len 1.04) wrapped in prefixes (0.46) AND suffixes (0.66); clusters +14 and codas +39 below threshold (noise 15.6); swap prefix/suffix +17 (no asymmetry seen). A hand-built Greek-like v1 genome scores 268-522, worse: v1 cannot express Greek. Diagnosis: v1 has only vowel-final roots plus optional fixed affix strings; Greek inflection is an OBLIGATORY ending resyllabified onto a CONSONANT stem (do-ro + -o -> CV sign), with consonant-sharing final alternations (Kober). | Model space too narrow: the LB control fails, so v1 verdicts on LA morphology are void. Fixed in v2 (cycle 2). |
| la10:LA-10.1d | Negative control LAshuf (LA signs shuffled across types; lengths and unigrams kept, rules destroyed). | Best 134.8. Rule genes flat: harmony -1.2, OCP -2.8, clusters 0.0, codas -1.3, final vowel +4.8, swap +13.7 (all below threshold 22). BUT prefixing and suffixing 'required' (+583,446 / +167,058): the same 1-syllable-root basin (root_len 1.04), where removing affixes leaves too few distinct words. | Flat on every phonological rule (PASS). The affix ablation is an artefact of the monosyllabic-root basin, not morphology: it fires on a rule-less target. |
| la10:LA-10.1e | Linear A, two GA seeds (v1). | Best 83.9 / 77.6. Both: no clusters (0.00 / 0.00), no harmony (copy 0.05 / 0.01; ablation +0.4 / +2.3), roots 1.15 / 1.41 syllables, prefix and suffix both ~0.2-0.26, swap prefix/suffix +1.8 / -0.5 (no asymmetry). Identity-OCP 0.71 (+14.5, just under threshold 17.8) in s1 and the cecho fit good (0.615 vs 0.636); s2 OCP 0.28 and cecho misfit (1.075, z +5.6). Unfittable in v1: LA's vowel echo is BELOW the shuffle (0.90 / 0.94) and the gaps are low; no v1 gene can produce vowel dissimilation, so the GA settles at ~1.0-1.5. | Robust across seeds and controls: no onset clusters, no vowel harmony. Prefix/suffix: no asymmetry, but LB control failed so not interpretable. OCP: present in one seed only. Model gap found: vowel dissimilation. |
| la10:LA-10.1f | Length-preserving ablation (tools/la10_ablate2.py): removing an affix adds its syllables to the root; 12 common seeds; also harm_copy and ocp_id alone. Same on all six v1 runs. | Affixes still 'required' on the SHUFFLED target (prefix +128, suffix +115, threshold 21): reused fixed strings fit the slot distributions whatever the language, so affix presence is undecidable here. Prefix/suffix SWAP is the usable item: PLANT1 +215, PLANT2 +97 (planted asymmetry seen), LAshuf +1.3, LA +5.1 / -1.6, LB +10.6 (control fail). Identity-OCP: LA +15.7 / +10.3, PLANT1 (truth 0.8) +17.9, LAshuf -5.6, LB +2.8. Copy-harmony: LA +5.1 / +6.7, PLANT1 +19.6, LAshuf 0. | LA identity-OCP is as strong as a planted 0.8 OCP and absent from the shuffled target (grade B pending v2). LA shows no prefix/suffix asymmetry; uninterpretable until the LB control passes. |
| la10:LA-10.1 | Cycle verdict | Planted controls pass; shuffled control flat on phonology; LB control fails (v1 cannot write Greek inflection). | Kept: Linear A needs no clusters and no harmony (both controls agree these are visible). Void: morphology (affix ablation fires on the shuffled target). Next: v2 with consonant stems + obligatory endings, vowel dissimilation (harm_copy < 0), Kober statistic; PLANT3 Greek-like planted control. |
| la10:LA-10.2a | Planted control PLANT3 (Greek-like). | Best 101.7 vs truth 150.5. Recovered suf_p 0.93 (truth 0.95), cluster 0.21 (0.20), root_len 1.49 (2.0), stem_c 0.33 (0.70, under-recovered). Length-fixed ablation: suffixing +627, prefixing +9.8 (correct: no prefixes), swap +422, clusters +42.6, codas +46.2, harmony not required, stem_c +18.3 (below threshold 27). False genes not required: harm_fb 0.58, fv_str 0.68. | PASS on suffix-only morphology, clusters and codas, no harmony. Consonant stems are below the method's power. |
| la10:LA-10.2b | Positive control LB (Greek) in v2. | Best 143.8 (fit still poor: cluster echo before R/N bred 1.18 vs 1.82; extension shares too high). Length-fixed ablation: suffixing +157 (required), prefixing +28.5 (not, threshold 39), swap +53 (suffix orientation required), harmony none (harm_copy -0.11, +6). Clusters +3.8 and codas +12.2 NOT recovered; stem_c 0.32 (-4.3). A Greek-like start hill-climbed on LB stays worse (219 vs 144): the cluster spelling cannot be bred in this model on the real Greek lexicon. | PARTIAL PASS: Greek is recovered as suffixing, not prefixing, without harmony. Cluster/coda spelling is not recovered from real Greek, so 'no clusters' from the GA alone is not calibrated on a real language. |
| la10:LA-10.2c | Negative control LAshuf in v2. | Best 101.8. Not required: prefixing +6.2, suffixing +19.9 (threshold 35.8), swap +3.3, OCP +10.7, clusters +0.1, stem_c +8.6. But the GA chose vowel DISSIMILATION (harm_copy -0.42; flipping it to harmony costs +43.9) and hiatus (+60.3): both fire on a rule-less target. | The v2 affix ablation is now clean (fixes LA-10.1d). Dissimilation and hiatus are null-model correctors (the generator's own vowel echo is above 1), not language features. |
| la10:LA-10.2d | Linear A in v2 (seed 1). | Best 77.7 (core fit: cecho 0.73 vs 0.64, vecho 1.07/1.09 vs 0.90/0.94, vinit 0.34 vs 0.20 the worst). Rules: cluster 0.00, harm_copy -0.54, harm_fb 0.54 (not required), ocp_id 0.82 (+13.8), ocp_place 0.07, pre_p 0.33, suf_p 0.35, root_len 1.45, stem_c 1.00 (top-20 0.80-1.00; +14.9, not required). Length-fixed ablation: prefixing +43.9, suffixing +98.5, both +119.9 (all required, threshold 19.6); swap +4.5 (no orientation). Dissimilation (flip +83.1) same sign as the shuffled control (+43.9). | Beyond the shuffled control: LA needs BOTH prefixes and suffixes (shuffle: neither), with no prefix/suffix asymmetry, unlike LB and PLANT3 (swap +53, +422). Same as FINDINGS 2f (prefix 83 / suffix 111 types, both z about 6). Dissimilation: killed by the control. Consonant stems: C (preferred, not required). |
| la10:LA-10.2e | Identity-OCP across all runs (ablation of ocp_id, length-fixed set). | LA: v1 s1 0.71 (+15.7), v1 s2 0.28 (+10.3), v2 0.82 (+13.8). Shuffled: v1 0.49 (-5.6), v2 0.11 (+8.1). PLANT1 (truth 0.8): +17.9. LB: 0.63-0.66 (+2.8 / +3.7). | LA's consonant-series avoidance (la5 0.63) is bred as a same-consonant restriction in 2 of 3 runs at about the planted-0.8 effect size, never on the shuffled target; but no single run crosses the 3x-noise threshold, and LB's stronger avoidance (0.41) is NOT bred as OCP. Grade C+ (direction consistent, power insufficient). |
| la10:LA-10.2 | Cycle verdict | Controls: planted Greek-like and real LB both come out suffixing-only; the shuffled target is now affix-free. | Linear A: prefixes and suffixes both required, no orientation (B). No vowel harmony of either kind (B; dissimilation killed by control). No onset clusters (B, from the direct statistic, not from the GA). OCP-identity C+. Consonant stems C. |
| la10:LA-10.3a | Replicate LA v2 with a new seed and a smaller budget. | Best 89.1. Length-fixed ablation: prefixing +21.3, suffixing +181.9, both +134.5 (required, threshold 15); swap +16.4; clusters +1.6 (cluster gene 0.01); harmony copy +10.3 (gene -0.60), front/back +0.3; ocp_id 0.51 (+8.0); stem_c 0.51 (+2.8); roots 1.03 syllables. | Replicates LA-10.2d: both affix sides required, no clusters, no harmony; OCP and consonant stems again sub-threshold. |
| la10:LA-10.3b | Site hold-out: non-Hagia-Triada LA types only (262; Khania, Zakros, Phaistos and others). Same GA, own calibration. | Best 67.7. Prefixing +41.0, suffixing +27.3 (both required), swap +6.1 (no orientation); clusters 0.01 (+0.2); harmony not required (+6.7 / +3.5); ocp_id 0.97 (+4.3); pre_p 0.47 >= suf_p 0.37. | The profile holds outside Hagia Triada (site-robust; same transcription conventions, so not independent replication). |
| la10:LA-10.3c | Spelling convention as a bred variable. Control: LB must fit best with its own (LB) spelling at equal budget. | LA: LB-spelling 89.1, drop 88.1, full 95.6 (12-seed rescored best; run-to-run spread at fixed spelling is about 6-11). LB: LB-spelling 134.3, drop 123.3 (seed noise 68.5 for the drop genome). | CONTROL FAILS: real Greek does not prefer its own spelling convention under this model, so the convention cannot be bred. LA's spelling of codas is undecided. Under all three spellings LA still needs both affix sides (drop: +37.6 / +83.2; full: +71.6 / +80.3) and no clusters or harmony. |
| la10:LA-10.3d | Orientation of affixation across all v2 runs (swap prefix and suffix rates and inventories; cost > threshold = the found orientation is required). | LB +53.1 / +73.4 and PLANT3 +421.6 (suffix-dominant, recovered); LAshuf +3.3. LA: +4.5, +16.4, +6.1 (non-HT), +31.1 (full), +10.8 (drop): 2 of 5 over threshold, all smaller than LB's. Prefix/suffix rate ratio pre_p/suf_p in the 7 LA runs 0.82-1.60 (v1 1.13, 0.90; v2 0.94, 1.60, non-HT 1.27, full 1.44, drop 0.82); LB 0.66-0.71 with suf_p 0.66-0.99; PLANT3 0.37. | Linear A is bred as BALANCED prefixing + suffixing (prefix rate about equal to suffix rate; orientation weak or absent), unlike the suffix-dominant LB Greek. Grade B. |
| la10:LA-10.3e | Vowel inventory as bred (vowel log-weights; the direct shares are data under LB-projected values). | O gets the lowest vowel weight in 7 of 7 LA runs (vw_O -0.04 to -1.8, 1.1 to 2.3 below A; LB runs +0.24 to +1.4); LA sign shares A 0.39, I 0.26, U 0.18, E 0.14, O 0.03 (LB: A 0.27, O 0.27, E 0.23, I 0.15, U 0.08). Shuffled LA keeps the unigrams, so this is not a rule test. | Under the conventional values LA behaves as a 3-4 vowel system (a i u, e weaker, o marginal). Grade B, with the backward-projection caveat (Steele & Meissner 2017). |
| la10:LA-10.3 | Cycle verdict | Replication, site hold-out and two alternative spellings give the same LA profile; the spelling-convention test fails its LB control. | Stable: both affix sides (balanced), no onset clusters, no vowel harmony, short roots, o-poor vowel system. Not identifiable: spelling convention, consonant-final stems, OCP strength. |
| la10:LA-10.typology | WALS v2020 CLDF (Dryer & Haspelmath, cldf-datasets/wals; features 2A, 12A, 26A, 27A, 51A) and Grambank v1 (Skirgard et al. 2023, grambank/grambank; prefix features GB079/090/092/094/430/431, suffix features GB080/089/091/093/432/433), downloaded 4 Oct 2026 to data/la10/wals, data/la10/grambank; tools/la10_typology.py -> data/la10/typology.txt. Data only; no proposed reading used. Hittite, Hattic, Elamite, Urartian, Ancient Egyptian are in neither database; WALS has no ancient language (modern relatives as proxies). | Balanced prefix + suffix (WALS 26A 'Equal'): NW Caucasian 3/3 (Abkhaz, Kabardian...), Basque 1/1, Semitic 3/12; Greek 'Strongly suffixing', Kartvelian 'Weakly suffixing', Nakh, Lezgic, Armenian, Turkic suffixing. Grambank (prefix / suffix features present): Sumerian 4/6 + 5/6, Akkadian 3/6 + 5/6, Phoenician 3/5 + 5/6, Basque 4/6 + 3/6; suffix-only: Hurrian 0/6 + 6/6, Hieroglyphic Luwian 0/6 + 4/6, Ancient Greek 1/6 + 4/6, Etruscan 0/6 + 1/6. Small vowel inventory (WALS 2A 2-4): NW Caucasian only; Greek, Semitic, Kartvelian, Basque, Lezgic 5-6; Turkic 7-14. Syllable structure (12A): every listed Aegean-Anatolian-Caucasian proxy is 'Complex' or 'Moderately complex' (modern languages). | As data, the bred profile (both affix sides, no harmony, small vowel system) sits with the prefix+suffix group (Sumerian, Akkadian/Phoenician, NW Caucasian, Basque) and away from the suffix-only Anatolian / Hurrian / Greek / Etruscan group; the small o-poor vowel set matches none of the WALS proxies except NW Caucasian. Weak: affix statistics of a name-heavy administrative vocabulary are not inflectional typology, 9 of 14 candidate languages are proxies or absent, and the vowel profile depends on LB-projected values. No family is supported or excluded. |
| la13:LA-13.final | Each site group (HT, KH, ZA, PH, KN, others pooled) treated as a language. (1) Exhaustive search of all clean cross-site one-sign-different pairs and every substitution/indel rule (position-specific and pooled), scored by independent pairs; (2) annealed sign maps aligning each site's sign contexts with the rest of Crete; (3) annealed 12 x 5 grid built from the substitution graph, held-out prediction; (4) scribes as idiolects. Nulls: site labels permuted over documents (plain, within support type), per-site bigram resampling, degree-preserving rewiring, shuffled pairs; max-statistic for search size. Controls: rules planted in Khania / Pylos / HT Scribe 9 (rate 1.0 and 0.5), unplanted LB KN vs PY, LA random splits, LB with known grid. | No rule beats any null: best HT KA <-> others JA (k 5, 2-sign words only, FWER P 0.07-0.17), equalled by random 2-way splits of LA; nothing at all on words of >= 3 signs. Planted rules: 0/18 (pair search, LA), 0/18 (LB LA-sized), 1/18 identified and 10/18 detected (sign-map anneal), 0/18 (scribes). Grid: full LB recovers partial rows (z 13.8, held-out beats shuffled pairs), LA-sized LB and LA do not (LA held-out 0.297-0.306 vs shuffled 0.287-0.312; post-hoc label ARI ~0). Side result, repeated in 3 groupings: one-sign near-variant words are site- and hand-local (fewer cross-group pairs than permuted labels, P <= 0.003). | NOT CRACKED; NO DIALECT. The decisive fact is power: sites share only 43 word types (scribes 26-30), so even an exceptionless planted sign substitution is invisible. Absence of regional sound laws is an upper bound only (< 3/18 recovery). No sign grid is recovered without outside values. Grades: KA<->JA as a regional rule C (would support: a 3+-sign pair with the same swap; would kill: already equalled by random splits - demoted to noise); -JA indel between HT scribes 8 and 9 C; locality of near-variant word families B. Next idea it suggests: since variant families are local, treat each site's family as one lexeme and look for cross-site matches of whole FAMILIES (shape of the variant set), not single words. |
| la13:LA-13.1a | Real search, word length >= 2 and >= 3. All 15 site pairs, ~2,100 clean pairs, every substitution and indel rule scored. | Length >= 2: 2,130 clean pairs; best rule HT KA <-> OTH JA, k = 5 (MI-KA/MI-JA, KA-SI/JA-SI, KA-KU/JA-KU, I-KA/I-JA, KA-TI/JA-TI); 11 rules with k >= 3, all HT-vs-OTH or HT-vs-KH on 2-sign words. Length >= 3: 113 clean pairs and NO substitution recurs even twice (max k = 1). | Every recurring rule lives on 2-sign words, where one-sign neighbours are abundant by chance. Nothing at length >= 3. |
| la13:LA-13.1b | Null N1: site labels permuted over documents (500); N1s: permuted within support type (500); N2: per-site bigram-resampled types, same counts and lengths (200). | Best rule k = 5: N1 P = 0.068, N1s P = 0.166, N2 P = 0.28. Rules with k >= 3: 11 vs N1 mean 10.5 (P = 0.46), N2 mean 14.9. Length >= 3: real max 1 vs null mean 1.4-1.8 (real is BELOW every null). Clean cross-site pairs: real 2,130 vs N1 2,504 (P(<=) < 0.002), N1s 2,330 (P(<=) ~ 0.02). | No rule beats any null after search-size correction (KA<->JA is the nearest, family-wise P 0.07-0.17: grade C at best, see 1d). Side result: real sites share FEWER one-sign neighbours than random document partitions, i.e. near-variant word families are site-local (a scribe/archive effect, not a dialect law). |
| la13:LA-13.1c | Positive control (power). Three random substitutions planted in Khania at rate 1.0 and 0.5 (3 draws each, signs of KH frequency 4-15); Linear B subsampled to LA type counts (KN 439, PY 87, TH 112, others 249) with 3 rules planted in PY (3 draws x 2 rates); 100 label permutations each. | LA planted: 0 of 18 planted rules recovered; best planted support k = 2 (rank 19-1,100), vs null 95% threshold 5. LB planted: 0 of 18 recovered (k <= 1). | The search has NO power at Linear A size: even a total, exceptionless sign substitution at one site is invisible, because the sites share almost no vocabulary (43 types). Absence of rules is therefore NOT evidence against dialects (< 3/18 power). |
| la13:LA-13.1d | Negative controls. LA split at random into two pseudo-sites (3 draws); LB unplanted LA-sized subsamples (3); full LB KN vs PY (unplanted, 30 permutations). | LA split: best k 4-6 vs its own null 95% = 6 (nothing). LB LA-sized: max k = 2, below null. Full LB KN vs PY: best rule (KN TA <-> PY absent) k = 11 vs null mean 12.3, 95% 15 (nothing). | Negatives behave. The real LA best rule (k = 5) is no stronger than what random 2-way splits of LA produce (4-6). KA<->JA demoted to noise; the 2-sign one-sign-neighbour space is saturated. |
| la13:LA-13.1 | Cycle verdict | Single-rule cognate-pair search: no rule beats nulls; but the positive control shows zero power at LA size, so the test cannot decide. | INCONCLUSIVE (no power). Next: a method that does not need shared words - align each site's sign CONTEXTS against the rest of the corpus (sign-level, all words count), annealed over sign maps. |
| la13:LA-13.2a | Real anneal for each of 6 groups (HT, KH, ZA, PH, KN, OTH as target). Null: 100 pseudo-sites per target from site labels permuted within support type (same number of documents, same support mix), same anneal. LB control: PY vs KN+TH (LA-sized subsample), 100 nulls. | Total map gain, real vs null: HT 1,414 vs 1,120 (P 0.11); KH 50 vs 112 (P 0.92); ZA 142 vs 49 (P 0.04); PH 54 vs 32 (P 0.15); KN 109 vs 36 (P 0.04); OTH 549 vs 436 (P 0.08). Best single entry beats null nowhere (P 0.11-0.90). LB PY: 122 vs 122 (P 0.45). | No group survives correction for 6 targets (best P 0.04 x 6 = 0.24). The overall tilt (5 of 6 above the null mean) says real sites are more self-contained than random document sets of the same kind, as in LA-13.1b: a vocabulary/archive effect, not a located sign law. Unplanted LB (one Greek dialect) shows nothing, as it should. |
| la13:LA-13.2b | Positive control: 3 substitutions planted at Khania (LA) or Pylos (LB) at rate 1.0 and 0.5, 3 draws each (18 rules per script). Recovery = the map sends the planted Y back to its true X; detection = Y is among the top-5 map entries. | Identification: LA 1/18, LB 5/18. Detection: LA 10/18 planted signs reach the top 5 (e.g. WI 62 bits, ZU 34 bits, NI 23 bits), LB 12/18. The map sends planted signs to frequent "absorber" signs (JA, DA, TA, TO, RO), not to their true source. | The method can FLAG a sign whose site use is anomalous (about 55% power at LA size) but cannot say what it corresponds to (identification <= 1/18 in LA, i.e. < 3/18 upper bound). So it cannot recover sound laws; it can only list site-anomalous signs. |
| la13:LA-13.2c | The real site-anomalous signs (top map entries), read only as flags. | ZA: JU, KO, SE, MU, DE, E, ME, PI; KN: NU, PI, KE, ZA, PA; PH: O, PA3; KH: *805, *352 (rare, also flagged in every planted run = artefact), U, E; OTH: TU, KA, *301, NU, TE, U. | Grade C flag only (would support: the same signs flagged by an independent method, e.g. cycle-3 grid or the rewiring null; would kill: flag lists as long and as strong in the null pseudo-sites, which they are: best-entry P 0.13-0.90). Effectively no finding. |
| la13:LA-13.2 | Cycle verdict | Sign-context alignment has detection power but no identification power at LA size; real sites are not more anomalous than random document sets after correction. | NO DIALECT RULE FOUND; the method is calibrated (planted detection ~55%), so at Khania (real best entry 14 bits vs planted 18-62 bits) a whole-site substitution of a mid-frequency sign is unlikely; elsewhere real flags sit inside the null range (weak upper bound, detection power only ~55%). |
| la13:LA-13.3a | Fit on the whole graph, 2 seeds; null: 20 degree-preserving rewirings each. Positive control: LB (DAMOS) all types >= 3 signs (3,568 pairs) and an LA-sized LB sample (1,000 types, 398 pairs). | Explained weight real vs rewired: LA_cross 0.448 vs 0.437 (P 0.05, z 1.8); LA_within 0.489 vs 0.488 (P 0.45); LA_all 0.414 vs 0.411 (P 0.30). LB_full 0.442 vs 0.384 (z 13.8); LB_small 0.660 vs 0.619 (z 4.0). | LB's alternations are grid-structured (and the fit shows a W row WE-WA-WI-U, an R row RO-RA-RI-RE, a vowel row E-O-I), but truth ARI is only 0.11 (rows ~ consonant) at full size and ~0 at LA size. LA cross-site edges are borderline above rewiring (z 1.8), within-site edges not at all. |
| la13:LA-13.3b | Held-out prediction: grid fitted on a random half of the word pairs, scored on the other half (4 splits). Null: the same with the Y side of the pairs shuffled (keeps sign frequencies) - this controls the hub effect that makes any fitted grid beat sign-permutation chance. | Held-out explained weight, real vs shuffled-pairs: LA_cross 0.297 vs 0.290; LA_within 0.298 vs 0.287; LA_all 0.306 vs 0.312; LB_full 0.347 vs 0.278; LB_small 0.299 vs 0.267 (chance ~0.255 for all). | Only full Linear B predicts held-out alternations beyond the frequency null. Linear A (cross, within, all) is indistinguishable from shuffled pairs. The LA-sized LB sample is marginal, so the power ceiling at LA size is low. |
| la13:LA-13.3c | Post-hoc, descriptive only: ARI of the LA grid rows/columns with conventional transliteration consonant/vowel (never used in fitting). | Cross: rows~C 0.008, cols~V 0.017; within: 0.0, 0.09; held-out fits: -0.04 to 0.11 for both LA and shuffled LA. | No part of a grid is recovered from Linear A substitutions, whether across sites (dialect hypothesis) or inside sites (inflection hypothesis). Agrees with LA-5.3d (no inflection grid). |
| la13:LA-13.3 | Cycle verdict | Grid construction works on full LB (positive control), not on LB at LA size, not on LA. | REJECTED at LA's power: cross-site substitutions do not tie signs into sound classes. Upper bound only. |
| la13:LA-13.4a | Cycle-1 search with scribes as groups; null: scribe labels permuted over documents, plain and within site (300 each). | All scribes: 386 types, only 30 shared by >= 2 scribes; best rule k = 2 (FWER P 0.63-0.72), 0 rules with k >= 3. HT scribes: 278 types, 26 shared; best rule HT Scribe 8 -JA <-> Scribe 9 absent (A-SE-JA / A-SE, *306-TU-JA / *306-TU), k = 2, FWER P 0.57. | Nothing beats the null. The final -JA indel is a known alternation (FINDINGS: A-/JA-), not a scribal law: grade C. |
| la13:LA-13.4b | Positive control: 3 rules planted in HT Scribe 9 (largest hand) at rate 1.0 and 0.5, 3 draws each, 100 permutations each. Negative: attributed HT docs split at random in two. | Planted: 0/18 recovered (best planted k = 2 vs null 95% = 2). Random split: max 1-2 vs null 95% 2-3. | Scribes share even less vocabulary than sites, so no power. |
| la13:LA-13.4c | Locality of near-variants: clean one-sign-different pairs between groups, real vs permuted labels. | Scribes: 515 vs 591 (P(<=) < 0.003 plain, 0.003 within site); HT scribes: 202 vs 256 (P(<=) 0.003 / < 0.003). Sites (LA-13.1b): 2,130 vs 2,504 (P(<=) < 0.002), within support type 2,330 (P(<=) ~ 0.02). | Repeated across 3 groupings: words that differ by one sign are written by the SAME hand and at the SAME site more often than chance, beyond the document level (documents stay whole in the null). Grade B structural fact: near-variant word families are local (archive/hand), not regional sound laws. |
| la13:LA-13.4 | Cycle verdict | No scribal spelling rule; the locality of near-variants is the only robust signal. | NO RULE; side finding B. |
| la15:LA-15.1a | Point estimates Chao1, ACE, Chao2, jackknife-2, sample coverage; parametric community bootstrap (1,000 refits). Same on LB. | LA words: Chao1 4,464 (boot 3,368-4,615), ACE 4,938, Chao2 4,506, jack2 2,398; sample coverage 0.39 (61% of the next word token would be a type never seen). LA signs: Chao1 171 (boot 151-197), ACE 169, coverage 0.994. LB words: Chao1 9,190, coverage 0.75. LB signs: Chao1 89.5 (89 seen). | Descriptive; calibration below decides how to read them. |
| la15:LA-15.1b | Planted control: 5 Zipf-Mandelbrot communities of known size (1,500-20,000 types) sampled at LA's size (1,282 tokens), 200 draws each; plus massive random guessing (ABC): 4,000 random communities (S, exponent, offset) per planted sample, nearest 80 kept. | Chao1/truth: 0.91 (S 1,500), 0.85 (5,000 flat), 0.70 (4,000), 0.42 (8,000), 0.20 (20,000). ACE similar. ABC 95% interval covered the truth in 5/5 (widths 2-10x). | Chao1/ACE are LOWER BOUNDS at LA's size once the tail is Zipf-like (A). ABC is calibrated but wide. |
| la15:LA-15.1c | Known-truth control for signs: 300 random LB sign samples of LA's size (5,440 tokens); truth = 89 sign types in all of DAMOS. | Seen 84 [80-86]; Chao1 87 [81-108]; ACE 87 [81-94]; Chao1 within 5% of 89 in 79%. | Sign-inventory estimates work at LA's size (A). |
| la15:LA-15.1d | Out-of-sample control for words: 40 LA-sized document samples of LB Knossos (1,332 tokens) -> predict the number of word types in all of KN (3,439 tokens), by community bootstrap (100 refits each) and ABC (1,500 guesses, 60 kept; 10 runs). | Truth 1,597. Bootstrap 1,552 [1,457-1,676], 95% interval covers truth in 72.5%; ABC 1,513 [1,422-1,724], covers 10/10. | Extrapolation to ~2.6x the sample is reliable on a real Aegean syllabic corpus (A). |
| la15:LA-15.1e | ABC on LA (30,000 random communities, nearest 300) for words; 20,000 for signs. | Words: 19,134 [5,247-81,966] types, Zipf exponent 0.98 [0.79-1.14]; nearest-fit distance 0.41. Signs: 206 [153-265]. | LA's written word stock: at least ~4,500 types (Chao1 lower bound, A), most likely 5,000-80,000 (B, ABC); >= 80% of the word types the scribes used are unseen (A). Syllabic signs: 139 seen, 170 (Chao1/ACE) to ~200 (ABC) in use, i.e. ~30-70 syllabic signs unseen or seen once (B). |
| la15:LA-15.2a | Endemism: share of recurrent words (in 2+ documents, n=144) found at only one site, vs curveball. | LA 0.63 vs null 0.21 (z 15). Shuffled LA 0.23 vs 0.20 (z 0.8). LB at LA size 0.78-0.84 vs 0.27-0.30 (z 14-16); all LB 0.73 vs 0.26 (z 42). | Words are strongly site-endemic (A). LA's excess (+0.42) is a little below LB's (+0.48 to +0.56): normal for a palace administration, not a sign of one shared lingua franca. |
| la15:LA-15.2b | Detection-based 'confident endemics': words at one site whose absence elsewhere has P(miss if present) < 0.05 (home token rate vs other sites' token exposure). | LA 30 (shuffled 2). Strongest: KI-RO (16 tokens) and SA-RA₂ (20) at HT; I-RA₂, KA-PA, SA-RU, SI-KA, KU-NI-SU, DA-KA (HT); *86-RO (KH); SI-PI-KI, DU-RE-ZA-SE, *28B-NU-MA-RE, DA-I-PI-TA (ZA); QE-DE-MI-NU (MA); AU-SI-RE (PH); I-DA-MA-TE (Arkhalkhori). | B for KI-RO and SA-RA₂: HT-only administrative terms, absent from ~600 non-HT word tokens where they should have appeared ~10 times each. (Would kill: one secure KI-RO or SA-RA₂ outside HT.) Others C. |
| la15:LA-15.2c | Migratory words: observed number of sites vs curveball, per word. | No LA word spreads beyond the null (best JA-SA 4 sites, P 0.18; I-PI-NA-MA 5 sites, P 0.23; JA-SA-SA-RA-ME, SI-RU-TE, U-NA-KA-NA-SI 4-5 sites, P 0.37-0.40). KU-RO at 3 sites vs 10.6 expected (tied to HT tablets). LB at LA size: TO-SO 5 sites, P 0.02. | No word is migratory beyond its frequency (upper bound). The widest-spread words are the libation-formula words, not administrative ones (B, descriptive). |
| la15:LA-15.2d | Nestedness NODF of the site x word matrix (sites with 15+ tokens) vs curveball. | LA 4.2 vs null 12.1 (+-0.6). Shuffled 12.7 vs 12.7. LB at LA size 3.6-6.0 vs 15.4-19.4. | Anti-nested: small sites are NOT subsets of Hagia Triada's vocabulary (A); same pattern and size as LB. |
| la15:LA-15.2e | Capture-recapture (Chapman) on word types between two 'trapping' lists. | LA HT vs rest 7,528 (overlap 29); tablets vs other supports 8,622 (21); random halves 2,652 (98). Shuffled: 2,629 / 2,652 / 2,609. LB at LA size: main site vs rest 6,302-9,939; random halves 2,081-2,605. Signs HT vs rest: LA 148 (overlap 82); LB 81-83 (truth 89). | Heterogeneity makes two-list estimates 3x the random-split value, in LA and LB alike (A). Sign inventory via recapture: LA ~148, x1.08 LB bias = ~160 (B), agreeing with 1e. |
| la15:LA-15.2f | Keystones: word pairs co-occurring on documents above the curveball null (P <= 0.01, 2+ shared docs), words in 3+ docs. Kill check: list the documents behind the top guild. | LA 48 pairs; LB at LA size 43. LA keystones: DA-ME, DI-DE-RU, MI-NU-TE, KU-NI-SU, SA-RU (6 partners each), *306-TU, PA-DE, QE-RA₂-U, *324-DI-RA, KU-RO. LB: O-NA-TO, KO-TO-NA, PE-MO, DA-MO (the Pylos land-tenure set). Kill check: the DA-ME...SA-RU guild sits on only HT 86a/b and HT 95a/b (sides counted as documents); *306-TU + PA-DE on HT 9a/b and HT 122a. | DEMOTED to C: the 'keystone guild' is one repeated list on two tablets (already known to repeat), inflated by counting sides separately. KU-RO as a hub is the only keystone not tied to a single list (B, known from 2a). LB's keystones are a real cross-document set; LA at this size has none beyond duplicates (upper bound). |
| la15:LA-15.2g | Species-area power law across sites; leave-one-site-out prediction of each site's types and endemics. | z = 0.94 (shuffled 0.98; LB at LA size 0.86-0.89). Mean absolute error of predicted types 8% (LB 10-18%). HT has 426 types vs 572 predicted. | Almost every new token at a new site is a new word (A). HT repeats itself more than its size predicts (B: list archive). |
| la15:LA-15.3a | Post-GORILA: number of new word types in 43 tokens. | Predicted EXCH 30 [23-35], HAB 33 [27-38], ABC 21 [13-30]. Truth 32. | EXCH and HAB hit (HAB within 1); ABC misses low. |
| la15:LA-15.3b | Post-GORILA: number of new syllabic sign types in 155 tokens. | Predicted 1 [0-3] (EXCH), 1 [0-5] (HAB), 0 [0-1] (ABC). Truth 0. | Hit. Later finds added no new syllabic sign (fits coverage 0.994). |
| la15:LA-15.3c | Which known words: 10 of 893 training types reappear. Ranked by predicted probability. | AUC 0.76 (EXCH), 0.75 (HAB). Top-20 hits: 4 (DA-RE, JA-SA-SA-RA-ME, I-PI-NA-MA, SI-RU-TE) vs 0.22 expected at random. Other reappearing: A-MI, I-DA, NU-RO, RE-TI, SA-SA-RA-ME, U-NA-KA-NA-SI. KU-RO, SA-RA₂, KI-RO (top 3) absent. | Frequency predicts reappearance (A), but the hits are libation-formula words: later finds are religious objects, not HT-type tablets. |
| la15:LA-15.3d | Control: random held-out GORILA documents of the same token count (60 reps; ABC 10). | Coverage of 95% intervals: EXCH 0.77, HAB 0.90, ABC 0.60. Mean truth 28.0/43. AUC 0.75 / 0.79. | HAB is the calibrated model on LA. Post-GORILA novelty (32/43) is only slightly above random GORILA (28/43). |
| la15:LA-15.3e | Control: LA with word tokens shuffled across documents; same post-GORILA split. | Truth 23. EXCH 30 [24-35] misses; HAB 26 [20-32] hits; AUC 0.77 / 0.79 (same as real). | Breaking word-site links lowers held-out novelty by 9 types (the real excess is site structure); known-word AUC does not depend on sites (frequency only). |
| la15:LA-15.3f | Control: Linear B, LA-sized training, held-out 43 tokens: Thebes (the most recently published large LB find; train KN+PY+others), random PY, random any (60 reps each). | TH: truth 35.4; EXCH cover 0.17 (err -10.8), HAB 0.83 (err -7.4), ABC 0/10; AUC 0.64. PY: truth 24.8; EXCH 0.67, HAB 0.67, ABC 0.8; AUC 0.79/0.85. Random: EXCH 0.68, HAB 0.62, ABC 0.8; AUC 0.78/0.81. | The pipeline predicts LB about as well as LA; a new site (TH) is underpredicted unless the habitat model is used. LA post-GORILA behaves like LB random, not like LB-TH. |
| la15:LA-15.3g | Chronological chain: G1+G3 (1976) -> G2 (1979) -> G4 (1982) -> G5 (1985) -> unlabelled -> post; each step predicted from all earlier. | Words: HAB in 3/5 (G2 35 vs 31; G4 113 vs 118; post 32.5 vs 32), misses G5 (29 vs 17) and unlabelled (109 vs 123). EXCH 1/5 (G4 88 vs 118). ABC 2/5. Signs: EXCH 4/5, HAB 4/5, ABC 3/5 (G2 brought 7 new signs; G4 0 vs 4 predicted). | Habitat novelty is the best of the three on real publication order (B); every model fails when the find type changes (G5 libation vessels, unlabelled joins/sealings). |
| la15:LA-15.4a | Sites as species, documents as individuals, GORILA-published documents only (45 named sites, 19 with one document, 6 with two). Predict new sites among the 63 post-GORILA documents (sites hidden). | Chao1 75, ACE 74 findspots. Predicted new sites 1 [0-3]; truth 6 (Kalo Chorafi, Kannia, Nerokurou, Skoteino Cave, Tel Haror, Zominthos). | MISS: later finds come from new places about 6x faster than an exchangeable draw (A, as a fact about discovery, not script). |
| la15:LA-15.4b | Control: 63 random GORILA documents held out (60 reps); planted 120 sites with Zipf document counts at GORILA size (200 draws). | Random: coverage 1.00, mean error +0.1. Planted: seen 90, Chao1 114 [96-142] vs 120 true. | The estimator is calibrated; the miss in 4a is real non-exchangeability: excavation seeks new sites. The site count (>= 75) is a lower bound (A). |
| la15:LA-15.4c | Scribal hands as species (592 attributed documents, 102 hands). Calibration: 200 random LA-sized samples of LB Knossos attributed documents; truth = 113 KN hands in 2,781 documents. | LA: Chao1 108, ACE 114 (HT 75 seen, Chao1 79). KN calibration: Chao1 108 [88-138] (>= truth in 37%); extrapolation to full KN 104 [88-115], covers truth 85%. | Attributed LA hands are nearly complete for the attributed set: ~105-115 (B; slight under-estimate as on KN). Most hands that wrote at the excavated archives are already identified. |
| la15:LA-15.final | Field-ecology census of Linear A, no readings used: richness estimators (Chao1, ACE, Chao2, jackknife, coverage), parametric community bootstraps (thousands of refits), massive random guessing over ~80,000 Zipf communities (ABC), curveball null models for endemism, nestedness and co-occurrence (keystones), two-list capture-recapture, species-area curves, and a pre-registered (hashed) prediction of the post-GORILA documents. Controls: planted communities of known size; Linear B (sign inventory with known truth, Knossos extrapolation, Thebes/Pylos held out, Knossos hands); LA with word-site links shuffled; random held-out GORILA documents; publication-order chain. | Word types: 925 seen; >= 4,500 (Chao1/ACE lower bound) and ABC 19,000 [5,200-82,000]; coverage 0.39. Syllabic signs: 139 seen, ~170 (Chao1/ACE) to ~200 [153-265] (ABC). Words are site-endemic (0.63 vs 0.21 null), the site x word matrix is anti-nested (NODF 4 vs 12), no word is migratory beyond chance; all of this matches LB at the same size and vanishes in the shuffle. KI-RO and SA-RA₂ are confident HT endemics. The apparent keystone guild (DA-ME, DI-DE-RU, MI-NU-TE, KU-NI-SU, SA-RU) is one list repeated on HT 86 and HT 95 (demoted); only KU-RO is a hub. Post-GORILA prediction: new words 33 [27-38] (habitat model) vs 32 true; new signs 1 [0-3] vs 0; known-word AUC 0.76 with 4/20 top hits (chance 0.2). New sites: 1 [0-3] predicted, 6 found. Hands: ~105-115. | The census works: calibrated on planted and Linear B controls, it predicted the later-published Linear A material (word novelty, sign novelty, which words return) before it was opened. It is a statement about sampling and administration (A/B), not a reading. Not cracked. |

| la16:LA-16.final | Private spellings as a Rosetta stone: if one hand writes X-B-C where another writes Y-B-C, X and Y are interchangeable. (1) Every one-sign-different word pair weighted by the share of its token pairs written by different hands; sign-pair statistic vs 2,000 hand permutations (max-z FWER); annealed sign-equivalence partitions (correlation clustering) vs anneals on hand-shuffled z; document bootstrap (LA 300); context-sharing filter. (2) Hand inference from spelling conflicts vs shared words, leave-one-out and 20 % held out; unattributed docs assigned. (3) 10,000 random 5-equation hypotheses per split, top 1 % re-tested on held-out documents (new word pairs only), 60 LA / 20 LB splits. Controls: Linear B KN+PY with DAMOS hands (known variant doublets A/A3, O/WO, RA/RA3 ...), LB cut to LA size, planted private spellings in HT Scribe 9 / PY hand 1 (rate 1.0, 0.5), hands shuffled. | LB positive control FAILS at every stage: known variant pairs share a hand as often as unrelated pairs (18.8 % vs 13.9 %); top cross-hand sign pairs contain 0 doublets and sameV at base rate (P 0.93); annealed classes at null (P 0.50-0.90); spelling conflicts do not predict different hands (0.123 vs null 0.124); held-out gain -0.02 (P 0.82). Planted private spellings: 0/16 recovered (sign search), 0/3 detected (hand inference). LA: no sign pair beats FWER with hands (best JA~KU 0.93); site groups KU~WA FWER 0.017 on two 2-sign pairs only; anneals BELOW null (P 0.97-1.00); conflicts carry no hand information (P 0.61); held-out re-test of random equation survivors -0.04 vs blind selection (P 0.89). Hands ARE recoverable from shared vocabulary: LOO 0.36-0.37 vs 0.06 permuted and 0.16 site-majority baseline (LB 0.36 vs 0.32). | NOT CRACKED; PREMISE FAILS ITS OWN CONTROL. Even in Linear B, where real variant spellings and hands are known, one-sign variants are not hand-private and no sign equation can be recovered this way; at LA size there is no power (planted 0/19). No Linear A sign equation survives (KU~SA, I~SA, MA~RA2, RA~RO/TA killed on held-out data; KU~WA, MA~ME grade C, 2-sign words). Grade B: LA hands are recoverable from shared words, not spellings (2.3x site baseline); 52 unattributed LA docs get grade-C hand assignments (held-out accuracy 0.31). Logs: loops/la16_cycle1-4.txt. |
| la16:LA-16.1a | Every pair of word types (>= 2 signs) differing in exactly one position. Weight of a pair = share of its token pairs written by DIFFERENT hands; sign-pair statistic T(X,Y) = sum over its word pairs. Null: hands permuted over documents within stratum, 2,000 permutations -> per-pair z, raw P, max-z FWER. Positive control: Linear B KN+PY, DAMOS hand attributions (1,987 docs, 132 hands, 4,903 word pairs, 1,748 sign pairs), scored post hoc with LB values (doublet = same/near-value special sign or glide, sameV = consonant differs, sameC = vowel differs). | LB (strata site+series): best KE~O z 3.4, FWER 0.24; nothing significant. Top-10/20/40 cross-hand sign pairs: doublet 0/0/0, sameV 1/6/9 vs base rate doublet 1.1 %, sameV 21.6 %. Mean z of (doublet+sameV) minus others -0.09 (P 0.93). LB (strata site): A~E best, FWER 0.25; same picture; only sameC (vowel changes = inflection) is shifted toward WITHIN-hand (P 0.006). Pair level: LB doublet word pairs (A/A3, O/WO, RA/RA3, A/JA, PU/PU2 ...) share a hand in 18.8 % of cases vs 13.9 % for unrelated pairs - known spelling variants are NOT more cross-hand than other one-sign pairs. | POSITIVE CONTROL FAILS. In Linear B, where spelling variants exist and hands are known, the hand-contrast statistic does not pick out variant signs; the one-sign pair space is 98 % different words (minimal pairs) and the few true variants (69 of 4,903 word pairs) are written both within and across hands. The premise "variants are private to hands" is false where it can be checked. |
| la16:LA-16.1b | Same on Linear A, attributed hands (188 docs, 51 hands, 431 types, 630 word pairs, 100 sign pairs with >= 2 pairs), strata = site; and site groups as pseudo-hands (all 543 docs, 6 groups, 2,745 word pairs), strata = support type. | Hands: best JA~KU z 0.8, FWER 0.93 (nothing). Total cross weight 590 vs null 606 +- 3.8 (P < 0.0005): one-sign pairs are hand-LOCAL (replicates LA-13.4c). Sites: KU~WA FWER 0.017 (only DA-KU/DA-WA, JA-KU/JA-WA, all 2-sign, OTH vs KN; 0.034 after correcting for the two LA analyses), KU~QE 0.07, MA~ME 0.11 (incl. JA-SA-SA-RA-MA KN vs -ME elsewhere, A-MA/A-ME, MA-ZA/ME-ZA, TU-MA/TU-ME); total cross weight below null (P 0.019). | No hand equation. KU~WA (sites) grade C: would support a >= 3-sign pair with KU/WA at different sites in the same slot; would kill: it is 2-sign words only, the space LA-13 showed is saturated, and the LB control shows the statistic does not find true variants. MA~ME grade C (same caveat; the libation-formula case is a known variant, not a new finding). |
| la16:LA-16.1c | Planted controls: one sign X rewritten as Y in every (rate 1.0) or half (0.5) of the words of one hand; LA HT Scribe 9 (3 draws x 2 rates), LB subsample at LA size (612 attributed word tokens), full LB PY hand 1 (2 x 2); 300-500 permutations each. Recovery = rank 1 and FWER < 0.05. | LA: 0/6 recovered, 0/6 in top 5 (planted pairs had 0-3 word pairs). LB at LA size: only 7 sign pairs have >= 2 word pairs at all; 0/6. Full LB PY:1: 0/4 (RE->RO rank 4, FWER 0.81). | NO POWER even on full LB with an exceptionless planted private spelling: the cross-hand weight is near 1 under the null too (with 50-130 hands two documents almost never share a hand), so a private spelling raises T only marginally. |
| la16:LA-16.1 | Cycle verdict | The hand-contrast statistic fails its positive control (LB variants not found; planted rules not found) and finds nothing in LA beyond the known hand-locality of near-variants. | NOT SUPPORTED. Upper bound only: < 3/16 planted recovery. Next (cycle 2): restrict to pairs sharing a context (same series or logogram), where LB doublets are enriched 2x, and anneal over sign partitions; then cycle 3: hand inference from spelling conflicts. |
| la16:LA-16.2a | Correlation clustering: edge weight of (X,Y) = hand-contrast z (cycle 1, 2,000 permutations, clipped at +-4, n >= 2 word pairs); a partition is scored by summed within-class weight, maximised by simulated annealing (60,000 moves). Null: the same anneal on 30-200 z-vectors from hand-permuted corpora. Two pair sets: all one-sign pairs, and pairs whose two forms share a context (same site+series/support or same logogram on the line), where LB variant doublets are enriched 2x (32 % vs 16 % of unrelated pairs share context; context & cross-hand 17 % vs 9 %). Positive control: LB KN+PY, scored post hoc with LB values. | LB all pairs: real score 111 vs null 112 +- 14 (P 0.50); 3 classes (one giant class of 41 signs); co-clustered pairs doublet 1.2 % / sameV 20.4 % vs null anneals 0.8 % / 17.2 %. LB context-only: 33 vs 40.5 +- 5.8 (P 0.90); doublet 1.2 %, sameV 21 % (base 1.1 %, 21.6 %). LA hands all pairs: 16.7 vs 21.2 +- 2.5 (P 0.985); LA hands context-only: 9.2 vs 11.9 +- 1.5 (P 0.97); LA site groups context-only: 0.9 vs 37.9 +- 7.8 (P 1.00). | FAILS ITS CONTROL AND LA IS BELOW NULL. In Linear B the annealed sign classes are no better than anneals on shuffled hands and carry no phonetic structure. Linear A real partitions score BELOW every null: there is less cross-writer swapping than chance (near-variants are writer-local, LA-13.4c), so no partition of "interchangeable" signs is supported. |
| la16:LA-16.2b | Stability: documents bootstrap-resampled (LB 20, LA hands 300, LA sites 100), z recomputed from 150-200 permutations, re-annealed; sign pairs co-clustered in >= 50 % of resamples. | LB: 23 pairs >= 70 % (doublet 1 = RA~RA3, sameC 3, sameV 5, other 14: at base rate). LA hands, all pairs: KU~SA 0.77, RA~RO 0.72, MA~RA2 0.66, RA~TA 0.66, RO~TA 0.63; context-only: KU~SA 0.78, RA~RO 0.69, RO~TA 0.66, RA~TA 0.60, ME~RE 0.50; site groups: RA~RO 0.61. | Bootstrap stability is not significance: the same anneals score below null, and in LB stable pairs are phonetically random. KU~SA and RA~RO/TA are reproducible accidents of a few frequent words (grade C, would support: the same pair beating hand-permutation in a held-out half (cycle 4); would kill: failing that). |
| la16:LA-16.2 | Cycle verdict | No sign-equivalence partition beats hand-shuffled nulls in either script, and the Linear B control (where the answer is known) gives nothing. | REJECTED as a method at these corpus sizes; no Linear A sign equation. |
| la16:LA-16.3a | For every pair of same-site documents: CONFLICT = a word in one and a word in the other differing by one sign (neither doc has the other's form); AGREEMENT = a shared word type. Private spellings predict P(same hand | conflict) < base. Null: hands permuted within site (1,000-2,000). Positive control: LB KN+PY (1,987 docs, 132 hands). | LB: base 0.134, conflict-only 0.123 (null 0.124, P(<=) 0.49), agreement-only 0.406 (null 0.122, P < 0.001). LA (188 docs, 51 hands): base 0.055, conflict-only 0.053 (null 0.051, P(<=) 0.61), agreement-only 0.181 (null 0.057, P < 0.0005), both 0.141. | Spelling conflicts carry NO information about hands in either script, including Linear B, where real variant spellings exist. Shared vocabulary does (x3 the base rate). There is no measurable "private spelling" signal for a Rosetta stone to be built on. |
| la16:LA-16.3b | Leave-one-out hand recovery, nearest document by (a) agreements, (b) agreements minus conflicts, (c) conflicts only (vote against conflicting hands, predict the commonest remaining same-site hand); 100 label permutations; majority-hand-at-site baseline. | LB: (a) 0.362, (b) 0.366, (c) 0.096 vs permuted 0.116/0.116/0.101, majority 0.316. LA: (a) 0.362 (50/138), (b) 0.372, (c) 0.123 vs permuted 0.063/0.064/0.107 (P 0.24 for c), majority 0.160. Held-out 20 % of attributed LA docs assigned by agreements-minus-conflicts: coverage 0.50, accuracy 0.31 (LB 0.73 / 0.56). | Hands are recoverable from shared words (LA 0.37 vs 0.16 baseline), not from spellings: adding conflicts changes recovery by < 0.01, and conflicts alone are at the permuted level. Confound: same-hand documents are often one series or deposit, so "shared words" partly means "same dossier". |
| la16:LA-16.3c | Planted private spellings: HT Scribe 9 rewrites 3 mid-frequency signs to other signs in ALL its words (3 draws). Must make conflicts point away from HT Scribe 9. | Conflict-only P(same hand) 0.048-0.053 vs null 0.051-0.052 (P(<=) 0.37-0.61); conflict-only recovery unchanged (0.123, P 0.26-0.34). | 0/3 planted private-spelling sets detected: even a three-sign private orthography in the largest LA hand is invisible, because the rewritten words rarely have a counterpart written by another hand. Upper bound only (< 1/3 power). |
| la16:LA-16.3d | Unattributed LA documents assigned to a hand by agreements minus conflicts (unique positive winner). | 52 of 355 assigned: HT Scribe 9 (9), PS Scribe 1 (6), HT Scribe 6 (4), KH Wc Scribe 54 (4), HT Scribe 2 (3), KH Wa Scribe 52 (3) ... (list in c3.json). Expected accuracy ~0.31 from the held-out check. | Grade C attributions only (would support: a palaeographic check of the assigned tablets; would kill: wrong-site or wrong-period assignments, or held-out accuracy at baseline). They rest on shared words, not spellings. |
| la16:LA-16.3 | Cycle verdict | Hands can be partly recovered (vocabulary), but private spellings play no role, in LA or in the LB control. | Rosetta-stone premise NOT SUPPORTED; side result B: shared vocabulary recovers LA hands at 2.3x the site-majority baseline. |
| la16:LA-16.4a | Per split: attributed documents halved at random within site (TRAIN/TEST). 10,000 random hypotheses = random sets of 5 sign equations, scored on TRAIN by summed hand-contrast z (300 hand permutations). Top 1 % survive and are re-scored on TEST using only word pairs never seen in TRAIN. Statistic: survivors' mean TEST z minus all hypotheses' mean. Null: same pipeline with TRAIN hands permuted. LB KN+PY 20 splits (positive control), LA 60 splits. | LB: survivors' held-out gain -0.018 +- 0.056 (45 % of splits > 0) vs permuted-train 0.001 (P 0.82). LA: +0.032 +- 0.171 (55 % > 0) vs permuted-train +0.073 (78 % > 0), real - permuted -0.041 (P 0.89). LA equations most often chosen on TRAIN: KU~SA 25/60, I~SA 22, MA~RA2 19, PA~RA 16, KA~RE 12, RO~TA 12. | NO HELD-OUT PREDICTION in either script: equations that look swapped between hands in one half of the documents do not predict swaps in new words in the other half, even in Linear B. KU~SA, I~SA, MA~RA2, RA~RO/TA (cycle 2 bootstrap favourites) are killed: selected often, predict nothing. |
| la16:LA-16.4 | Cycle verdict | Thousands of random equation sets, held-out re-test: no survivor beats the blind-selection null in LB or LA. | REJECTED. |
| la18:LA-18.final | Anti-co-occurrence as a morphology detector: alternants of one lemma should fill one slot, so never meet. (1) Every form-related pair (w/w+X, X+w, last- or first-sign swap) scored for co-presence on a tablet vs a site-stratified curveball null (word frequencies and tablet sizes fixed; 500-2,000 samples), pair-level FWER (min-P) and BH; (2) pooled by alternation class, two-sided max-|z| FWER, plus context similarity vs df- and site/support-matched random pairs; (3) ending-level segregation (case harmony) and annealed ending partitions calibrated by equal-effort anneals on 200 null matrices; (4) entry-level exclusion by within-tablet permutation. Controls: LB (DAMOS) with Greek inflection labels, LA-sized LB draws, LA shuffled / endings relabelled, planted avoiding, attracting, case-harmony and entry-exclusive paradigms in LA. | LB: alternants of one lemma ATTRACT on a tablet (3.1x, z 11.6; 9/10 LA-sized draws), and the class test recovers real Greek endings (+JO, RA~RO, +O, +DE; precision 0.73 at LA size); LB tablets are written in one case (ending homogeneity z 13.8; 6/6 LA-sized draws); and alternants AVOID the same ENTRY (0.27x, P 0.001). LA: perfect planted avoidance is never detectable (0/48 pairs); planted attraction (4/4) and planted case harmony (4/4, z 26-31) always are. Real LA: no attracting alternation class (0/27), pooled form pairs 1.29x (below all 10 LA-sized LB draws, 1.7-5.4x); ending homogeneity z 1.8 = relabelled-ending controls; the one significant ending partition is the libation formula (unstable across subsets); only 5 co-present form pairs for the entry test. Context: SI- prefix pairs share contexts (P 0.0025, q 0.065). | Arrow REJECTED as stated (absence is uninformative at LA size, and in LB alternants do not avoid the tablet), but the refined mechanism (seek the tablet, avoid the entry) is validated on LB: grade B as a method. LA, grade B (calibrated negative): one-sign alternants are not listed together and tablets are not case-harmonic as in LB. Grade C: SI- as an alternating prefix (context only). No paradigm, no alternating ending beyond the null. |
| la18:LA-18.1 | Cycle verdict | Avoidance is undetectable in LA even when planted perfectly, and the LB control shows real paradigms attract rather than avoid. The usable signal is the opposite tail: LB form-related pairs share tablets 3x; LA form-related pairs do not (1.29x vs 1.7-5.4x in every LA-sized LB draw). | Avoidance arrow REJECTED (method). Side result, grade C: LA one-sign-different word pairs are not tablet-mates the way LB inflected/derived forms are; would support: the same deficit in cycle 2 by alternation class and with a planted attracting paradigm recovered; would kill: a planted attracting paradigm in LA is not recovered either (then LA simply lacks power). |
| la18:LA-18.2 | Cycle verdict | In LB, alternants of one lemma ATTRACT on tablets, and the class test finds real Greek inflection even at LA size (precision 0.73, power ~0.9). Planted attraction in LA is always found. Linear A shows no attracting alternation class at all, and its only context-sharing class (SI-) is marginal. | Avoidance REJECTED (no power, wrong direction in LB). Grade B (method-calibrated negative): LA one-sign alternants (+X, X+, X~Y) are not listed together on tablets the way LB inflected forms are; either LA tablets do not list a lemma in several forms, or one-sign alternants in LA are mostly different words. Grade C: SI- prefix alternation (context only). |
| la18:LA-18.3 | Cycle verdict | LB tablets segregate endings strongly (case harmony) and the test keeps full power at LA size; planted harmony in LA is obvious. Real LA endings show no harmony beyond what relabelled endings give, and the one 'significant' partition is the libation formula. | REJECTED for LA. Grade B (calibrated negative): LA administrative documents are not written 'in one case' the way LB tablets are (z 1.8 vs LB-245 3.4-5.8; upper bound on any LB-strength harmony). HT-only homogeneity (P 0.007, unreplicated outside HT) grade C: would support: the same in HT with the formula words removed; would kill: it vanishes when scribe is used as stratum. |
| la18:LA-18.4 | Cycle verdict | The full mechanism is now calibrated on LB: alternants SEEK the tablet and AVOID the entry. LA has power for the tablet half (cycle 2: planted attraction always found, real attraction absent) and no power for the entry half. | Method B (validated in LB). LA: nothing found; the absence of tablet-level co-presence of one-sign alternants is the only usable result (grade B, from cycle 2). |
| la18:LA-18.1a | Positive control, LB full. Family-level O (units holding both members) vs curveball null; pair-level lower-tail mid-P, FWER by min-P over the null rows, BH. | TRUE inflection pairs (477): O 79 vs E 25.6, ratio 3.08, z +11.6, P(attract) 0.001, P(avoid) 1.0. FORM_OTHER 2.77x (z +8.3), SUF1 3.46x, FIN 3.15x; PRE1 1.26x, INI 1.27x (ns); RND 0.83x (ns). Pair level: only 8 form pairs have any power (null P(O=0) < 0.5); best avoider E-KE / E-KE-QE O 3 vs E 7.9, FWER 0.055; AUC true-vs-other on lower P 0.33 (true pairs are LESS avoiding). | PREMISE KILLED BY THE POSITIVE CONTROL. Where inflection is known, alternative forms of one lemma do not avoid each other on a tablet: they ATTRACT ~3x (DO-E-RO/DO-E-RA 12 vs 2.8; TO-SO/TO-SO-DE; nominative and genitive of one name listed together). "Same slot -> never on the same tablet" is false for a Bronze Age Aegean archive. |
| la18:LA-18.1b | Positive control, LA-sized LB: 10 random draws of 245 units, 500 nulls each. | TRUE pairs attract in 9/10 draws (ratio 1.7-6.4, P(attract) <= 0.008 in 8/10, 0.056 in 1); 1/10 0.69x. All form pairs pooled: 1.7-5.4x in 10/10. RND: 0.3-1.7x, P(attract) < 0.05 in 1/10. | The attraction signal is detectable at Linear A size: power ~0.9 per draw. |
| la18:LA-18.1c | Linear A, same pipeline. | SUF1 86 pairs O 2 vs E 1.71 (1.17x, z +0.24); PRE1 60: 1 vs 0.98; FIN 53: 1 vs 0.56; INI 34: 1 vs 0.64; pooled form pairs 5 vs 3.89 (1.29x). RND 1 vs 2.85. Pair level: 0 testable pairs (every form pair has null P(O=0) >= 0.5), 0 FWER, 0 BH. | No avoidance and no attraction. LA's pooled 1.29x is below all 10 LA-sized LB draws (1.7-5.4x). |
| la18:LA-18.1d | Negative control: LA word types shuffled across units within site (sizes kept), 5 draws x 1,000 nulls. | Families 0-2.3x on 0-2 co-occurrences, all P > 0.09; 0 FWER hits in 5/5. | Calibrated (no false positives), and indistinguishable from real LA. |
| la18:LA-18.1e | Planted avoidance in LA: 8 words (df >= 3) split into two forms, each unit using ONE form (perfect mutual exclusion); 6 draws (48 planted pairs), 1,000 nulls. | Planted pairs: O 0 vs E 0.20-2.14; family P(avoid) 0.09-0.82; pair level 46/48 untestable; the 2 testable (KU-RO split, df 30) P 0.07-0.08, FWER 0.15-0.16. Recovery 0/48 (< 3/48 = 6 % upper bound). | NO POWER for avoidance in LA: a perfect paradigm of the commonest word is not significant. Absence of co-occurrence carries no information at this corpus size. |
| la18:LA-18.2a | Positive control LB full (1,000 nulls; 39 classes). | 6 classes FWER < 0.05, all ATTRACTING: +JO (104 pairs, O 16 vs 2.6, z 6.7), +E (5.6), RA~RO (4.9), +O (2.9), +TA (2.9), +DE (2.7); 4/6 are known Greek case/gender alternations (genitive -o-jo, -ra/-ro, gen. pl. -a-o, allative -de). No avoiding class. Context: truth classes have lower context P than others, AUC 0.73 (15 vs 24 classes). | Positive control PASSES for attraction + context, not for avoidance: the method recovers real Linear B inflection classes from tablet co-presence alone. |
| la18:LA-18.2b | LA-sized LB: 8 draws of 245 units, 500 nulls. | 0-2 FWER classes per draw (11 in all), 8/11 are truth classes (+JO 5x, RA~RO 2x, +DE, +O); 3 false (+E 2x, +TA). All attracting. | At LA size the class test still finds real inflection in 7/8 draws (power ~0.9 for at least one class; precision 0.73). |
| la18:LA-18.2c | Linear A (2,000 nulls; 27 classes of >= 3 pairs). | 0 form classes FWER < 0.05; largest |z| 0.71 (A+); every suffix/prefix class has O 0-1 vs E 0.01-0.3. The frequency-matched RANDOM comparison class attracts (O 5 vs 1.55, FWER 0.026): the site-stratified null is anti-conservative for unrelated pairs, which makes LA's zero form-class attraction stronger, not weaker. | NULL. LA shows none of the LB-type co-presence of alternants (upper bound: 0/27 classes). |
| la18:LA-18.2d | Negative control: LA types shuffled across units (5 x 1,000 nulls). | 0 FWER classes in 5/5; max |z| 1.36. | Calibrated. |
| la18:LA-18.2e | Planted LA paradigms, 8 words each, new suffix sign: attract mode (P 0.5 the doc also holds w+#) and avoid mode (P 0.5 the doc uses w+# instead of w); 4 + 4 draws, 1,000 nulls. | Attract: 4/4 recovered as the rank-1 class, z 8.2-10.4, FWER 0.001. Avoid: 0/4 by co-occurrence (O 0 vs E 0.09-0.23, FWER 1.0), but context similarity flags 3/4 (ctx P 0.0005, 0.0015, 0.0125; 1 at 0.06). | The cycle-1 "would kill" condition is not met: LA has power for attraction, so its absence is real. Avoidance is visible only through context, never through co-occurrence. |
| la18:LA-18.2f | Context of LA classes. First pass (site/support in the bag, df-matched baseline): SI+ ctx 0.81 P 0.0005 q 0.014; +RO, +JA P 0.006-0.007 (q 0.063). Robustness (c2b): bag without site/support, random pairs matched on df AND (site, support) of first attestation, 400 resamples per class. | SI+ (MI-TA/SI-MI-TA, DA-RE/SI-DA-RE, *805-MI/SI-*805-MI, KI-RA/SI-KI-RA, TE-TU/SI-TE-TU, I-SI/SI-I-SI) ctx 0.83 vs 0.58, P 0.0025, q 0.065. +ME P 0.037 (q 0.49). +RO, +JA, KA+, +NE fall to P 0.14-0.31 (they were site effects). | SI- as an alternating prefix: grade C. Would support: SI-X and X in the same entry slot (both before the same logogram / numeral pattern) in new texts, or a third SI- pair with the same context; would kill: q > 0.1 once the 6 pairs are split by site (most are Hagia Triada hapaxes). |
| la18:LA-18.3a | Positive control LB full (500 nulls). | Ending homogeneity O 4,282 vs E 2,882, z +13.8, P 0.002: LB tablets are 'written in one case'. Self-attracting endings: -QE z 14.2, -TA 8.2, -NA 7.9, -U 7.4, -DE 7.0, -JA 6.7, -JO 5.0. Avoiding ending pairs (FWER 0.002): WE/NA, WO/NA, NA/WA, WO/QE, WO/MO, TO/WA. Partition P 0.005 for g = 2-4; g = 2: {JO RO WO TA NO WE U SO WA} vs {TO JA NA MO DE QE} (the -U/-WE/-WO alternants land together). | Positive control PASSES for homogeneity; the partition is real but mixes case with document genre. |
| la18:LA-18.3b | LA-sized LB (6 draws of 245 units). | Homogeneity P <= 0.006 in 6/6 (z 3.4-5.8). Partition: P <= 0.03 in 1/6 (g = 2), 3/6 (g = 4); class contents differ between draws. | Homogeneity has full power at LA size; ending partitions are unstable at LA size even where truth exists. |
| la18:LA-18.3c | Linear A (1,000 nulls). | Homogeneity O 112 vs E 89.6, z +1.8, P 0.049. Self: -TI z 3.2 (11 vs 4.7), others |z| < 1.7. One ending pair FWER < 0.05: RO/RE attract (14 vs 6.8, FWER 0.036). Partition g = 2 score 5.16, P 0.005: {RO RE TI RA TA SI} vs {NA JA TE RU NE MA}; g = 3, 4 P 0.015. | Weak signal, needs controls below. |
| la18:LA-18.3d | Negative control: LA endings re-assigned at random across types (incidence kept), 5 draws x 500 nulls. | Homogeneity P 0.02 in 1/5 (z 2.3), else 0.23-0.83 - LA's z 1.8 sits inside this band. Partition P 0.075-0.92 (0/5 < 0.05). | Homogeneity: LA indistinguishable from relabelled LA. Partition: calibrated. |
| la18:LA-18.3e | Planted case harmony in LA: a new final sign added to every word of 10 % / 20 % of the units (2 draws each). | Homogeneity z 26-31, P 0.002 in 4/4; planted ending self z 28-35. Partition P 0.01-0.075 (10 %), 0.005 (20 %). | Planted harmony is found at once: LA has the power that its real endings do not use. |
| la18:LA-18.3f | Robustness of the LA partition (c3b): drop words with doc frequency >= 8 (KU-RO, SA-RA2, KI-RO, A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA ...); Hagia Triada only; list the word pairs carrying the within-class excess. | Drop-high: P 0.02 but different classes ({NA RE TI TE RU TA MA} vs {RA JA NE SI TU}). Excess carried by I-PI-NA-MA / SI-RU-TE (5 units), A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA / SI-RU-TE (4), A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA / I-PI-NA-MA (3) - the libation formula on stone vessels - and MI-NU-TE / SA-RU, DI-DE-RU / MI-NU-TE. | The LA partition is a fixed-phrase (libation formula) artefact, not an ending paradigm: it changes with every subset. |

FORM_OTHER 51 pairs 0.65 (P 0.021). ANY 1.24 (attract, P 0.001). | The arrow is RIGHT at this scale: in LB, alternants of one lemma share the tablet (cycles 1-2) but AVOID the entry (0.27x), the reverse of ordinary word pairs. |
| la18:LA-18.4b | Linear A. | Only 5 form pairs co-present on a multi-entry tablet; same entry 0 vs E 0.21 (P 0.80). ANY 1.04 (P 0.36-0.69). | No material: upper bound only. |
| la18:LA-18.4c | Planted entry-exclusive alternants in LA: 10 words that recur in >= 2 entries of a tablet, rewritten as w+# in a random half of their entries (4 draws). | Planted pairs 5-9, same entry 0 vs E 0.41-0.83, P(avoid) 0.39-0.64; 0/4 detected. | NO POWER: LA tablets rarely repeat a word across entries, so an entry-level paradigm cannot be seen. |
| r2:R-2.final | ~8.9 million programs on 21 corpus x mode runs: 3 real scripts, 13 nulls, 3 planted generators (grille, 2 ledgers), 2 natural languages (Latin, Linear B); layout-given rerun on 11 | Planted generators recovered 3/3, the mechanism readable in the winning program (`PL 0 LI if tab1` = grille row from the line number; `... AB ... tab2` = commodity cycle read from the line above): +24 to +97 mbit/token on held-out text. Linear B: no gain (0/30). Latin: gain only from line-end layout; with layout given <= 2 mbit/token. Real, layout given, vs KN + document cache: LA +28 bits net (p 0.14), PE +20 (p 0.28), Voynich -50 on KN alone (Markov resynthesis +42). Shuffles always beat KN (+97 to +3,900 bits) via the token skeleton | No program beats the baselines on held-out data beyond its nulls in any script. What the search finds in LA and PE is 'copy what the line or entry above had' (logograms, entry-initial signs, word length): within-document repetition, which a cache model already explains (known in kind: list entries repeat). Voynich glyph order is not reproduced by any short procedure better than a 3-glyph Markov chain; Markov-resynthesised text gains slightly more from the search than the real text. Grades: 'no short generator (<= 15 ops, these primitives) for Voynich' A (negative, calibrated: planted grille found at 24.7 mbit/token, real <= 0); 'LA/PE entries copy from the line above' B (known kind); procedural origin of any of the three texts: not supported. Not cracked |
| la14:LA-14.1a | Null (2): forgeries vs forgeries from the same forger (independent draws), every corpus, forger and group, 6 seeds. | Mean AUC 0.45-0.55 in every cell (e.g. LA/MK2 all-groups 0.499, LA/FLAT 0.479; LB 0.47-0.52). COPY0 (unedited copies of half A vs real half B) 0.48-0.52. | PASS: the discriminators have no built-in bias. |
| la14:LA-14.1b | Raw contest, LA. Every generative forger vs real half B. | All caught: all-groups LR AUC UNI 0.983, WMK1 0.900, WMK2 0.871, MK3 0.859, NEUR 0.856, MK1 0.856, MK4 0.841, MK2 0.839, FLAT 0.828 (best generative forger = FLAT, the la8 flat form). Every single group > its null. | Not interpretable until 14.1c. |
| la14:LA-14.1c | Negative control: the forger's own world (FW_X, 292 docs written by X) put through the same pipeline against X. Should be 0.5. | FAILS as an absolute: FW_MK2 vs MK2 0.775, FW_FLAT vs FLAT 0.732, FW_NEUR vs NEUR 0.782 (uni 0.68-0.71, ngram 0.71-0.79). Re-estimating a forger from half a small corpus leaves a large, detectable finite-sample signature (over-common n-grams, missing rare tokens). So most of 14.1b is NOT Linear A structure. | Key methodological result: raw forger-catching AUCs on corpora of ~150 documents are mostly estimation artefacts. Survival must be measured against the world, not against 0.5. |
| la14:LA-14.1d | Calibrated survival = AUC(real vs X) - AUC(world of X vs X), same group, 6 vs 6 seeds; survive if t > 3 and d > 0.03. | LA, all-groups: +0.063* (MK2), +0.096* (FLAT), +0.074* (NEUR). Surviving groups: rep +0.119*/+0.112*/+0.155* (3 of 3 forgers); arith +0.115*/+0.130*/+0.015 (2 of 3; the neural LM reproduces it); long +0.071/+0.123*/+0.092* (2 of 3); pos +0.049/+0.052*/+0.073* (2 of 3). uni, sign, sbig, ngram, numw, len: at most 1 of 3. | Real but modest excess over every forger: within-tablet repetition/line-template regularity, number arithmetic, head-to-body agreement and position. Lexical and phonotactic statistics are fully explained by Markov forgers. |

LA's excess is about 0.5-0.7 of LB's on these groups. |

One edit per tablet. | Detected: E_num arith 0.604*, E_numnear arith 0.562* (even a +-50% change of ONE number); E_word sbig 0.617*, E_first 0.637*, E_last 0.637* (sign bigrams, not head-tail pairs); E_swapadj tbig 0.839*; E_splice 0.730*; E_wordshuf pos 0.557*. NOT detected: E_swapline 0.527, E_numshuf 0.533 (arith 0.528), E_logo 0.517. | Numbers on an LA tablet are arithmetically constrained (B); a foreign word is detectable from its signs (tablet-level sign coherence, B/C); the order of lines, the logogram choice and which number goes with which word are NOT detectable at this size (upper bounds). |

A difference of documents (or of size), not yet of language. |

The cycle-1 E_splice result (0.730) is suspect for the same reason. Cycle 3 reruns a length-matched splice. |
| la14:LA-14.2e | Univariate dissection (la14_c3d.py): each scalar statistic alone, real-side AUC on cycle-1 forgeries minus the same forger on its own world, 6 seeds. | Totals (tot_last, near_last, tot_run, tot_all): d = -0.01 to +0.04, never significant against any forger. Survivors in LA for all 3 forgers: arith:lsd (spread of log numbers on a tablet, LOWER in real) -0.12*/-0.12*/-0.03; maxshare -0.12*/-0.12*/-0.10*; distinct numbers -0.09*/-0.07*/-0.05; desc (descending pairs) +0.06*/+0.08*/+0.07*; rep:tplsame +0.13*/+0.07*/+0.08*; tpldist -0.15*/-0.12*/-0.12*; word repeated on a tablet +0.06*/+0.06*/+0.05*. Logogram homogeneity (long:Lsame, nLdist): LA +0.00 to +0.05 (n.s.); LB +0.12*/-0.13*. Copy forgers vs COPY0: E_num lsd -0.087*, maxshare -0.067*; E_numshuf desc +0.069*. | The arithmetic signal is NOT totals: written totals are too rare per tablet to catch a forger. It is magnitude coherence (numbers on one tablet share a scale), repeated values and size order. Line-template regularity and repeated words are the 'rep' signal. LB tablets keep to one commodity; LA tablets do not (upper bound). |
| la14:LA-14.2f | Targeted test T1, magnitude coherence: mean within-tablet SD of log(1+n), tablets with >= 3 numbers, totals dropped. Null G: numbers permuted across tablets; null K: permuted only within the same site AND the same context (preceding logogram base, or word). Planted kill: numbers redrawn at random. | LA 0.683 (175 tablets) vs G 0.992, K 0.907 (P = 0.002, the floor of 500 runs); HT 0.675 vs K 0.976; non-HT 0.698 vs K 0.772 (P = 0.018); LB 0.676 vs K 0.943. Plant-random: 0.978 vs 0.999 / 1.007 (P = 0.16 / 0.08), i.e. killed. | Grade A: numbers on one LA tablet share a scale beyond what site and commodity explain. Expected for real accounts (one tablet = one kind of transaction), but it is a structure every forger misses and a copy-editor breaks with one number. |
| la14:LA-14.2g | Targeted test T2, size order: share of strictly descending pairs among unequal adjacent numbers (totals dropped); null: numbers permuted within each tablet (2,000). Planted: 30% of tablets sorted descending. | LA 0.549 of 659 pairs (null 0.500 +- 0.013, P = 0.0005); planted 0.653 (found). Same context 0.527 (P = 0.055); ACROSS a context change 0.588 (P = 0.0015); HT across 0.601 (P = 0.0005); non-HT 0.552 (P = 0.17). LB: same context 0.583 (P = 0.001), across 0.554. | Grade B: on HT tablets the commodity sections run from large to small amounts (big-quantity commodity first). Ranking of entries WITHIN a list is not supported in LA (C, P = 0.055), but is clear in LB (0.583), which differs here. |

LA: 0 planted-key pairs and only 5-10 head>tail pairs of any kind. | PASS: the contest names the planted agreement explicitly. Real LA has no comparable head-to-tail sign agreement (upper bound at this size). |
| la14:LA-14.3b | Parametric bootstrap: each statistic on all 292 real tablets vs 25 forged corpora of 292 from forgers fitted IN-SAMPLE (6 for NEUR).
