LA vs MK2 / MK3 / FLAT / NEUR: tot_last z +4.6/+4.8/+4.2/+5.8, near_last +6.6/+7.3/+6.6/+3.9 (2.6% of tablets end with an exact total; forgers about 0.5%); lsd -8.1/-8.7/-9.3/-2.3; maxshare -8.4/-5.1/-8.1/-4.9; distinct -4.7/-3.0/-6.5/-2.0; tplsame +13.5/+9.1/+10.2/+5.1; bgrep +14.3/+7.5/+24.6/+4.5; wdist -6.9/-6.8/-10.4/-6.5; same_ls +6.1/+4.5/+4.2/+5.9; Lsame +4.1/+4.4/+3.7/+1.1; heading word without a number +8.5/+3.3/+0.6/+1.5. LB: same pattern plus Lsame +9.6/+11.5, nLdist -7.9/-9.2. | At corpus level all four forgers miss totals, number-scale coherence, repeated values, line-template repetition and repeated words. FLAT reproduces the heading slot; NEUR reproduces most of the logogram homogeneity. |
| la14:LA-14.3c | Shared signs between DISTINCT word types (>= 2 signs) on one tablet. Null: word tokens permuted across tablets within site (2,000), then within scribe (where GORILA gives one; 142 of 292). Positive control LB; negative FW_FLAT; planted: 25% of tablets given one common final sign. | Final sign: LA 1.36x (P=0.004) within site, but 1.17x (P=0.046) within scribe and 1.13x (P=0.12) without KU-RO/KI-RO/PO-TO-KU-RO. First sign: 1.34x (P=0.002) site; 1.30x (P=0.001) scribe; 1.28x (P=0.006) scribe and no total words; variants sharing another sign excluded: P=0.009. Consecutive entries share the initial sign: non-HT 0.145 vs 0.092 shuffled within tablet (P=0.001), still 0.115 vs 0.062 without A- words (P=0.0005), 0.085 vs 0.052 without A-, MA-, *309- (P=0.013); HT 0.040 vs 0.037 (P=0.39). LB final 1.62x (P=0.0005); FW_FLAT 0.97x / 0.98x; planted 5.1x. | Final-sign agreement is mostly the scribe's hand: dropped. First-sign agreement survives scribe and variant controls: on non-HT tablets, entries with the same initial sign are written next to each other (ZA 10a A-KU-MI-NA, A-TA-NA-TE, A-MI-DA-U, A-DU-KU-MI-NA; PK 1 MA-TI-ZA-I-TE, MA-TE-TI, MA-KA-I-TA; TY 2 *309-). Grade C (found post hoc). |
| la14:LA-14.3d | Splices, the copy forgers for tablet coherence. E_splLine (cycle 2) and E_splLM (length-matched) vs COPY0, 8 seeds, all corpora. | Both caught at 0.73-0.75 (all groups) in LA, LB and PLA, and EQUALLY in FW_FLAT (0.733 / 0.753), a world with no tablet coherence. Cause found: the splice reuses fragments of the same source tablets in several forgeries, which fall in different CV folds, so the discriminator memorises fragments (uni, tbig, ngram). | ARTEFACT (leakage), also behind cycle-1 E_splice (0.730). Fixed in 14.3e. |
| la14:LA-14.3e | Leak-free splice E_splPair: tablets paired without replacement, each pair gives a-head + b-tail and b-head + a-tail (line count of the head tablet kept), both forgeries in one CV group. 8 seeds. | LA all-groups 0.504 (COPY0 0.473; no group significant, best arith 0.538, rep 0.549); LB 0.500; FW_FLAT 0.491; PLA long 0.774* (planted agreement still found). | Two halves of different LA tablets joined at a line boundary are NOT detectable: no long-range agreement between the top and the bottom of a tablet at this size (upper bound). The planted control shows the test would have found one. |
| la14:LA-14.3 | Cycle verdict | Every survivor is accounting structure, not grammar: a tablet keeps one number scale, repeats its line template and its values, ends sometimes with a total, and on HT orders commodity sections from large to small. Head-to-tail agreement, word-number pairing and line order are not detectable. One lexical lead (same-initial entries side by side outside HT) is grade C. | No reading. The contest is a useful detector of document structure and a strong warning about two artefacts (re-estimation and fragment leakage). |
| la14:LA-14.final | 292 LA tablets; 24 forgers of rising power (sign unigram; sign Markov 1-4; word Markov 1-2; the la8 flat form; a feed-forward neural LM; 15 copy-and-edit forgers) trained on one half; 11 feature groups + 5 learner types on the other half, group-CV AUC. About 16,000 forger/discriminator pairings plus bootstraps. Controls: forger-vs-forger null; the forger's own world (negative); a planted head-to-tail agreement (PLA) and Linear B (positive); planted kills for every targeted test. | Null 0.45-0.55 everywhere. Raw AUCs of 0.83-0.98 against every generative forger are mostly a finite-sample artefact: a forger is caught at 0.73-0.78 in its own world. Splice forgers leak through reused fragments (fixed; leak-free splices give 0.50). Planted agreement found in every variant (long +0.26-0.30; 43-52 of 72 top features are the planted pairs). Survivors after calibration: (A) number-scale coherence on a tablet, T1 0.683 vs 0.907 site+context null, P=0.002; (A) line-template repetition, repeated words and values, bootstrap |z| 4.5 to 25 against every forger; (A, known) exact totals at corpus level, z +4 to +6, but invisible tablet by tablet; (B) on HT, commodity sections run from large to small amounts, 0.601 descending, P=0.0005; (B) token-kind order, tbig +0.086 vs the world; (C) outside HT, consecutive entries share their first sign, 0.145 vs 0.092, P=0.001. Not detected (upper bounds): head-to-tail agreement, which number goes with which word, line order, logogram swaps, joins of two tablets at a line. | The hidden structure is accounting (scale, templates, totals, size order), not agreement or grammar. Linear A tablet halves are interchangeable at this corpus size. Nothing here is a reading. |
| la17:LA-17.final | Words (and, separately, sign types) as pathogens, 13 sites with >= 10 word tokens as hosts. Per-word SI/SIR outbreak in C on a gravity x distance contact network with an unknown source, unknown adoption times, founder copying, loss, Zipf popularity and effort-calibrated detection; cycle 2 adds an unknown deposit (sampling) time per site so turnover can seriate. Likelihood-free inference: 390,000 simulated outbreaks (LA words 160k + 80k, LB 40k + 30k, signs 70k), rejection ABC (0.2 %) and ABC random forests on 121 summaries; plus a simulator-free turnover seriation (pair z vs 300 document-label permutations, geography regressed out, Fiedler ordering). Dates never input; deposit phase per site (median find context: PH MM II ... HT/KH/ZA LM IB) used only to score. Controls: 1,000 held-out prior outbreaks; a planted PH-source outbreak ordered by distance (20 reps); 80 outbreaks planted by a different generator (lexicon copying); doc-label shuffled incidence (30); Linear B KN (LM II-IIIA1) vs PY TH MY TI KH (IIIB). | Source posterior is flat in every version: LA words TH 0.15 / TY 0.12 (flat 0.077), all rejection 95 % rank intervals [1-13]; LA signs HT 0.24, but shuffles also give HT 30/30. Planted PH source recovered 1/20 (cycle 1) and 0/20 (cycle 2, order rho -0.36); alternative-generator outbreaks: source 0.05 (chance 0.077), order rho -0.01. LB: KN first in two of four runs, but shuffled LB makes KN (words) or PY (signs) the source in 29-30/30 with the same order, i.e. document mass, not history; the deposit-time run puts KN 3rd. Outside-corpus test: inferred LA order vs deposit phase rho -0.18 (P 0.73), 0.28 (P 0.17), -0.01 (P 0.52); turnover seriation |rho| 0.43 (P 0.14). PH, the only MM II site, is placed 9th-12th of 13 in every run. Direction-only hint: pairs farther apart in date share fewer words (LA -0.21 z/phase, P 0.25; LB -2.1, P 0.16); PH-HT (3 km, MM II vs LM IB) z -1.6 vs -0.0 for other near pairs. The strongest sharing structure is IO-SY-PK (z 4-4.7), the libation-formula sanctuaries: genre, not contagion. | NOT CRACKED; METHOD FAILS ITS CONTROLS. The site x word matrix (63 % endemic words, 43 shared types) holds no recoverable transmission history: source and order are not identifiable (planted < 3/20; alternative generator at chance), and every apparent signal is reproduced by shuffled incidence. No inferred source or order is reported as a finding. Grades: "Linear A spread from site X" - none (no grade; posterior flat). Time-separated sites share fewer words than distance predicts (LA and LB, same sign, neither significant) C - would support: the same negative date-difference coefficient at P < 0.05 when more MM II-III material (e.g. Phaistos, Malia, Knossos MM III) is added; would kill: a zero or positive coefficient on a larger dated set. Sanctuary sites (IO, SY, PK, with PE/MA) form a vocabulary cluster beyond chance B (z 4-4.7 against document-label permutation; known in kind: the libation formula). |
| la17:LA-17.1a | LA source and adoption order: ABC-RF (classifier for source, regression forest for 13 adoption times) and rejection ABC (0.2 % nearest of 159k). Planted control 1: 1,000 held-out prior outbreaks. | Held-out (in-model) source accuracy 0.137 (chance 0.077), 90 % sets cover 0.93, order Spearman 0.45. LA posterior: TH 0.15, TY 0.11, AR 0.10, PE 0.10 (flat = 0.077); rejection: TH, MA, ZA, KN all 0.09. Predicted adoption times all 0.35-0.47 (prior mean 0.46); rejection 95 % rank intervals for every site span [1-13] (TH [1-13] mean 5.7 ... HT 7.3). | FLAT POSTERIOR. The incidence matrix carries almost no information on the source; the order estimate does not leave the prior. |
| la17:LA-17.1b | Planted control 2: outbreak with known source PH and adoption by distance from PH (20 replicates, beta 3, phi 0.5). | P(PH) 0.08, PH top-1 in 1/20; order Spearman with truth 0.57. | PLANTED SOURCE NOT RECOVERED (1/20 top-1); order partly recovered in-model. |
| la17:LA-17.1c | Outside-the-corpus test: inferred order vs deposit phase (permutation P, 20,000 perms); baseline "biggest site first" (effort) with no epidemic. | RF order TH < ZA < AR < PE < MA < TY < KH < PK < KN < PH < SY < IO < HT: rho -0.18 (P 0.73). Rejection mean ranks rho 0.22 (P 0.23). Effort baseline rho -0.41 (big sites are LATE: HT, KH, ZA are LM IB). PH (the only MM II site) is placed 10th of 13. | NO MATCH with the dates. |
| la17:LA-17.1d | Shuffled control: document site labels permuted (30 replicates), same forest. | Max P 0.17 (obs 0.15), entropy 2.47 (obs 2.49, flat 2.56); date rho 0.09 +- 0.21 (obs -0.18; 93 % of shuffles match the dates at least as well). Shuffles converge on KH (27/30). | Real data are indistinguishable from shuffled incidence. |
| la17:LA-17.1e | Linear B control: KN (LM II-IIIA1) must come first vs PY TH MY TI KH (LH/LM IIIB). | RF source KN 0.31 (chance 0.17), order KN < KH < TI < MY < PY < TH (KN rank 1); rejection ABC KN 0.14 (5th). BUT shuffled LB incidence gives P(KN) 0.66 in 30/30 replicates. In-model held-out accuracy 0.32. | CONTROL PASSES ONLY SPURIOUSLY: KN "first" is produced as strongly by shuffled data, i.e. by KN's document mass, not by transmission structure. Counted as a FAILED control. |
| la17:LA-17.1 | Cycle verdict | With all sites observed at one time, an epidemic on a symmetric shared-word matrix cannot tell who infected whom: the only directional signal is richness per effort, which is genre/size, not time. | NOT SUPPORTED. Source and order unidentifiable (< 3/20 planted recovery). Next (cycle 2): let each site be sampled at its own unknown deposit time with word loss (SIR turnover): sites sampled at similar times should share more words, which is a seriation signal that symmetric data CAN carry. |
| la17:LA-17.2a | ABC-RF and rejection ABC for the source and the 13 deposit times. Held-out planted outbreaks (1,000). | In-model: source accuracy 0.22 (chance 0.077), deposit-order Spearman 0.46. LA source posterior TY 0.12, TH 0.12, PE 0.11, AR 0.10 (rejection: IO 0.13, TH 0.11, PK 0.09, PH 0.09); every rejection 95 % rank interval spans [1-13]. | FLAT POSTERIOR for the source. |
| la17:LA-17.2b | Planted outbreak, source PH, adoption and deposit by distance from PH (20 reps, gamma 1). | P(PH) 0.07, top-1 0/20; deposit-order Spearman with truth -0.36. | PLANTED CONTROL FAILS (0/20, upper bound < 3/20); recovered order is anti-correlated with the truth. |
| la17:LA-17.2c | Outside-the-corpus test: inferred deposit order vs deposit phase; shuffled (doc-label) incidence, 30 reps. | RF order IO < TH < TY < KN < PK < HT < MA < AR < PH < ZA < KH < SY < PE: rho 0.28 (date-permutation P 0.17); rejection rho 0.07 (P 0.41). Shuffles give rho -0.22 +- 0.22, so 3 % of shuffles reach 0.28. PH (MM II) is 9th of 13. | NO MATCH at the pre-set test (P 0.17). The shuffle-relative P 0.03 is one of several looks and the planted control has failed, so it is not evidence. |
| la17:LA-17.2d | Linear B control (deposit-time version): KN must be sampled first. | RF order TI < PY < KN < KH < TH < MY (KN 3rd of 6); rejection KN mean rank 3.72 (5th). Shuffles: KN top in 29/30 (P 0.34). | LB CONTROL FAILS. |
| la17:LA-17.2e | Turnover read directly (no simulator): per-pair shared-word z against a document-label permutation null (300 perms); geography regressed out; spectral seriation (Fiedler vector) of the residual; pair z regressed on log distance + |deposit-phase difference| with dates permuted over sites (5,000). LB same. | LA seriation KH < KN < PH < MA < PE < SY < TY < PK < IO < TH < AR < ZA < HT: |rho| 0.43 with dates (P 0.14); unresidualised 0.17 (P 0.60). Date-difference coefficient -0.21 per phase (pairs farther apart in time share fewer words), perm P 0.25. PH-HT (3 km apart, MM II vs LM IB) z -1.6 vs mean -0.0 for the 28 other pairs < 60 km. The strongest structure is IO-SY-PK (z 4.0-4.7) and SY-PE-MA (1.7-1.8): peak-sanctuary/libation sites sharing one formula, i.e. genre. LB: KN at one end of the residual seriation (|rho| 0.65, P 0.34 with 6 sites); date-difference coefficient -2.1 (P 0.16). | Direction right in both scripts (time-separated pairs share less), significant in neither. The PH-HT deficit fits the turnover idea (C), but HT is under-shared with every site (endemism, LA-15). Genre clustering dominates the sharing network. |
| la17:LA-17.2 | Cycle verdict | Adding turnover does not make the source or order identifiable: planted PH outbreak inverted, LB control failed, LA date match P 0.17. | NOT SUPPORTED. Next (cycle 3): (i) test whether the inference is merely mis-specified by scoring outbreaks planted with a DIFFERENT generator (scribal lexicon copying); (ii) switch the pathogen from words to sign types, which are shared across sites and so carry far more cross-site signal. |
| la17:LA-17.3a | Misspecification control: 80 outbreaks planted by a different generator (each new site inherits 30-90 % of a donor's current lexicon, donor chosen by distance; local coining, 15 % turnover per step, 5 % borrowing; documents drawn by site-specific Zipf at the real LA token counts; source and order random and known), scored by the ABC-RF trained on 160,000 epidemic-simulator outbreaks. | Source top-1 0.05 (chance 0.077), mean P(true source) 0.075; order Spearman -0.01 [5-95 %: -0.50..0.36]; the no-epidemic effort baseline -0.02. | FAILS: on data where a real founder-copying history exists and is known, the inference recovers nothing. Any order it reports for LA is not evidence of history. |
| la17:LA-17.3b | Pathogen = syllabic sign type (139 types, 644 site-sign cells; effort = sign tokens), same simulator (300 lineages), 40,000 outbreaks; held-out planted; doc-label shuffle (30); dates only for scoring. | In-model source accuracy 0.12 (chance 0.077), order rho 0.41. LA source HT 0.24, KH 0.10, IO 0.09 (rejection HT 0.15). RF order HT < PK < KH < MA < SY < TH < KN < AR < TY < IO < PE < PH < ZA: rho with dates -0.01 (P 0.52); rejection -0.31; effort baseline -0.42. Shuffled incidence: HT top in 30/30 (max P 0.21). | HT "source" is an effort artifact (shuffles give it too). NO MATCH with the dates; PH (MM II) again near the end. |
| la17:LA-17.3c | Linear B, sign pathogen (89 types, 6 sites, 30,000 outbreaks): KN must be first. | RF source PY 0.36, KN 0.32; RF order KN < PY < TI < MY < TH < KH (KN 1st, rho 0.66, P 0.17); rejection PY 3.2, KN 3.4 (3rd). Shuffle: PY top 30/30 and the same date rho 0.65 in every replicate. | LB CONTROL FAILS: the KN-first order is produced identically by shuffled data (size ordering), and the source call is PY. |
| la17:LA-17.3 | Cycle verdict | Neither pathogen (words, signs), neither model (common time, own deposit time), and neither inference (rejection, random forests) passes the three controls together. Every apparent signal (KN first in LB, HT source in LA signs, LA date match P 0.03 relative to shuffles in 2c) is reproduced by shuffled incidence or fails the planted and alternative-generator tests. | NOT SUPPORTED. The site x word matrix of a script with 63 % site-endemic words has no recoverable transmission history at this sample size. |
| la19:LA-19.final | Arrow in the dark: Linear A words come from several languages, as in a trading port. Mixtures of K = 1-8 sign-level bigram phonotactic models (initial / final sign preferences, length via EOS; sign identities only) fitted to word types by collapsed Gibbs, about 31,000 runs over 65 corpora; K by 5-fold held-out likelihood (2-SE rule) and restart stability. Then (2) components vs site, support, commodity, number size, position, hand; (3) HT -> non-HT transfer and tablet homogeneity; (4) a minority tongue as outliers (per-sign surprisal under K=1). Controls: K=1-resampled LA (K=1 in 3/3), same generator twice (K=1 in 3/3), 10 planted 2-language mixtures in a shared syllable space (K >= 2 in 8/10; stable ARI 0.45-1.0; outlier test z 10-19 in 5/5 at 80/20), LB personnel names (K 5; attributes fire in about half the fits; outlier test KN z 2.6, non-Greek label z 6.8), Ur III and OB names, 36 single real languages. | LA best K = 4, gain 0.079 nats/word, stability ARI 0.17-0.18. But 33/36 single languages also choose K >= 2 (median gain 0.114); LA ranks 21/36 on gain and 25/36 on stability: LA is no more mixed than one language. Components track nothing outside the model (0 of 24 tests after Holm; none inside HT), do not transfer from HT to other sites (held-out gain negative), and cluster on tablets only by site (HT-only z 0.8-1.4). Outliers: Phaistos-only words (z 3.3) and roundel words (z 4.2) look foreign, but both effects vanish when words with rare or unnamed signs are removed (z 1.4, -0.5). | MANY TONGUES NOT SUPPORTED (B, calibrated): Linear A word types look phonotactically like ONE lexicon, no more heterogeneous than a single real language at the same size. Limit: a minority tongue < ~20% with phonotactics close to the majority (like sux+akk or jpn+tur at 80/20) would not be detected by the component test; the outlier test would see it (planted z >= 9.8), and sees none. Method lesson (A): 'K >= 2 by held-out likelihood' is the default for any real word list; mixture-K is not evidence of multilingualism without a single-language calibration. |
| la19:LA-19.1a | Linear A, all word types (952, 130 signs). Same-capacity single-language null: interpolated trigram (K=1), tuned on the same grid. | K_sel = K_best = 4; held-out gain over K=1 0.079 nats/word (K=2: 0.024). Trigram loses (z -8.7). Stability ARI 0.18 (K=2), 0.17 (K=4). Components: two big ones split on the initial sign (KU-, DU-, TU-, PI-, I-, SU- initial: KU-RO, KI-RO, A-DU, KU-PA3-NU ... vs SA-RA2, A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA, DA-RE, JA-SA ...), plus a 13-word bin of long words and *34 / *309B words. | K >= 2 on paper, but partitions unstable. See calibration. |
| la19:LA-19.1b | Entry words only (472 types followed by a number or logogram). | K_sel 2 (best 3), gain 0.024 nats/word, ARI 0.17. | Weaker than all words. |
| la19:LA-19.1c | Null: 3 LA-size lists resampled from the K=1 model fitted to LA. | K_sel = 1 in 3/3 (K=2 loses 0.03-0.05 nats/word). The trigram also loses on them (z -8.5 to -8.9). | Calibrated: no false K >= 2. The trigram test is uninformative (it loses on pure bigram data too). |
| la19:LA-19.1d | Planted 2-language mixtures in a shared syllable space (bigram generators of 2 real languages, LA size): fin+arb, jpn+tur, ell+eus, sux+akk, haw+kat at 50/50 and 80/20; same generator twice (fin, jpn, sux); two Dirichlet perturbations of LA's own bigram (c = 2, 5, 20). | 50/50: K >= 2 in 5/5, stability ARI 0.45-0.91, ARI to truth 0.24-0.70. 80/20: K >= 2 in 3/5 (missed jpn+tur, sux+akk). Same generator twice: K = 1 in 3/3. LA perturbations: K = 2, ARI to truth 0.59-1.0. | Method has power for a substantial second tongue (>= 20% and phonotactically distinct); planted mixtures are STABLE (ARI 0.45-1.0). |
| la19:LA-19.1e | Positive control: LB personnel names (full 1,636; three LA-size draws), single-site KN and PY lists; Ur III (Sumerian + Akkadian) and Old Babylonian seal names (LA size). | LB full K 5, gain 0.136; LA-size draws K 5, 1, 5 (gain 0, 0.057, 0.077); LB-KN K 1; LB-PY K 6 (!). Ur III K 3, gain 0.058, ARI 0.06; OB K 2, gain 0.20, ARI 0.05. Stability ARI 0.05-0.31 on every real name list. | K >= 2 on known mixtures, but ALSO on a single site; real name lists never give stable components. |
| la19:LA-19.1f | Negative control: 36 single real languages (la11 held-out word lists, LA size, 18 families). | K_sel >= 2 in 33/36 (only cmn, tur, uig give 1). Gain at best K: median 0.114, 90th pct 0.216 nats/word; stability ARI median 0.38. LA: gain 0.079 = rank 21/36 (below the median); ARI 0.18 = rank 25/36. | KILLS the K test: a single real language almost always prefers K >= 2 (sublexicons, morphology). LA is, if anything, MORE homogeneous than a typical single language. |
| la19:LA-19.1 | Cycle verdict | K = 4 for LA is a property of any real lexicon, not evidence of several tongues. Planted tongues are recovered with stable components; LA's components are less stable than one language's. | MANY TONGUES NOT SUPPORTED at the component level (calibrated, B). |

K=4: site p 0.027 (Holm 0.19; the 13-word bin is Knossos-rich, z 4.2 on 4 tokens), all else p >= 0.25. | No independent support. |

LA-size draws: site p 0.0005-0.42, label p 0.0005-0.17. Over all 7 LB fits: site p < 0.05 in 4/7, label in 4/7. | The attribute test has partial power: on a real Greek + non-Greek name mix it fires in about half the fits. LA (1 of 24 raw p < 0.05, 0 after Holm) is therefore a moderate, not strong, negative. |
| la19:LA-19.2 | Cycle verdict | Components track no attribute in LA (site, support, commodity, number, position, hand), with or without the HT restriction. | NOT SUPPORTED (B, power partial). |

Stone-vessel words LESS surprising (z -4.4, formula repeats). Others |z| < 1.7. | Two outlier groups at nominal p < 0.005 (12 tests). |

But within-word shuffle keeps part of the truth signal on LB (word co-membership), so the real excess is LB 0.80 - 0.67 = +0.13 rows, +0.03 columns. |

Columns: the two column groups mix vowels; colAUC excess +0.10. Needs null spread (cycle 2). |
| la21:LA-21.1 | Cycle verdict | Method recovers consonant rows of a known syllabary at LA size (B-level control), vowel columns poorly. LA behaves like LB, not like its shuffles. | Promising; grade pending null distributions and ablation. |
| la21:LA-21.2a | Null spread: LA vs 5 within-word (W) and 5 global (G) shuffles; LB LA-sized samples (3 seeds) vs 3 W and 2 G shuffles. | LA row70 4 (W 0.8 +- 1.2, G 0.6 +- 0.8), col70 8 (W 0, G 0). LA consonant-row AUC vs LB-derived values 0.735 (W 0.617 +- 0.012, z 9.9; G 0.508 +- 0.038, z 6.0); column AUC 0.586 (W 0.504 +- 0.029, z 2.9; G 0.522, z 2.6). LB: rows 0.791 +- 0.006 (W 0.678 +- 0.017, G 0.521); columns 0.625 +- 0.025 (W 0.615 +- 0.028, G 0.519). | Rows: PASS on the LB control against both nulls (+0.11 over W). Columns: FAIL on the LB control (real 0.625 = W null 0.615): what looks like vowel recovery is word co-membership, so LA's column result cannot be read. |

Stable row pairs drop to 1 (LA, LB). | Both universals add real-signal recovery on the control and on LA in the same direction; the likelihood alone already beats its shuffle. |

Without a shuffle null these are upper bounds. |

| la21:LA-21.3a | Held-out replication. Grids built separately on disjoint halves (top 50 signs each): LA Hagia Triada (439 types) vs all other sites (542); LB control Knossos-only vs Pylos-only words, sampled to the same type counts (2 seeds); within-word-shuffled halves as null. Survivors = pairs sharing a row in >= 50% of half-A runs; re-test = their row-sharing rate in half B. | LB: 13 / 10 row survivors -> 0.45 / 0.49 in the other half (all pairs 0.06), rank correlation of row co-assignment 0.40 / 0.41; shuffled LB halves 5 -> 0.15 (0.08), rho 0.09. LA: 8 survivors -> 0.045 (all 0.049), rho 0.17; shuffled LA halves 3 -> 0.01, rho 0.11. Truth (outside check) per half: LA HT 0.696, non-HT 0.609 (shuffled halves 0.565 / 0.600); LB 0.71-0.83. Pure-vowel row: A 0.99 / 1.00 in both LA halves, QA 0.48 / 0.58 (shuffled 0.15 / 0.32), I only in non-HT (0.75). | LB rows replicate across sites (PASS); LA's specific row pairs do NOT (0/8 above base). Caveat: LA halves have 28-35% fewer bigrams than the LB halves. Non-HT agreement with LB values is at its shuffle level. |
| la21:LA-21.3b | Grid-size sensitivity: (rows incl. vowel row x columns) 13x4, 15x3, 17x6, 18x5 besides 15x5, on LA, LB (LA-sized) and LA within-word shuffle. | LA consonant-row AUC vs LB values 0.708-0.730 (shuffle 0.607-0.638); LB 0.749-0.786. Columns: LA 0.579-0.599, LB 0.595-0.636, shuffle 0.517-0.534. | The row-level agreement does not depend on the grid size; columns stay at the level that LB shows is reachable from word co-membership alone. |
| la21:LA-21.3 | Cycle verdict | Whole-corpus LA rows agree with LB-derived consonant series beyond both shuffles at every grid size, but the specific LA groupings do not replicate across site halves, while LB's do. | The agreement is a diffuse statistical tendency (B), not a recoverable grid. No new sign grouping survives held-out re-test. |
| la21:LA-21.final | Stability, nulls (within-word and global shuffles), positive controls (LB at LA size, full LB, Japanese, Ancient Greek, Hawaiian syllable tables), LL-only ablation, held-out site halves (LA HT vs non-HT; LB KN vs PY), 5 grid sizes. | Controls: LB consonant rows recovered (AUC 0.79 +- 0.01 at LA size, 0.84 full; within-word shuffle 0.68, global 0.52; stable pairs mostly correct and replicate KN -> PY at 0.45-0.49 vs 0.06). Vowel columns are NOT recovered beyond word co-membership (LB 0.625 vs shuffle 0.615). Other languages: rows 0.63-0.71, columns only Japanese. LA: stable where shuffles are not (row70 4 vs 0.6-0.8; col70 8 vs 0); rows agree with LB-derived consonant series at AUC 0.724-0.735 (W 0.617 +- 0.012, z 9.9; G 0.508, z 6.0), at every grid size (0.71-0.73), and ablating the universals lowers it (0.647). But LA row pairs do not replicate across HT / non-HT halves (8 survivors -> 0.045 vs base 0.049). Stable LA groups: pure-vowel row {A, U, I (+O)}; QA lands there (0.70; in both halves 0.48 / 0.58). LA column clusters {A DA KA KU PA KO} vs {NA NE TI TE RA RE} split initial vs final word slots, not vowels. | B: Linear A's blind consonant-row structure, built only from universals, matches the Linear B-derived consonant series (independent confirmation of the la5 consonant-avoidance result, now without values as input). C: QA behaves like a pure vowel (word-initial, OCP-free). Would support: QA in the vowel row on new texts and in a larger HT-only or non-HT-only run; would kill: QA falling to its shuffle level (0.15-0.32) in a larger or re-sampled run. Killed: any specific new sign grouping (none replicates across halves); vowel columns (no power on the LB control). |
| la20:LA-20.final | Arrow in the dark: THE PECKING ORDER. Each pair of items in one list = a contest won by the one written first; BT, PL, Elo, mean position, Bayesian BT (MCMC), mixtures of K=1-4 orders, fitted to all LA tablets; held-out pair-order prediction on unseen tablets. Controls: within-list shuffles (200-500, family-wise max over 26 configurations and over K); Pylos town order and LB lists at LA size (positive); planted hierarchies and planted 2-order mixtures in LA-sized data; an amount-driven null. | Words, entry words, first signs: no pecking order (0.51, 0.52, 0.52; FWER >= 0.49) while LA-sized Pylos words give 0.61 (10/10). Logograms: 0.715 (FWER 0.005), beats an amount-driven null (0.548, P 0.002), transitive (cyclic triads 0.038 vs 0.098), robust to side-splitting and to dropping adjunct signs; CYP, GRA (*307) > VIR, OLE > OLIV, VIN, QA2; not ranked by quantity (rho 0.31, P 0.17); HT order predicts other sites at 0.655 (P 0.045). Pylos canonical order recovered only when full lists exist. | Commodity pecking order: A (existence), B (shared beyond HT), known in kind (grain-first sequences on HT tablets). No word hierarchy: B (calibrated negative, but blind to canonical tours written only in pieces). *307 as a top-rank (grain-class) commodity: C. |
| la20:LA-20.1a | Words (W): 232 lists, 776 types (163 recur), 1,462 testable held-out pairs (1,079 unmet). BT/ELO/POS vs 200 within-list shuffles. | BT 0.509 (null 0.504 +- 0.024, z +0.2); ELO 0.527 (z +0.9); POS 0.504 (z -0.1). Unmet pairs: BT 0.510, ELO 0.523 (z +0.7). FWER >= 0.82. | No global word pecking order (calibrated negative, see 20.1e/20.2a). |
| la20:LA-20.1b | Entry words (E: word directly before a number/logogram, removes headers): 181 lists, 773 testable pairs (585 unmet). | BT 0.521 (z +0.6), ELO 0.502, POS 0.505; unmet 0.488-0.509. FWER >= 0.94. | Negative: the people/places named in LA entries are not written in a fixed rank or tour order that recurs across tablets. |
| la20:LA-20.1c | Logograms (L, base sign): 82 lists, 48 types (19 recur), 235 testable pairs (31 unmet). BT, PL, ELO, POS. | BT 0.715 (null 0.500 +- 0.054, z +4.0, P 0.005, FWER 0.005 = floor of 200 reps); PL 0.715 (z +4.1); ELO 0.689; POS 0.694. Unmet pairs only 31: BT 0.613 (z +1.2), ELO 0.677 (z +1.8, P 0.055), FWER > 0.37. | A real commodity pecking order survives the search-size correction. Too few unmet pairs to show transitivity on its own (see 20.3c). |
| la20:LA-20.1d | First signs of words (F): 232 lists, 131 signs, 3,542 testable pairs (829 unmet). | BT 0.524 (z +1.2, P 0.09), POS 0.529 (z +1.5, P 0.045 raw), unmet 0.540-0.545 (z +1.4). FWER >= 0.49. | Not significant after correction. |
| la20:LA-20.1e | POSITIVE CONTROL, Linear B Pylos (DAMOS, 716 PY lists). (i) Place order: the 16 district towns (9 Hither + 7 Further; spelling variants mapped to slot ids by stems, control side only); the relative order of places in the 22 lists naming >= 2 of them; Kendall tau of the fitted ranking vs the canonical order; 200 shuffles. (ii) Same lists without the 6 lists naming >= 6 towns, and with only lists naming < 4. (iii) General held-out pair accuracy on PY words, logograms, first signs, full and subsampled to LA's list counts (10 draws, each vs its own shuffle). | (i) All lists: tau BT 0.43 (null 0.00 +- 0.17, P 0.005), ELO 0.60 (P < 0.005), PL 0.55 (P < 0.005), POS 0.40 (P 0.02). BT order P01 P04 P02 P03 P16 P06 P05 P07 / P11 P14 P15 P13 P08 P12 P10 P09: Hither towns mostly on top. (ii) Without the 6 long lists: tau -0.03 to +0.03 (P 0.48-0.69); short lists only: -0.12 to -0.24. (iii) PY words 0.598 (null 0.500 +- 0.012); LA-sized 0.614 vs 0.497, 10/10 draws beat their shuffle. PY logograms 0.820 (LA-sized 0.826 vs 0.493, 10/10). PY first signs 0.579 (LA-sized 0.569, 10/10). | Positive control passed for the existence test: at LA size Linear B lists carry a strong word pecking order (0.61), LA does not (0.51). Canonical order is recovered only when some lists spell it out in full; from short partial lists the rankers fail (tau ~ 0). So "no LA word order" means no recurrent long canonical list; it cannot exclude a canonical tour that LA scribes only ever wrote in pieces. |
| la20:LA-20.2a | PLANTED hierarchy (positive control): real LA lists keep their item sets; every item gets a hidden score N(0,1) x strength s; a fraction f of lists is re-ordered by a Plackett-Luce draw on it (Gumbel keys), the rest shuffled. 10 plants per cell, s in {0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4}, f in {0.25, 0.5, 1}; detection = held-out BT accuracy above the 95th percentile of the cycle-1 shuffle null (W 0.542, L 0.589). | Words: detected 10/10 at s 1 f 1 (acc 0.66), s 2 f 0.5 (0.60), s 4 f 0.5; 7/10 at s 0.5 f 1; 6/10 at s 1 f 0.5; <= 3/10 when only a quarter of lists follow (except s 4: 6/10). Logograms: 10/10 at s >= 1 with f 1, and s 4 f 0.5; 4/10 at s 0.5 f 1. Planted order recovered with tau 0.59-0.78 (recurrent items) where detected. | The pipeline finds a moderate hierarchy (s >= 1) that most lists follow. Real LA words (0.509) are below what a planted s 0.5, f 1 order gives (0.561): any word hierarchy is weak or followed by a minority of lists. Real logograms (0.715) sit at a planted s ~1, f 1. |
| la20:LA-20.2b | MIXTURES of K = 1..4 hidden orders (EM over weighted BT components, 2 restarts), held-out composite log-likelihood gain over a coin-flip order; the max over K is taken in each of 20-30 shuffled replicates (search-size correction). Positive control: planted 2-order mixture (half the lists follow a hidden order, half its reverse, s 2), 3 plants. | Logograms: K=1 +6.8 nats, best K=2 +7.2 (null max -1.0 +- 1.2, P 0.032 = floor of 30); a second order adds only 0.4. First signs: best K=4 +0.55 vs null max -3.8 +- 2.1 (P 0.032 floor), K=1 -2.5 (below coin flip). Words: best K=4 -2.4 vs -6.0 +- 3.4 (P 0.095); entry words -2.6 vs -5.0 +- 2.7 (P 0.24). Planted 2-order mixture: found (K=2 best, gains +3.6 to +16.9) only at first-sign size; not found at word, entry-word or logogram size (gains <= +1.2). | One commodity order is enough; no evidence of several competing hierarchies. First signs: the fit overfits less than shuffles but never beats a coin flip, so no usable order (the la14 large-first effect is a likely source). Mixture searches are blind at LA word size: no conclusion about several hidden word orders. |
| la20:LA-20.3a | Amounts. In held-out lists: accuracy of "larger amount first" vs the training ranking (BT); BT on pairs whose amounts are equal or missing; BT where it contradicts the amounts. Null: within-list shuffles. | Logograms: larger-first 0.665 (n 161); BT 0.715 (n 235); BT on pairs without an amount order 0.661 (n 115; null 0.563 +- 0.061, z +1.6); where BT and amounts disagree BT wins 0.60 (n 45). Entry words: larger-first 0.571 (n 2,110), BT where it contradicts amounts 0.39 (n 250). First signs: larger-first 0.606, BT on pairs w/o amount order 0.530 (null 0.507 +- 0.020), BT vs amounts 0.40. | For words and first signs the only order is the known "large amounts first" (la14). For logograms the order competes with amounts and partly wins (tested properly in 20.4a). |
| la20:LA-20.3b | Sites: fit on Hagia Triada lists, predict the order in other sites' lists, and the reverse (200 shuffles of the training lists). | Logograms HT -> other sites: 0.655 (n 55 pairs, null 0.490 +- 0.101, P 0.045); others -> HT: 0.629 (n 159, null 0.509 +- 0.093, P 0.105). First signs: 0.516 (P 0.31) and 0.501 (P 0.50). Entry words: 0.484 and 0.513 (n 95, 113). | Weak sign that the commodity order is shared beyond Hagia Triada (one direction at P 0.045, the other P 0.1; small n). Nothing shared for words or first signs. |
| la20:LA-20.3c | Transitivity: among item triples where every pair has a majority verdict, the share of cyclic (rock-paper-scissors) triads, vs shuffles. | Logograms: 6 cyclic of 157 triads (0.038) vs null share 0.098, P 0.035. First signs 0.165 vs 0.175 (P 0.12). Entry words 0.007 vs 0.008 (P 0.23; almost every triad comes from one list). | Logogram verdicts are more transitive than chance: a single scale, not a set of local habits (B). |
| la20:LA-20.3d | Bayesian Bradley-Terry by random-walk Metropolis (150k steps for L, 60k for E/F; N(0,1) prior): posterior median rank and 90% interval. | Logograms (48 types): top *307 (n 3; 0-25), CYP (n 24; 0-14), GRA (n 43; 1-15), VIR (n 23; 3-26), OLE (n 35; 5-26); bottom OLIV (n 16; 12-43), VIN (n 27; 18-43), QA2 (n 9; 15-45), *OLIV (n 3; 17-47). Entry words / first signs: all 90% intervals span most of the range (e.g. SA-RA₂ 38-233 of 562; first sign DI 12-65 of 131). | Commodity order: CYP, GRA (and VIR, OLE) before OLIV, VIN (QA2 late). Words and first signs: no stable rank. |
| la20:LA-20.4a | Amount-driven null: every logogram list re-ordered by a Plackett-Luce draw on log(amount) (items without an amount get a random key), strength tuned so the share of larger-first pairs matches the real lists (0.665; tuned null 0.667). Held-out BT accuracy, real vs 500 such worlds. | Real 0.715 vs amount-driven 0.548 +- 0.048, P 0.002. | The commodity order is not a by-product of writing large amounts first (A for existence). |
| la20:LA-20.4b | Rank vs typical amount: Spearman between BT score and each logogram's median amount (12 logograms with >= 3 amounts), vs 500 shuffles. | rho +0.31 (null 0.00 +- 0.29, P 0.17). CYP ranks first with median 4; VIR third with median 26; VIN last with median 4.5. | High-ranked commodities are not the large-quantity ones. The order is by commodity, not by size. |
| la20:LA-20.4c | Robustness, each vs 500 within-list shuffles: sides not joined (each face its own list); Hagia Triada only; Khania only; only multi-letter commodity logograms (syllabic signs used as logograms dropped). | Sides separate: 91 lists, 0.726 (null 0.502 +- 0.047, P 0.002). HT only: 56 lists, 0.689 (P 0.002). KH only: 16 lists, 0.607 (null 0.507 +- 0.139, P 0.31). Commodity-only: 77 lists, 0.697 (P 0.002). | Robust at Hagia Triada and not an artefact of joining sides or of adjunct signs. Khania has too few lists to tell (0/16 is not evidence of absence). |
| r3:R-3.final | Grow the script in a box (see voynich/loops/r3_final.txt): 62,000 simulated societies evolving scripts for ledger or text worlds, ABC on 20-22 blind statistics; known-world controls Ur III, LB, Latin, Italian; shuffled corpora; held-out statistics | LA = named, numbered ledger lines (ledger share 0.78 / 0.97 / 0.90 across box 1, box 2, refinement), ~100-200-sign syllabary + tens of logograms, dividers written, no secrecy. Refined fit inside the pseudo-data cloud (2.72) and better than shuffled (3.26). Held-out: heaps, doubled signs, line-final KL pass; tablet purity of line-final words predicted 0.56 [0.47-0.70], real 0.28: miss. Controls: Ur III 'many people, few goods' never recovered; LB ledger recovered | 'One commodity carried down each tablet' (p_docgood 0.86) KILLED by its own prediction. Ledger-world label C (agrees with known structure; adds nothing new). |
| la25:LA-25.final | Destruction-season clock: 10^5-10^6 random agronomic calendars x destruction month per archive, ABC on commodity profiles of HT, KH, ZA, TY (LA) and KN, PY, TH, MY (LB). Controls as above. | Inferred months (flat posteriors, max 0.10-0.12 vs 0.083; 80% sets 9-10 months): HT MAP Jun / Mar / Nov, KH Aug / Sep / Jun, ZA Aug / Oct / Nov, TY Jan / Jan / Mar (free / transfer s 0.3 / transfer s 1.0): the MAP moves with each setting. Planted LA size, free base rates: true-month mass 0.087-0.094 (chance 0.083), unchanged at 64x size. Oracle base rates: 0.11-0.14, MAP +-1 month 0.5-0.6. LB control: KN, PY spring mass 0.43, 0.36 (core), 0.38, 0.32 (extended); LOO 0.50 (KN), 0.32 (PY); prior 0.33. Shuffled labels as informative or more (P 0.10, 0.76, 0.74). Agronomic vs random calendars P(agro) 0.48-0.52. One fire vs several: P(same) 0.49, calibration AUC 0.52-0.61 (free), 0.64-0.75 (oracle). HT split halves not recognised as simultaneous (0/20; planted gap 0.003). Outside check: no published season for any LM IB fire; the Zakros bowl of preserved olives is the only hint (autumn-winter if fresh). | KILLED (grade A, calibrated negative). No destruction month and no one-season/several-season verdict can be read from the commodity mix: base rates and season are confounded, and more tablets do not help. Grade C, outside the method: Zakros olives as an autumn-winter hint (support: fresh-olive stores in other LM IB fire layers; kill: shown to be cured olives). Lesson: a fire-clock needs an independent anchor (month names, dated intake records, or seasonal plant remains in the same burnt room). |
| la25:LA-25.1a | LA month posterior, 10^6 simulations (random agronomic calendar x month per archive). | HT MAP Jun p 0.114, 80% set 9 months; KH MAP Aug 0.116 (9 mo); ZA MAP Aug 0.122 (10 mo); TY MAP Jan 0.114 (9 mo). P(one month for all) 0.47 (prior 0.5). KL from uniform 0.012-0.033. A 10^5 test bank gave different MAPs (HT Aug, KH May, ZA May, TY Mar). | Posteriors are flat (max 0.12 vs 0.083) and MAPs move with the bank seed: no month inferred. |
| la25:LA-25.1b | Planted control, same generator, 300 LA-size datasets (prior draws; and strong clock gamma > 1.2, sigma < 0.4). | Mass on true month 0.087 / 0.089 (chance 0.083); MAP within +-1 month 0.33 / 0.33 (chance 0.25); 80% sets cover 0.82 / 0.81. | The method cannot recover even a planted strong clock at LA size with free base rates. Calibrated, not biased (coverage ~0.8). |
| la25:LA-25.1c | Null: commodity labels shuffled within each LA archive (200 shuffles), random-calendar model (harvest months uniform). | Summed KL real 0.089 vs shuffled median ~0.06, P 0.10 (per site P 0.10-0.55). ABC model choice agronomic vs random calendars: P(agro) 0.483. | Real data no more informative than shuffled labels; agronomic calendars fit no better than random ones. |
| la25:LA-25.1d | Positive control, Linear B KN, PY, TH, MY (core 5 categories; extended 8 with figs, livestock, wool). Blind to month names. | Core: KN mass Feb-May 0.425, PY 0.364 (prior 0.333); all four LB archives get MAP Jun-Jul (TH Sep). Extended: KN 0.379, PY 0.324. Planted LB-size strong clock: see c2b.log (LA-25.2 file). | Fails: LB archives are not placed in their argued spring; the common Jun-Jul MAP is a generator pull, not information. |
| la25:LA-25.1 | Cycle verdict | With free commodity base rates, base rate and season are confounded: an archive that records much grain may be post-harvest or a grain economy. The clock is blind at LA and LB size (planted 0.087 vs 0.083) and fails the LB control. | KILLED as a free-base-rate method. Next: anchor the base rates (oracle; transfer from LB). |
| la25:LA-25.2a | Oracle: commodity base rates known (sd 0.1), calendar still random-agronomic. Planted, 100 x 3 reps, LA and LB size. | LA size: mass on true month 0.109-0.117 (chance 0.083), MAP within +-1 0.45-0.50 (chance 0.25) with a strong clock; 0.095-0.099 / 0.33-0.43 with prior-random strength. LB size: 0.124-0.133 / 0.50-0.53. Coverage 0.81-0.91. | Even with base rates known, the clock locates a planted month only to about a season (MAP +-1 ~0.5), with < 0.14 posterior on the true month. Calendar uncertainty (decay time, floor, window) and terroir noise blur it. Ceiling of the idea at this size. |
| la25:LA-25.2b | LB leave-one-out: one archive held at Feb-May, the other inferred blind to month names (shared base rates and calendar). | KN fixed -> PY: MAP Jul, mass Feb-May 0.318 (prior 0.333). PY fixed -> KN: MAP Apr, mass Feb-May 0.502. Planted power of this setup (strong clock): mass 0.109, MAP+-1 0.53. | 1 hit, 1 miss. PY's grain-heavy profile pulls it to summer. Positive control FAILS overall (combined mass 0.41 vs 0.33, n = 2). |
| la25:LA-25.2c | Transfer LB -> LA (KN, PY fixed spring; LA base rates = LB + N(0, s_off), s_off 0.3 and 1.0). Nulls: LA commodity labels shuffled per site (200); random calendars (ABC model choice). | s_off 0.3: HT MAP Mar (p 0.118), KH Sep (0.112), ZA Oct (0.104), TY Jan (0.120); s_off 1.0: HT Nov, KH Jun, ZA Nov, TY Mar (all p <= 0.122, 80% sets 9-10 months). Shuffled labels give MORE concentrated posteriors than real data (P 0.755, 0.735). P(agro vs random calendar) 0.518 and 0.505. Planted power (s_off 0.3, strong) mass 0.094, MAP+-1 0.40. | KILLED. The MAPs change with s_off and seed; real data are less informative than label-shuffled data; agronomic calendars are not preferred over random ones. No destruction month for any LA archive. |
| la25:LA-25.2 | Cycle verdict | Anchoring base rates is the only way the clock can see anything, and even the oracle reaches only season-level resolution (MAP +-1 ~0.5) at LB size; the honest anchor (LB transfer) fails its own LB control. | KILLED for months. Method grade A (calibrated negative): commodity profiles of archives this size cannot date a destruction to a month. |
| la25:LA-25.3a | ABC model choice: all four LA archives (HT, KH, ZA, TY) burned in one month vs independent months (prior 0.5); pairwise P(same month). | P(one month) 0.490. Pairs 0.518-0.548 (prior 0.542). | No information either way. |
| la25:LA-25.3b | Calibration of 3a: AUC of P(same) on planted one-month vs different-month datasets at LA size. | Free base rates: AUC 0.52 (prior-random strength), 0.61 (strong clock). Oracle base rates: 0.64, 0.75. Mean P(same) 0.58 vs 0.56 (free, strong). | Even a planted single fire with a strong clock moves P(same) by 0.02 with free base rates. The test cannot say whether the LM IB sites burned in one season. |
| la25:LA-25.3c | Split-half reliability: HT documents split into two random halves (20 splits) that certainly burned together; P(equal month) HTa-HTb must exceed cross-site pairs. Planted version (strong clock, halves same month, third archive different, 200). | Real: HTa-HTb 0.629, HTa-KH 0.631, HTa-ZA 0.633, KH-ZA 0.627; HTa-HTb above every cross-site pair in 0/20 splits. Planted: gap 0.003. | Fails: the clock does not even recognise two halves of one burnt archive as simultaneous, in real or planted data. |
| la25:LA-25.3d | Outside check (archaeology, used only as a check): published evidence for the season of the LM IB fires. Web search (Zakros, Mochlos, Pseira, Khania, HT; LM IB destruction horizon). | Found: a bowl of olives with flesh preserved in the Zakros palace (olive picking Oct-Jan if fresh, but they may be stored or cured olives); no published seed or stored-crop study giving a season for any LM IB fire was found. The LM IB destructions are not shown to be simultaneous (some sites may be decades apart). | No check possible: the method gives no month and the archaeology gives no secure season. Zakros olives = grade C hint of autumn-winter (would support: fresh-olive morphology or unpressed olive stores in other LM IB fire deposits; would kill: the olives shown to be cured). |
| la25:LA-25.3 | Cycle verdict | Same-season and split-half tests are powerless at LA size (planted AUC 0.52-0.61; planted split-half gap 0.003). | KILLED. Whether the LM IB fires were one season or several cannot be read from the tablets' commodity mix. |
| la25:LA-25.4a | Free base rates, planted at x1 / x4 / x16 / x64 LA size (342 to 21,888 units). | Mass on true month 0.090 / 0.092 / 0.091 / 0.094 (chance 0.083); MAP within +-1 0.38 / 0.37 / 0.39 / 0.37 (chance 0.25). | Flat in size: more tablets do not help. The confound (commodity base rate vs season) is structural, not a sampling limit. |
| la25:LA-25.4b | Oracle base rates, same sizes. | Mass 0.116 / 0.120 / 0.134 / 0.139; MAP +-1 0.54 / 0.51 / 0.53 / 0.62. | Even 64 Hagia Triadas with known base rates give only season-level resolution, limited by calendar uncertainty (storage decay, floors, windows). |
| la25:LA-25.4 | Cycle verdict | No archive size makes a commodity-profile clock work without an independent anchor for what each site normally records. | Grade A (method, calibrated negative): destruction month is not recoverable from commodity mix; future finds will not change this unless they carry month names or dated intake records. |
| la23:LA-23.1a | Cross-site transfer at LA size: classifiers (logistic, gradient boosting) trained on LA-sized LB draws (919 word occurrences) from KN, scored on PY draws and vice versa, 12 reps x 2 directions. Controls: training labels permuted; test words shuffled across all word slots (position broken). | Logistic AUC 0.59 (5th pct 0.51), precision of p>=.5 set 0.58 vs base rate 0.52; GBM AUC 0.54. Label-permuted 0.50 / 0.51; slot-shuffled test 0.50. | Signal is real (beats both controls) but weak: at LA size, entry position and number size separate LB personal names from other words only slightly. Most LB non-names (places, adjectives) are rare entry words too. |
| la23:LA-23.1b | Blind two-component Gaussian mixture on the same features (no labels), component with lower frequency called 'person'. Scored on LB draws. | AUC 0.51 (chance). On LA, its ranking does not agree with the classifier (rho -0.10). | Killed: unsupervised separation finds frequency, not persons. |
| la23:LA-23.1c | Ensemble (16 LB-trained models) applied to all LA tablets; LA with words shuffled across slots as control. | HT tablet word types: 395; p ranges 0.46-0.71 (5-95 %); 86 % have p>=.5 (shuffled LA 74 %). Top 'person-like' list includes KI-RO and SA-RA2 (transaction words in la15) next to DE-JU-KU, DA-QE-RA, A-DU, KA-NA, PA3-NI-NA. | The classifier cannot make a usable person list for LA (compressed probabilities; known transaction words on top). Cycle 2 therefore runs two sets: 'clf' (membership sampled from p) and a blind operational 'strict' rule: mostly in entry position, at least once followed directly by a number <= 5, on <= 5 tablets (177 of 395 HT types). Rule precision is measured on LB in cycle 2. |
| la23:LA-23.1 | Cycle verdict | Person-like words cannot be separated from other entry words with confidence at LA size (calibrated AUC 0.59). | Grade B (calibrated negative): any population count of Linear A 'people' is a count of a set that is roughly 55-60 % persons at best. Counts below are of 'person-like entry words'. |
| la23:LA-23.2a | Planted populations of known size written onto the real HT tablet frame (same slots per tablet and findspots), 40 reps each; 'easy' (little heterogeneity, no locality, no homonyms/variants) and 'hard' (lognormal sigma 1.2, 70 % local to the findspot, 15 % homonyms, 10 % variant spellings). | Easy: Chao1 recovers N = 150 / 400 / 1,000 / 3,000 as 147 / 371 / 893 / 2,998; Chapman similar. Hard: Chao1 149 / 288 / 554 / 1,430 (under by up to 2x); Bayes Mh 322 / 722 / 850 / 1,634 (over at small N, under at large); log-linear 'best' unstable (upper limits to 10^8). | Estimators rank population sizes correctly but are off by up to 2x under realistic heterogeneity; log-linear models on 4 findspots or 4 commodity classes are unusable at this size. Raw estimates need calibration (done in cycle 3 by ABC). |
| la23:LA-23.2b | Linear B control: LA-sized draws (919 word occurrences) of KN and PY tablets, persons = series-derived name labels; occasions = top-3 hands + rest, top-3 series + rest. 60 draws per site. Truth: distinct labelled names in the full site corpus (KN 798, PY 963), itself a lower bound on the people. | KN: seen 214; Chao1 974 [611-1,449]; Chapman 684; Bayes Mh 1,602 [951-2,081]. PY: seen 178; Chao1 907 [554-1,742]; Chapman 750; Bayes Mh 1,078 [629-1,942]. | Pass for Chao1 and Bayes Mh: from an LA-sized sample they reach the number of distinct named people in the whole archive (intervals cover 798 / 963). Chapman falls short on KN (hand-specific lists). |
| la23:LA-23.2c | The 'strict' rule as a person detector, scored on the same LB draws. | Precision 0.66 (KN) and 0.36 (PY) vs base rate 0.53 / 0.52; recall 0.13 / 0.06. | Killed as a person detector: it is no better than chance across sites. HT counts below are counts of 'small-number entry words', of which perhaps half are personal names. |
| la23:LA-23.2d | Hagia Triada, strict set, 2,000 draws over ambiguity. | Seen 174 [164-182] after merges; Chao1 546 [468-677]; Chapman (Villa 92, Casa 108, both 26) 372 [318-439]; Bayes Mh 961 [563-1,595]; log-linear 4 findspots 862 [596-1,330]; commodity classes 497 [360-1,876]. No ambiguity: Chao1 545, Bayes 978. | Raw range 370-1,000; the heterogeneity-robust estimators sit at 550-1,000. |
| la23:LA-23.2e | Controls on HT: (i) findspot labels permuted across tablets; (ii) the complementary non-person entry words through the same pipeline. | (i) Chapman 363 vs 372, Chao1 552 vs 546: no change. (ii) Non-person words: seen 212, Chao1 913, Bayes 1,674. | (i) Villa and Casa do not deal with different people more than chance: no locality signal (upper bound; power is low with 26 shared names). (ii) The 'other' words are just as open-ended: the estimates count an open set of rare entry words, not a set that is specifically personal. Shuffling tablets cannot break frequency-based estimators, and it does not. |
| la23:LA-23.2 | Cycle verdict | The machinery passes its LB and planted controls only roughly (factor 2). On HT it gives a few hundred to ~1,000 distinct small-number entry words in use. | Grade B for the method calibration; grade C for any reading of the HT number as 'people' (cycle 1 and 2c: the set is not clean). |
| la23:LA-23.3a | Planted control: 60 pseudo-observed archives drawn from the reference table (true N known), each re-estimated with itself left out. | 90 % intervals cover the true N in 95 % of cases; median error factor 1.29 (|log| 0.25), bias +3 %. By N: 30-300 cover 0.95 (error x1.12), 300-1,000 0.93 (x1.22), 1,000-3,000 1.00 (x1.51), 3,000-20,000 0.94 (x1.57). | PASS within the model (optimistic: generator and plants share assumptions). |
| la23:LA-23.3b | Linear B control (the outside-truth test): LA-sized KN and PY draws (labelled names), ABC on the draw's own frame, then the posterior population re-written onto the FULL site frame (4,228 KN / 1,004 PY tablets with their name slots) to predict how many distinct names the whole archive shows. Truth: KN 798, PY 963. | KN: predicted 714 [470-990], 784 [559-992], 636 [461-864]; PY: 678 [445-928], 969 [679-1,152], 917 [755-1,231]. 5 of 6 intervals cover the truth; median ratio predicted/true 0.92. Implied LB population sizes N: KN 2,300-5,200, PY 3,400-5,500 (wide). | PASS: from an LA-sized sample the method predicts the number of distinct named people in the whole Linear B archive within ~10 %. |
| la23:LA-23.3c | Hagia Triada, strict set (177 small-number entry words, 246 captures on 80 tablets; Villa 93, Casa 110, both 26). | N = 1,020 [510-2,430] (rejection 1,010 [390-2,760]); sigma 1.23, locality 0.22 (low), homonyms 0.16, variants 0.11 (both near prior mean: not identified). Fit typical (42nd percentile of pseudo-observed fits). Predicted distinct names in a 5x larger HT archive: 480 [370-605]. | Grade B as a count of distinct small-number entry words in use: ~1,000 (500-2,400). As a count of people: grade C (cycle 1/2: the set is perhaps half persons). |
| la23:LA-23.3d | Same on the 'clf' set (p >= .5; 341 types, 537 captures). | N = 4,580 [2,660-7,660]. | This is the whole open vocabulary (cf. la15: >= 4,500 word types), not people. Shows the answer is set-dependent by 4x. |
| la23:LA-23.3e | Shuffle control: HT names shuffled across tablets (slot counts kept). | N = 1,040 [560-2,230] vs 1,020; summaries almost unchanged (both 29 vs 26). | The control does NOT break the estimate: it rests on how often each word recurs, not on which tablets it shares. No locality or co-listing structure is in the data at this size. Honest limit, not a pass. |
| la23:LA-23.3 | Cycle verdict | Calibrated on Linear B (whole-archive name counts predicted, 5/6 covered) and planted data (95 % coverage). HT: ~1,000 [510-2,430] distinct small-number entry words; the LB analogue gives 2,300-5,500 named people for KN/PY. | Method B. The HT figure is about a word set, not a proven set of persons. |
| la23:LA-23.4a | New names on a new tablet: incidence extrapolation (Chao et al. one-step, Q1/Q2) and the cycle-3 posterior predictive. Calibration on Linear B: LA-sized training draw, 30 held-out tablets of the same site, 200 reps. | LB KN: predicted 0.65 new names per tablet, observed 0.65; 30-tablet union 19.3 vs 19.3. PY: 2.01 vs 1.97; union 57.8 vs 57.0. Planted (hard) 150-3,000: union 4.7/4.5, 9.4/9.8, 14.3/16.1, 19.3/18.3. | PASS: the new-name rate is predicted within a few % on unseen LB tablets. |
| la23:LA-23.4b | HT room hold-out: each findspot held out, predicted from the other rooms; null = random hold-outs of the same size (2,000). | Villa Magazine 67 new vs 83 predicted (null 76, P(<=) 0.24); Casa Room 7 39 vs 28 (P(>=) 0.15); Casa del Lebete 17 vs 27 (P(<=) 0.14); Casa Room 9 22 vs 10 (P(>=) 0.045; Holm 0.18). | No room is significantly different after correction. Grade C: Casa Room 9 lists people (or places) not met elsewhere. Would support: new Room 9-type tablets again full of unseen entry words; would kill: P > 0.2 with more Casa tablets. |
| la23:LA-23.4c | Endemism: share of HT person-like words also written at another LA site vs the HT non-person words (frequency-matched label permutation, 2,000). LB analogue: KN names vs other KN words found at PY. | HT person-like 7.3 % vs other 6.9 % (P 0.67). LB: KN names at PY 16.8 %, other words 10.6 %. | Uninformative: in LB, names travel MORE than other words (common names), so endemism cannot mark persons. Dropped. |
| la23:LA-23.4d | Outside check against the settlement (published excavation data, used only as a check): excavated area at Hagia Triada ~135 x 135 m (~1.8 ha); Minoan town densities from comparable excavated towns (Mochlos 0.8 ha, 220-330 people; Gournia 1.68 ha, ~64 houses) give ~165-410 people per ha, so ~300-740 residents in the excavated area. | HT strict set ~1,020 [510-2,430] distinct small-number entry words; if 36-66 % are persons (LB precision of the rule), ~370-670 named people [~180-1,600]. LB analogue: KN/PY archives name thousands. | Consistent in order of magnitude with a town of a few hundred plus dependants, and an order of magnitude below the palatial LB archives. Weak check (wide intervals; person share unknown): grade C. |
| la23:LA-23.4e | Pre-registered prediction for the next HT tablet found (written before any new text is seen). | A tablet like the published ones carries on average 1.2 small-number entry words (3.1 on tablets that have any); of those, ~55 % (90 %: 0-5 names, median 1, mean 1.7 per list tablet) will be unseen in the current corpus. | Testable when a new HT tablet is published: would kill: fewer than 25 % or more than 85 % of its small-number entry words new across 5+ new tablets. |
| la23:LA-23.final | Treat small-number entry words on the 205 Hagia Triada tablets as individuals and count the population by capture-recapture: (1) blind person-like separation (identity-free features; LB-trained classifiers, cross-site and LA-sized; blind mixture); (2) Chao1, coverage, Chapman Villa vs Casa, log-linear M0/Mt/Mh/Mth on 4 findspots and 4 commodity classes, Bayesian beta-binomial Mh, 2,000 draws over homonym/variant ambiguity; (3) ~110,000 simulated archives (random N, heterogeneity, locality, homonyms, variants) for rejection ABC; (4) new-name prediction, room hold-outs, endemism, settlement check. Controls: LB KN/PY at LA size (whole-archive distinct-name counts as truth), planted populations, label-permuted and slot-shuffled words, findspot permutation, names shuffled across tablets, non-person words through the same pipeline. | Separation: LB-calibrated AUC 0.59 (controls 0.50); blind mixture 0.51; LA probabilities compressed, transaction words (KI-RO, SA-RA2) at the top; the operational 'strict' rule (entry, a number <= 5, <= 5 tablets; 177 HT types) has LB precision 0.66 / 0.36. Estimators: raw HT 370-1,000; ABC N = 1,020 [510-2,430] (strict), 4,580 [2,660-7,660] (clf set = open vocabulary). Controls: ABC planted coverage 95 %; LB whole-archive names predicted 5/6 (ratio 0.92); new names on held-out LB tablets predicted within 2 %. Shuffling names across tablets leaves N unchanged (1,040); findspot permutation too: no locality signal. New HT tablet: ~55 % of its small-number entry words unseen (mean 1.7 per list tablet). Settlement: ~1.8 ha excavated, ~300-740 residents at Minoan densities, vs ~370-670 named people if 36-66 % of the set are persons. | Grade B: the capture-recapture machinery is calibrated (LB archive counts, planted data) and HT used ~1,000 [510-2,430] distinct small-number entry words; ~55 % of such words on a new HT tablet should be new (pre-registered). Grade C: these are a few hundred people, the size of a town (+ dependants), an order of magnitude below Knossos/Pylos. Killed: blind person/non-person separation at LA size; endemism as a person marker; room-specific populations (Room 9 P 0.045, Holm 0.18). Not cracked; no reading. |
| la24:LA-24.1a | Per-list MDL: each list picks its own rule, grid and alphabet (143, then 49 alphabets x 13 rule-grid combinations, paid ~10 bits per list). Nulls: N3b per list (each distinct amount -> random corpus amount within +-25 %, same letter status, repeats kept; 100 + 200 extra for lists that compress), N1 amounts shuffled across lists (50), N2 amounts drawn from the corpus distribution (50). Positive controls: 40 planted apportionments of the same lengths in the LA corpus; 100 Ur III ration lists; 113 LB lists. | LA: 14/144 lists compress (summed gain 40 bits) vs N3b 24.6, N1 18.6, N2 20.3 (real below every null; upper P 1.0, 0.94, 0.98). 6/144 lists at p < 0.05 (7.2 expected), 0 at q < 0.1. Planted: 7/40 compress, 2/40 at p < 0.05, rule recovered 14/40 (chance about 8/40). Ur III rations: 1/100 compress (N3b 5.2). LB: 10/113 (N3b 21.6). | FAILS ITS POSITIVE CONTROLS. With the rule paid per list, a 3-6 entry list can never repay the hypothesis cost, and planted apportionments and real Ur III rations are not seen. The 'real below null' direction appears in every dataset, controls included, so it is a property of the method (real amounts are the cheapest for the baseline), not of Linear A. No list is explained. |
| la24:LA-24.1b | One clerk, one rule: rule, grid and alphabet chosen once per corpus; each list pays only u, class bits and misses; corpus score = sum of positive gains under the best global hypothesis (search re-optimised in every null). Nulls: N3c (band replacement drawn by corpus frequency, 60), N1 (60). Positive controls: 40 lists planted by ONE clerk (one rule, one alphabet) and by 3 clerks, in the LA corpus; Ur III; LB. | LA: best h = CEIL, S={1} (equal shares = 'repeated amounts'), score 272 vs N3c 337 +- 9 and N1 396 +- 33 (lower-tail P 0.016 for both). PLANT1 (one clerk): best h still CEIL {1}, score 233 vs N3c 293 (planted rule and alphabet not recovered). PLANT3: CEIL {3,4,6}, 226 vs 263. Ur III: CEIL grid 5 {4,5}, 29 vs N3c 98. LB: EXACT {1}, 238 vs 340. | FAILS ITS POSITIVE CONTROLS again: the corpus-level optimum is always 'equal shares', i.e. the repeat structure the baseline already knows (la14 grade A), and 40 planted one-clerk lists among 104 real ones do not move it. Every dataset sits below its value-perturbing nulls. The MDL-against-an-adaptive-baseline design cannot see apportionment at Linear A list lengths (median 4-5 entries). Method rejected. Next: a baseline-free fingerprint (cycle 2). |
| la24:LA-24.2a | Fingerprints counted per corpus: F1 remainder signature (a list holds q and q+1, q >= 3: the trace of a total split into equal shares and rounded); F1r (that cluster sums to a multiple of 5: a round total split); F2 common unit (>= 3 distinct values > 1, all u x w with w <= 8, u >= 2); F2r (u = 5, 10, 20, 25 or 50). Nulls (2,000 each): N1 amounts shuffled across lists (keeps the preference for round numbers), N1s shuffled within site, N3c frequency-weighted band replacement. Power: 20 planted largest-remainder/ROUND lists in the corpus. Comparators: 300 Ur III ration lists, 113 LB lists. | LA: F1 22 (N1 20.5, P 0.38; N1s P 0.46), F1r 0 (N1 2.0), F2 4 (N1 3.2, P 0.38), F2r 2 (N1 0.5, P 0.09). Planted: F1 26 vs N1 26.7 (P 0.63): F1 has no power. LB: F2 5 vs N1 1.1 (P 0.006), F2r 3 vs 0.1 (P 0.002). Ur III: F2 47 vs N1 103 (rations are everywhere multiples of 10 sila, so the shuffle raises the count), vs N3c 4.3 (P 0.002). | No apportionment fingerprint in Linear A. The common-unit test passes its Linear B comparator (P 0.006), so Linear A's 4/144 vs 3.2 is a calibrated negative for 'lists built on one unit share' (grade B, upper bound: < 3/144 beyond chance would be missed). The remainder signature has no power (planted control fails) and says nothing. |
| la24:LA-24.2b | Fraction absorption (F3): a fraction-bearing list whose entries (all but at most one, >= 3) are u x w (w <= 8) with u NOT an integer, i.e. the fractions carry the remainder of an exact division. Count over the 58 fraction lists under CONV (lineara.xyz values), LA1BIN (attack-1 binary set) and 3,000 random value sets (22-value pool). Specificity controls (3,000 each): CONV's values permuted among the letters; letter combinations shuffled among fraction amounts (CONV kept); random sets from simple values only (1/2, 1/4, 3/4, 1/8, 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 1/6, 1/16). Power: 200 planted fractional-u lists written in CONV letters. | CONV 15/58 vs random sets 9.1 +- 2.1 (P 0.011); LA1BIN 10 (P 0.38). Planted: found 1.00 with CONV, 0.13 with random sets (power is high). But: letter shuffle 13.8 +- 2.0 (P 0.36), simple-value sets 13.3 +- 2.7 (P 0.31), value permutation 10.6 (P 0.04). Lists hit under CONV: HT 8a, 20, 23a, 24b, 28a, 41a, 50a, 91, 98a, 115b, 126a, KH 6, 9, 58, PH 7b. Letter 'pinning' in the random sets (J 1/2, E 1/2, JE 3/4, L2 1/2) follows the simplicity of the values. | The fits do NOT constrain the fraction values. CONV beats arbitrary value sets only because its values are simple (halves and quarters): any simple set, and CONV with the letters scrambled, does as well. LA1BIN is not favoured either. Fraction readings stay as graded in FINDINGS (J = 1/2 C). |
| la24:LA-24.3a | Document sides; entries = recipient word (2+ signs, once per side) with one amount of one commodity. Every pair of sides sharing >= 2 words (same commodity): k = largest group of shared words whose ratios agree within 2 % (r != 1). Statistics: pairs with k >= 2, k >= 3, summed k; eq2 = pairs with >= 2 equal amounts. Nulls (2,000): P1 amounts permuted within each side (keeps the side's values, breaks word-amount links); P2 band replacement keeping repeats. Positive controls: Linear B (438 sides, 1,836 pairs), PY Es (Es 644-729, known proportional contributions), 15 planted LA pairs (second side = round(k x first)). | Controls: PY Es k2 27 vs P1 9.1 (P 0.0005), all LB k2 1,633 vs 1,543 (P 0.004); planted LA k2 11 vs P1 3.2 and P2 8.3 (P 0.0005, 0.0045), k3 7 vs 0.16. P2 kills even PY Es (28.7, P 1.0), because sides whose amounts are all equal are proportional under any value swap; P1 is the usable null. Linear A: 15 pairs only; k2 4 vs P1 2.5 (P 0.13), k3 2 vs 0.65 (P 0.11), summed k 10 vs 5.6 (P 0.044); P2 P 0.87. eq2 3 vs P1 1.9 (P 0.15). | Weak and not robust: P 0.04-0.13 against within-side permutation, nothing against band replacement. The method works (Linear B and planted pass), but Linear A has only 15 testable pairs. Grade C overall. |
| la24:LA-24.3b | The pair driving it: HT 9a / HT 9b. Within-side permutation P for that pair (20,000), and a scan of 22 candidate values of J for which the 3-word group survives. | HT 9b = 4/5 of HT 9a for *306-TU (10 -> 8), *324-DI-RA (2 J -> 2) and TA-I (AROM 2 J -> 2); DI-NA-U (4) and QE-PU (2) are unchanged; PA-DE 5 JE -> 3. Pair P = 0.016 (0.25 after the 15 pairs). The 4/5 group exists only if J = 1/2 (1 of 22 values). Written totals: 9a KU-RO 31 JE, 9b KU-RO 24 (= sum of 9b). The two HT 86/95 pairs (KU-NI-SU, SA-RU, DI-DE-RU, MI-NU-TE: 20 vs 10) are equal-amount sides (ratio 2 for every word), which P2 shows is trivial. | Grade C guess: HT 9b is a reduced (x 4/5) reissue of part of HT 9a's allocation, and then J = 1/2. Would support: another tablet pair whose shared recipients scale by one non-trivial factor with fractions that only work at J = 1/2 (or a photo check showing HT 9a's two J signs). Would kill: HT 9a *324-DI-RA or TA-I re-read without J, or the pair being from different commodities/sections. |
| la24:LA-24.final | Three cycles (tools/la24_*.py; C core la24_core.c). (1) MDL search, about 90 M (list, rule, alphabet) fits in all: 49-143 share alphabets x 5 rules (exact/fractions absorb, D'Hondt-, Sainte-Lague-, Adams-like divisor, largest remainder) x 3 grids, per list and as one clerk's global rule, against an adaptive baseline; nulls value-band replacement, cross-list shuffle, corpus draws; planted lists, Ur III ration lists (CDLI) and Linear B as positive controls. (2) Baseline-free fingerprints (remainder signature, common unit share, fraction absorption under 3,000 + 9,000 value sets with simplicity-matched controls). (3) Share-vector reuse across tablets (within-side permutation; PY Es and planted pairs as positive controls). | (1) Fails its positive controls: planted apportionments 2/40 found, Ur III rations 1/100; every dataset compresses less than its nulls. (2) Common unit: LA 4/144 vs 3.2 (P 0.38) while LB passes (P 0.006). Fraction absorption: CONV 15/58 beats random value sets (P 0.011) but not simple-value sets (P 0.31) or scrambled letters (P 0.36). (3) LA 15 pairs, k3 P 0.11, summed k P 0.044 (P1), nothing vs band null; HT 9a -> 9b x 4/5 for 3 recipients (pair P 0.016, 0.25 corrected), which needs J = 1/2. | NOT CRACKED; no tablet is explained beyond the null and no share classes or recipient ranks can be read. Grade B (calibrated negative): Linear A lists are not built on one unit share (LB comparator passes). Grade B (method): MDL apportionment search has no power at LA list lengths; fraction-absorption fits favour simple values, not specific letter values, so they do not test the fraction readings. Grade C: HT 9b is HT 9a scaled by 4/5 for three recipients, implying J = 1/2 (would kill: re-reading of the 9a J signs). |
| la27:LA-27.1a | Kendall cosine quantogram on 70 core masses 5-1600 g, q 8-130 g; max over the grid against 2,000 x 15 % multiplicative jitter and 2,000 log-uniform random mass sets; phi at the pre-registered 61 g | Best q 17.1 g (phi 2.45), then 53.4 and 68.4 g; P(jitter) 0.59, P(log-uniform) 0.71. At 61 g: phi 0.85, P 0.45. All 78 masses: best 53.4 g, P 0.62; lead only (49): best 68.6 g, P 0.72 | No quantum in these published masses. The 61 g 'Minoan unit' is not recovered from the data at hand (agrees with Cherry's unpublished result reported by Michailidou 1990: no 30 or 60 g quantum at Akrotiri) |
| la27:LA-27.1b | Planted control: 70 masses = k x 61 g x (1 + N(0, s)), k from {1/4,1/3,1/2,2/3,1,4/3,2,3,4,8,12,16,24}, 200 sets each | s 2 %: lattice found 0.85 (as the harmonic 61/4 or 61/3, which is the true lattice step of that k set), s 4 %: 0.81, s 6 %: 0.69; exactly 61 g at most 0.01 | The quantogram has power at this sample size for a clean (<= 6 % error) system, so 1a is a real negative for these masses: either the errors are larger (lead oxidation, mixed standards), or several standards are mixed (Michailidou, de Zwarte both argue for foreign standards at Akrotiri) |
| la27:LA-27.1c | Denominations: share of masses within 4 % of p/r x 61 g (r <= 4, up to 26 units) against 15 % jitter; sub-unit tally below one unit | 0.662 vs null 0.658 (P 0.55). Sub-units: 1/5 x5, 1/3 x2, 3/4 x2, 1/4 x1, 2/3 x1, 1/2 x0 | Nothing beyond chance; the grid is dense enough that any masses 'fit'. The 1/3 unit (20.2 g, twice, marked with a triangle on No 370) is a single real denomination, not a ladder |
| la27:LA-27.1d | Upper steps: masses >= 300 g (n 29) within 5 % of a multiple of k x 61 g, k 4-16; jitter null for the best k | Best k 4 (244 g; share 0.59), k 8 'mina' 0.38 (rank 4 of 13); P(best, jitter) 0.049 before correcting for 13 k values (about 0.5 after) | No mina step at 8 units in the masses. The 244 g hint is the Linear B N size (c. 250 g), grade C at most |
| la27:LA-27.1e | Ingot anchor: a typical oxhide ingot of c. 29 kg in units | 475 units of 61 g = 59.4 minas of 488 g | Consistent with the literature ladder 1 : 8 : 60 (unit : mina : talent) but this is one anchor and the ladder's middle step is not seen in 1d. The ladder taken forward to cycle 2 is therefore the literature one (P1 = 1:8:60) plus the data hint (P2 = 1:4:120), both fixed before scoring Linear A |
| la27:LA-27.2a | All LA integers (1,299; numbers > 2000 dropped) | P1 gain +0.0005 nats/number, P 0.37; P2 +0.0017, P 0.063. Best random ladder r1 = 6 (+0.0039, the 95 % point of the null) | P1 not supported. P2 borderline, killed by 2d-2e below |
| la27:LA-27.2b | Per label (logogram on the line, else 'W' = after a word): W 917, OLE 89, GRA 92, CYP 29, VIN 36, VIR 33; all logogram numbers 382; 'fraction-bearing' labels (>= 25 % of numbers with fractions: CYP, MI, QA2, OLE, OLIV, VIN ...) 199 | P1: P 0.56-0.996 everywhere (gain negative in all 8). P2: GRA P 0.024 (1 of 16 tests; Holm > 0.3), all others P >= 0.52. Best random ladders differ by set (W 6, OLE 17, GRA 20, VIN 15, VIR 18, fraction-bearing 3) | No commodity heaps on the 1:8:60 ladder. Grain (measured by volume, not weight) is the only P < 0.05 and only for the 1:4 step; not significant after correction. Which signs carry weighed goods cannot be read from heaping |
| la27:LA-27.2c | Planted control: LA-sized samples (1,299) from the fitted base with 15 % or 5 % of numbers moved to the nearest multiple of r1; ladders (8,60) and (7,30), 2 draws each | (8,60): planted P 0.001-0.03 and r1 = 8 is the top ladder 4/4, even at 5 %. (7,30): P 0.043-0.049 (ties with other r1 = 7 ladders) and r1 = 7 on top 4/4 | PASS: a weight-step habit touching 5 % of amounts would be seen at LA size |
| la27:LA-27.2d | Jitter control: every integer moved by +-1 or +-2 (keeps magnitude, destroys divisibility), 2 draws | P1 P 0.80 / 0.66; P2 P 0.053 / 0.20; best random ladders 22 and 3 with gains +0.0016 / +0.0048 | The real data's best (r1 = 6, +0.0039) is inside what jittered numbers give, and P2's P 0.06 recurs on jittered data (0.053): the small P2 edge is a property of the magnitude mix, not of divisibility |
| la27:LA-27.2e | Split-half re-test: best of the 10^4 ladders chosen on half the documents, scored on the other half | Half A picks (2,80) (+0.0099); on half B it gives -0.0005, P 0.75; half B's own best is (20,50) | No ladder replicates across halves. Verdict cycle 2: Linear A integers do not heap on the physical ladder or on any ladder beyond decimal counting (calibrated negative, grade B) |
| la27:LA-27.3a | Linear B control (DAMOS). Search sees only the counts of a sub-unit written after a larger unit (L>M 8, LANA>M 34, M>N 72, N>P 3, M>P 5). 10^4 random ratio vectors (L:M, LANA:M, M:N, N:P; each log-uniform 2-60); held-out log-lik (uniform 1..r-1, error floor) over 20 random document halves; modal value in the top 50 | M:N = 4 (48 of top 50; N is never written more than 3 times, n 72). LANA:M = 3 (38/50; M <= 2 under LANA). L:M: only a lower bound (M up to 26 under L, n 8; modal values scatter 6, 54, 27), truth 30. N:P unknown (n 3) | PASS where n >= ~30: the ceiling search recovers the known M:N = 4 exactly and LANA:M = 3 from numbers alone; with n = 8 it gives only r >= 27 for L:M |
| la27:LA-27.3b | Planted LB-sized ceilings (same documents and pair counts, counts uniform below random ratios) and counts shuffled across unit pairs | Planted: pairs with n 34-72 recovered (17 -> 20/22, 29 -> 29-32, 14 -> 14-16, 32 -> 32); pairs with n 3-8 not. Shuffled: M:N 5, LANA:M 4-5 (truth lost) | Control behaves: real structure is found, shuffled counts lose it |
| la27:LA-27.3c | Do the la1 candidate fraction values fall on physical sub-unit weights? Share of a set's distinct values v with a core mass within 4 % of B x v; B = 61, 65.5, 488 g; sets: conventional, attack-1 binary, pure binary, thirds; null 10^4 random value sets; planted power (one mass per planted value added) | 61 g: conventional 3/8 hits (1/5, 1/3, 3/4), P 0.90; binary 0/4. 65.5 g: conventional 6/8 (P 0.14), pure binary 3/4 (P 0.32). 488 g: all P >= 0.73. Planted power only 0.28 (61 g), 0.41 (65.5 g), 0.00 (488 g) | NO POWER: the published masses are too dense and too few for the sub-unit test (the null already hits ~0.5). Nothing is concluded from it about the fraction values |
| la27:LA-27.3d | What the LA side offers the ceiling search | LA integers are never written as counts of a sub-unit under a named larger unit (no metrograms; amounts are one integer + fraction signs). The only repeated sub-unit signs are the fraction letters themselves | Leads to cycle 4: run the LB ceiling estimator on the fraction letters |
| la27:LA-27.4a | Repeats per fraction letter in the 414 fraction-bearing amounts; document bootstrap (2,000) | D: repeated in 15 of 37 amounts (DD x14 at HT, PH, ZA; DDDD once, HT 115a) -> r >= 3 secure (boot 1.00), r = 5 if HT 115a is sound (boot 0.63). The corpus also has a separate 'DD' fraction letter (6x); counting only D written twice as two signs, 9 of 31 D amounts repeat. B: BB x5 (ZA 3, KH, KE) -> r >= 3 (boot 0.99). E: EE x3, all Phaistos. J: JJ x2, both Phaistos (photo check: doubtful). A, L: twice once or twice each. Never repeated: K 0/34, L2 0/30, JE 0/31, F 0/19, H 0/16 | Two behaviours. D (and B) are COUNTED parts, written 2-4 times like LB N under M; K, L2, JE, F, H are never repeated, like single denominations or binary digits |
| la27:LA-27.4b | Is 'never repeated' real? Fisher exact, repeat rate vs D (15/37) and vs B (5/36) | vs D: K P 1e-5, L2 4e-5, JE 2e-5, F 0.001, H 0.002, J 6e-10, E 2e-6 (without the 'DD' letter, 9/31: K 0.0006, L2 0.002, JE 0.002, F 0.009, H 0.019). vs B: K 0.054, L2 0.058, JE 0.057, F 0.15, H 0.31 | D repeats far more than every other letter (A). B's lower rate cannot be separated from the never-repeated letters at this n |
| la27:LA-27.4c | Planted control: as many amounts as the real D, B+A, J+E+F+K, drawn uniformly in fifths (D), sixths (B 1/3, A 1/6) and sixteenths (J E F K), written greedily | Estimator returns D 5, B 3, A 2, J/E/F/K 2 in 200/200 | The estimator recovers fifths, thirds and binary steps at LA size when amounts cover the range |
