Cycle 1c: the denomination grid as a whole is not beyond jitter (P 0.55) | Fifths and thirds of the unit exist as physical pieces; halves are not seen in these masses. Suggestive only (n small, several standards mixed) |
| la27:LA-27.4 | Cycle verdict | The LB-calibrated ceiling search gives a value-free constraint: D is a counted part with >= 3 (probably 5) per next unit, B >= 3; K, L2, JE, F, H behave as single denominations | D = 1/5 of its parent and B = 1/3 (conventional) are consistent; binary values for D or B (attack-1 B = D = 1/8) are disfavoured, since a binary sub-unit is never written twice. Grade B (D counted, >= 3 per parent), grade C (D = 1/5 exactly; would support: a sound DDD or DDDD elsewhere; would kill: 5 Ds in one amount, or HT 115a's DDDD re-read) |
| la27:LA-27.final | (1) Quantal analysis of 70 published Neopalatial weight masses (Akrotiri, Knossos, Zakros, Miletus) against jitter and log-uniform nulls, with planted lattices. (2) Held-out heaping of LA integers on the physical ladder 1:8:60 (and the data hint 1:4:120) vs 10^4 random ladders, per commodity, planted (5-15 %), jittered and split-half controls. (3) LB control: ceiling search over 10^4 random ratio vectors from sub-unit counts; fraction values vs physical sub-units with planted power. (4) The LB ceiling estimator applied to repeated LA fraction signs, with planted fifths/thirds/binary and Fisher tests | (1) No quantum in the masses (61 g: P 0.45; best 17.1 g, P 0.59), although planted lattices with <= 6 % error are found 0.69-0.85; no 8-unit mina step. Ladder used = literature 1:8:60 (ingot 475 units = 59 minas). (2) LA integers: P1 P 0.37 (all), 0.56-0.996 per commodity; nothing replicates (split-half P 0.75); planted 5 % heaping found 8/8. (3) LB: M:N = 4 and LANA:M = 3 recovered exactly from numbers alone; L:M only >= 27 (n 8). Fraction-vs-weight test has no power (0-0.41). (4) D is written up to 2-4 times (15/37 amounts; 9/31 without the 'DD' letter), B up to 2 (5/36), E twice only at Phaistos; K, L2, JE, F, H never (0/16-0/34; vs D P 6e-4-0.02) | NOT CRACKED. The balance pans do not show in the written integers: no LA commodity heaps on the 1:8:60 ladder or any ladder beyond decimal counting (B, calibrated negative), and the published masses alone do not fix a quantum (B). Weighed-goods logograms cannot be identified by heaping. Positive, value-free: by the LB-calibrated ceiling rule, D is a counted part (>= 3, probably 5, per next unit) and B >= 3, while K, L2, JE, F, H act as single denominations (B). D = 1/5 and B = 1/3 fit; a binary D or B (attack-1 1/8) does not (C, would kill: a re-read of HT 115a DDDD plus a fifth D anywhere). Physical pieces near 1/5 (12 g x5) and 1/3 (20 g x2) of the unit exist; halves are not seen (C) |

| la28:LA-28.final | Inscribed roundels (141), nodules (876) and sealings (7) treated as receipts and matched to the tablets of the same site and deposit by term (word / single sign / logogram, exact and ligature base), quantity form, receipt-class counts (singles and pair sums vs KU-RO totals and amounts), summary tablets, roles and signs. References: other documents, tablet half vs half. Controls: Linear B sealings/nodules (KN Ws/Wn/Wm, PY Wr, TH Wu, MY Wt, MI Wv) vs their tablets; planted copying at LA size. | Match rate: 41/131 receipt types on own-site tablets vs site swap 35.9 (z 1.7, P 0.064), the same as vessels (z 2.4) and far below tablet-to-tablet sharing (z 6.3); LB control z 4.2 (P 0.0005); planted 10 % copying found 0.97. Khania roundel classes (*411-VS, *164, O, *301+*311, *86+*188, VIR+KA) are absent from all 104 KH tablets (4/32 vs 7.6). HT Room 13 tablets share less with the Room 13 nodules than random HT tablets (1 vs 3.0). Quantities: 3/14 term+form matches (site swap P 0.36); 9/14 receipt quantities are fraction-only vs 17 % on tablets (P 0.0001). Counts vs totals: HT 4 vs jitter 3.2 (P 0.39). No summary tablet (max 4 vs 4.0). Linking terms on ledgers lack a number more often (0.66 vs 0.77, P 0.001; without TE P 0.007). 19 % of receipt types use signs no tablet uses (other documents 6.5 %, tablet hold-out 5.6 %; P 0.005). | NOT SUPPORTED (B, calibrated): the surviving LA receipts did not feed the surviving ledgers in any visible way; fewer than ~10 % of receipt types could have been copied without detection. Grade A (counts): receipts use their own sign set (19 % of types) and fraction-only amounts (9/14). Grade B: receipts and ledgers are separate administrative layers; the KH roundels record vessel-class (*4xx-VS) and other goods that no surviving ledger lists. Grade C: receipt signs KA, KU, SI, I appear in HT ledgers as markers or headings, not counted entries (would kill: the effect vanishing under a stricter heading/entry parse or with new HT tablets). Arithmetic reconciliation is not testable (4 integer receipt amounts). Not cracked; no reading. |
| la22:LA-22.final | Arrow in the dark: restore Linear A's lost signs, numbers and commodity signs with random ensembles (4,000 random predictors per fold for signs, 1,000 for numbers and logograms) over 15 sign experts (frequency, position, Markov, lexicon with edit model, tablet words/signs, site, scribe, support, accounting slot, 2 neural MLPs x 2 seeds), 7 number experts (frequency, round, tablet scale, repetition, KU-RO arithmetic, word->number, commodity->magnitude) and 6 logogram experts. Calibrated by masking known items at the real damage pattern (break at a word's left or right end, from the edition's own break mark; 2,100 breaks on 512 documents). Controls: frequency and Markov baselines, Linear B LA-sized positive control, shuffled-tablet null. Then froze predictions for all 974 real break edges, all 10,464 sign positions and 19 broken total sections (sha256 37303998...a1f1), and scored them against SigLA's independent re-reading. | Signs: top-1 0.28 / top-5 0.52, ECE 0.07-0.09 (frequency 0.06, Markov 0.22); LB 0.45; shuffled tablets give the same 0.28, so word-internal language carries it and the tablet adds nothing. Numbers: 0.252 / 0.609 vs 0.238 / 0.562, gain only from accounting experts, gone in shuffled tablets (0.238); inside total-closed sections TOT 0.31 vs 0.20. Commodity signs: 0.194 vs 0.089 (shuffled 0.126), carried by the tablet's other commodities. Outside test: SigLA and lineara.xyz agree on 98.8 % of 2,789 aligned signs; of 19 clean re-readings the frozen top-5 holds the new sign 1/19 (the old one 5/19, P 0.024); edges 2/13 top-1 (P 0.03) but 1/4 in the clean subset (P 0.42), and the shuffled model scores the same. Low P(reading) flags changed readings with AUC 0.65, but sign frequency alone gives 0.60 (ratio P 0.12). | Restorer PASSES in-corpus controls (B). Out of corpus NOT SUPPORTED (B for substitutions, untestable for edges). Constraint split: accounting constraints restore quantities and commodities (B); language restores signs (B). Not cracked. |
| la22:LA-22.1a | LA, out-of-fold calibration, 10,464 masks. Mean confidence 0.19-0.20 vs accuracy 0.26-0.28: slightly under-confident. | The restorer beats both baselines; at broken edges the gain over Markov is small (+0.01). Calibrated to within 0.07-0.09. |

ECE 0.10-0.16 (random ensemble), 0.04-0.07 (learned weights). | PASS: the same machine restores Linear B much better (+0.17 top-1 for int masks), so LA's lower ceiling is a property of the corpus, not of the method. |

| la22:LA-22.1d | Which constraints carry it: learned-weight ensembles without each group / with that group only (+FREQ). | LA int: all 0.276; without language 0.264, without tablet 0.273, without meta 0.277, without accounting slot 0.277; language alone 0.271, tablet alone 0.270 (its neural expert also sees word context). LB int: without language 0.412, without tablet 0.434 (all 0.447); R: without tablet 0.342 (all 0.386). Single experts: LA DOCW 0.084, LB DOCW 0.215; LA LEX 0.225, neural 0.263. | Language (within-word n-grams, lexicon, neural context) carries LA restoration. Tablet repetition helps Linear B (formulaic words recur on a tablet) but not LA. Site, scribe and the accounting slot add nothing. |

Shuffled tablets erase the first and leave the second. | Grade B: the la14 accounting skeleton is a usable restoration constraint for quantities and commodities, not for words. |

Shuffled-tablet model: the same 2 hits (P 0.03). Post hoc, in inventory and same SigLA word (n 4): 1 hit (HT 3), perm mean 0.51, P 0.42. | Weak. The nominal P 0.03 rests on 2 events, one of which is an alignment-grouping ambiguity; the clean subset is 1/4 at chance. The hit HT 3 MA-DI comes from the lexicon (MA-DI is a known word, also on HT 3 itself), not from the tablet: the shuffled model gets it too. |

Controls: shuffled-tablet model, frequency of the sign alone, model divided by frequency. | Model AUC 0.651 (perm P 0.012); shuffled-tablet model 0.639 (P 0.013); sign frequency alone 0.599 (P 0.065); model / frequency 0.576 (P 0.12). | Changed readings are rarer, less expected signs, but context adds little beyond sign frequency (P 0.12). Grade C at most. |

These are ordinary arithmetic restorations, not new. |
| la22:LA-22.3 | Cycle verdict | The restorer's frozen guesses do not predict the independent later reading beyond chance in any clean test (substitutions 1/33 top-5; clean edges 1/4). The editions differ in only ~1 % of signs, so the outside test has little power. The changed positions are hard ones (the model puts lineara's own hidden reading X in its top 5 only 5/19 times, against 52 % overall), but even there SigLA's Y reaches the top 5 only 1/19 (binomial P 0.024 against X's rate): the restorer is anchored to the corpus's own spellings and does not anticipate re-readings. For edges the test is uninformative (n 4 clean). | NOT SUPPORTED out of corpus (B for substitutions: the corrections are not the kind of error context can fix; C/untestable for edges). |
| la26:LA-26.final | The seal-sharing network as data, with no text: 7 same-seal groups (6 gold rings or seals plus 1 stone seal) impressed at 2-4 sites each, plus 1 look-alike group (CMS numbers as compiled by Montecchi 2018; Arachne holds the CMS records but no impression findspots or identity links). Tests: (1) site level, 4 measures (shared words, sign profiles, logogram ligature variants, entry structure), standardized by doc-perms within support type, distance-residualized, with QAP, seal-graph rewiring and distance-only nulls; 3 node sets; all and admin documents. (2) Word level, the reverse: do multi-site words sit on ring paths? Doc-perm, rewiring, distance-gravity, competing no-seal graphs, site jackknife, all-graphs and QAP references. (3) Blind: all 32,766 site graphs scored on the text, half-split, held-out re-test. (4) Partial tests with Khania removed or modelled. Controls: planted ring vocabulary at LA size (m = 2-16 per edge), planted on random pairs, document-shuffled corpus, and Linear B calibration of the null (no LB cross-site seal data exist). | Network: the text-bearing part is a star on Hagia Triada (HT-ZA 3 seals, HT-KN 2, HT-GO 2, HT-Thera 1, KN-ZA 1, ZA-GO 1) plus Sklavokambos, which has no text; Khania has only a look-alike. (1) No measure passes (best QAP P 0.058, signs); the broad graph is negative everywhere. Positive control: detection 0/30 at m=2 and m=4, 2/30 at m=8, 22/30 at m=16 (about 4x the real multi-site vocabulary); random-pair plants 0/30. (2) The doc-perm P (0.0005) is an artefact: on Linear B, random graphs 'pass' 37.5% of the time. Against all graphs of the same size: P 0.16-0.66. HT shares 9 admin words with Zakros (3 rings) and 9 with ring-less Khania; 1 with Knossos (2 rings). The signal is 'Zakros shares, Khania does not': drop ZA and it vanishes, and a graph of 'everyone but Khania' does as well. (3) Replicable site structure exists (held-out +7.3 vs shuffled +0.04), but it is not the ring graph (Jaccard 0.29 vs 0.26 shuffled; planted 8/edge still 0/12). (4) With Khania removed or modelled: words P 0.43-0.95, ligatures 0.76-0.85, structure 0.12-0.22, signs 0.058-0.17. | **NOT SUPPORTED; NO POWER FOR THE STRONG FORM.** The ring network does not predict vocabulary, sign variants or entry structures beyond distance and size, and multi-site words do not cluster on ring paths. But only a ring process ~4x larger than all real cross-site sharing would have been seen, so absence is an upper bound. The network is confounded with Khania's isolation (sign z -11, structure z -15 to -17 vs HT/ZA), and the unlinked control sites (Phaistos, Malia) are partly earlier. Grades: network record A; Khania is a lexical, sign-profile and entry-structure island beyond size and support B (extends la15); ring pairs slightly more alike in sign profile C (P 0.06; would support: the same with new ZA or KN texts; would kill: P > 0.2 without GO/Thera); HT-ZA shared words (SA-MA, PA-JA-RE, KU-RO, A-RI-NI-TA, TI-TI-KU, A-SE, NU-TI, KI-RA, U-*49, RU-DI, SI-TU) as ring-office terms C, demoted (HT-KH sharing is equal); same seal -> same incised sign (S68: RO on 3 of 4 nodules) C (P 0.088 for any sign). Method lesson (B): doc-perm nulls for site-pair statistics must be calibrated against random graphs, since they reject for ~40% of random graphs on Linear B. Not cracked. |
| la26:LA-26.1a | Seal-sharing network built as data, with no text: which seal face was impressed at which site (CMS numbers as compiled by Montecchi 2018; the CMS records exist on Arachne, but Arachne stores no impression findspots or identity links). 7 same-seal groups: R15 (HT 5, KN 2), R19 (HT 2, Sklavokambos 4, Akrotiri 3), R44 (HT, SK, GO), R43 (HT 3, SK 2, GO, ZA), R71 (HT 1 disputed, ZA 2, KN 3), Levi 117 = Marinatos 8 (HT, SK), stone seal S68 (HT 3, ZA 1 disputed). Plus look-alike LA41 (HT, SK, ZA, KH, Thera) and Zakro-Master style (HT, ZA). Versions: core, strict (disputed attributions removed), broad (+ look-alike + style). | Core site graph (shared seals): HT-ZA 3, HT-KN 2, HT-GO 2, HT-TH 1, KN-ZA 1, ZA-GO 1, plus Sklavokambos (no Linear A text) tied to HT, ZA, GO, TH. Khania has NO same-seal link, only the look-alike. Among text sites the graph is a star on HT plus ZA; strict has the same binary graph as core. Of the 6 sites, GO has 3 words and Thera 11. | Data, grade A as a record of published seal identities. Structural limit: ring-linked versus unlinked is almost the same as "not Khania" versus "Khania". |
| la26:LA-26.1b | Site level. For 4 measures (shared word types of 2+ signs; syllabic sign-profile 1-JSD; shared logogram ligature variants such as OLE+KI; entry structure as token-class bigram 1-JSD): Z_ij = observed minus the mean under 1,000 document-label permutations within support type (controls size and support), divided by the SD. T = mean distance-residual Z on ring pairs minus on unlinked pairs. Nulls: QAP node relabelling (exact 720 for 6 nodes, 5,000 for 9), curveball rewiring of the seal x site graph (2,000), and the distance-only model built into T. Node sets: A (6 sites in the seal system), B (A + Phaistos, Malia, Tylissos with no shared seal recorded), C (4 sites with >= 50 docs); all documents and administrative documents only. | Nothing passes. Core graph, set A: words T +0.39 (QAP P 0.43, rewire 0.82); signs +2.34 (0.14, 0.45); ligature variants -0.27 (0.63); structure +2.89 (0.17, 0.51). Set B: best signs T +2.11 (QAP 0.058, rewire 0.27); words -0.08 (0.64). Set C: QAP floor 0.25 (24 relabellings), all P >= 0.25. Broad graph (with look-alike): T negative for all 4 measures in every set (QAP 0.66-0.96). Admin only: same pattern. Almost every pair shares FEWER words than permutation predicts (endemism, as in la15). Khania is extreme: sign profile z -11.4 vs HT and -8.7 vs ZA; entry structure z -15.2 vs HT and -17.2 vs ZA. | No effect at site level. The only positive drift (signs, structure) comes from Khania being unlike everyone, and Khania is also the one site with no ring. |
| la26:LA-26.1c | Positive control at Linear A size: m new word types planted on each of the 6 core ring edges (each inserted into a random document at both sites, replacing a word), m = 2, 4, 8, 16, 30 replicates, 100 permutations, QAP. False-positive control: the same number of planted types on random site pairs. | Detection (QAP P <= 0.05): m=2 0/30 (median P 0.27); m=4 0/30 (0.19); m=8 2/30 (0.08); m=16 22/30 (0.04). Random-pair plants: 0/30 at m=2, 4, 8 and 16 (median P 0.46-0.51), so the false-positive control is clean. For scale, the whole corpus has only 22 word types at 2+ of these 6 sites. | **The site-level test has no power at Linear A size.** A ring process that planted 48 shared words (twice the real number of multi-site words) is found in about 7% of runs; detection needs about 96 planted shared types (m=16, 73%), four times the real multi-site vocabulary. The null for LA-26.1b is an upper bound only and cannot rule out ring-mediated vocabulary sharing. |
| la26:LA-26.2a | Word level. For every word type (2+ signs) found at >= 2 node sites, count its site pairs; F = share of those pairs that are ring-linked. Nulls: (a) document-label permutation within support type, 2,000; (b) curveball rewiring of the seal x site graph, 5,000; (c) distance-only model: each word keeps its largest site and redraws the others with probability ~ size x exp(-d/lambda), lambda fitted to the observed mean pair distance, 2,000. | Set A (6 sites): 22 multi-site words, 24 pairs, F 0.54 (13 ring pairs); doc-perm mean 0.35, P 0.0005; distance model 0.36, P 0.049 (best lambda = no decay); rewiring P 1.0 (the rewired graphs give exactly the same F). Admin only: F 0.45 vs 0.33, P 0.019. Set B (+PH, MA, TY): 30 words, F 0.36 vs 0.22, P 0.0015; distance model P 0.14. Broad graph (with the look-alike): F 0.67-1.0, doc-perm P 0.004-0.24, distance P 0.50-0.90. | A real departure from the size and support null, but not from the distance model in set B, and the seal-rewiring null is degenerate (every seal contains HT, so rewiring never changes which text sites are linked). Tested further in 2b. |
| la26:LA-26.2b | Which words travel along rings (set A, core graph). Per-word ring share and rewiring P. | Ring-path words: 11 HT-ZA (SA-MA, PA-JA-RE, KU-RO, A-RI-NI-TA, TI-TI-KU, A-SE, NU-TI, KI-RA, U-*49, RU-DI, SI-TU) and 1 HT-KN (DI-NA-U). Off-ring: 8 HT-KH (DI-NA, DA-RE, DA-I, RE-ZA, A-DU, A-RA, QA-NU-MA, PI-SA), KU-PA (HT, KH, ZA), *21F-*118 (KH, ZA). Per-word rewiring P = 1.0 for every word (degenerate null, as in 2a). | Candidate 'ring-office' terms, grade C only, and demoted by 2f (HT shares as many admin words with ring-less Khania): the HT-ZA set, which includes the total word KU-RO. Would support: the same words on new Zakros or Knossos tablets or on sealed documents bearing the travelling rings. Would kill: they turn up at Khania or Phaistos as often as at Zakros, scaled by size. |
| la26:LA-26.2c | Competing graphs with no seal information, same F and doc-perm null (2,000): KHISO (every pair linked except those with Khania), HUB (every pair with HT), DIST (pairs below median distance). Site jackknife (1,000 perms each). Degree-preserving double-edge swaps of the site graph (5,000). | Set A z-scores: RING +3.9 (P 0.0005), KHISO +3.1 (P 0.003, same F 0.54 as RING), HUB +4.3 (P < 0.0005), DIST -2.3 (P 0.99). Set B: RING +3.3, KHISO +2.8, HUB +4.6, DIST -0.4. Jackknife: drop ZA gives F 0.10 vs 0.17 (P 0.90; the effect vanishes); drop HT gives F 0; drop KN, GO, TH, PH, MA or TY leaves P <= 0.002; drop KH gives P 0.014 (A) and 0.032 (B). Degree-preserving swaps: 1 distinct F in 5,000 (degenerate, P 1.0). | **The ring signal is two facts: Zakros shares words with HT, and Khania does not.** A graph that only says 'everyone but Khania' does as well, and 'everyone to HT' does better. Distance works against sharing (near pairs share less). Without Khania a weak residual remains (P 0.01-0.03), but there the unlinked sites (Phaistos, Malia) are partly MM II-III, so date is a confound (la17: time-separated sites share fewer words). Ring-specific sharing: not shown. |
| la26:LA-26.2d | Same seal, same inscribed sign? The stone seal S68 impressed 3 HT nodules and 1 ZA nodule; RO is incised on HT Wa 1117, HT Wa 1118 and ZA Wa 36. Base rate from 846 single-sign nodule and sealing inscriptions. | RO base rate 0.108 (top signs: *301 232, KU 152, KA 150, SI 93, RO 91). P(ZA = RO and >= 2 of 3 HT = RO) = 0.0035. But P that ANY sign repeats this much on one seal's 4 nodules = 0.088. | Grade C: a seal-to-sign link is plausible but not significant once the sign is not chosen after the fact (0.088), and it rests on one seal. Would support: other multi-nodule seals carrying one sign. Would kill: same-seal nodules at HT carrying mixed signs at the base rate. |
| la26:LA-26.2e | Calibration of the word-level doc-perm null on Linear B (tools/la26_c2b_lb.py; DAMOS has no cross-site seal data, so there is no true LB ring network to use as a positive control). The 6 LB sites with >= 20 docs (KN, PY, TH, MY, TI, KH; 5,902 docs) as nodes, 1,000 random 6-edge 'ring' graphs, each tested with the same F statistic against 500 shared doc-perms. P should be uniform. | P <= 0.05 for 37.5% of random graphs and P >= 0.95 for 44% (quantiles 10/25/50/75/90%: 0, 0, 0.70, 1, 1). | **The doc-perm null is badly miscalibrated.** Real corpora have strong site-pair affinities, so ANY graph that happens to include the affine pairs 'passes'. The P values of 2a and 2c (0.0005-0.03) are not evidence for rings. |
| la26:LA-26.2f | Fair reference (tools/la26_c2c.py): rank of the ring graph's F among ALL graphs with as many edges (set A: all 5,005; set B: 200,000 sampled of 1.9 M), and among node relabellings of the ring graph (QAP). | Set A: P 0.31 (all graphs), 0.34 (QAP); admin 0.66 / 0.62. Set B: 0.16 / 0.19; admin 0.32 / 0.29. Shared multi-site types per pair (A): HT-ZA 12, HT-KH 9, ZA-KH 2, HT-KN 1; admin only: HT-ZA 9 and HT-KH 9. Set B adds HT-PH 6. | **Killed.** Multi-site words do not cluster on ring paths. Hagia Triada shares as many administrative words with ring-less Khania (9) as with ring-linked Zakros (9), and only 1 with Knossos (2 shared rings). |
| la26:LA-26.3a | All 32,766 non-trivial site graphs on HT KN ZA KH GO TH scored on Linear A only (no seal data) on a random half of the documents. Score = mean distance-residual excess similarity on the graph's edges minus off-edges, summed over 4 measures (words, signs, ligature variants, entry structure), each standardized by 40 doc-perms within support. The top 1% vote an edge consensus with as many edges as the ring graph (6). It is re-tested on the held-out half and compared with the real seal graph (Jaccard), against 2,000 random graphs of the same density. 12 splits per arm. Arms: real; planted 4 or 8 new shared words per ring edge; document-label-shuffled corpus. | Held-out replication of the consensus: real +7.27 (12/12 splits positive), shuffled +0.04 (5/12). Consensus vs seal graph: Jaccard real 0.29, plant4 0.35, plant8 0.40, shuffled 0.26; P <= 0.05 in 0/12 splits in every arm. Edge votes (real): KH-GO 0.93, GO-TH 0.95, HT-GO 0.87, HT-ZA 0.84, KN-GO 0.83 ... HT-KH 0.004, ZA-KH 0.14. Real seal graph on held-out halves: T +3.28 (12/12 positive) vs shuffled +0.36 (9/12); planted 3.87-3.97. Rank of the seal graph among all graphs on half A: median top 19% (shuffled 53%). | Linear A has a real, replicable site-similarity structure, but it is not the ring graph. Its strongest feature is the same as in cycle 2: Khania stands apart from HT and Zakros, and pairs involving the tiny sites (GO, Thera) score high because their z-scores sit near zero. The positive control fails: 8 planted words per edge do not bring the blind consensus to the seal graph (0/12). Blind recovery of a ring network from Linear A text is impossible at this size. |
| la26:LA-26.4a | Partial tests on the cycle-1 excess similarity (1,000 doc-perms within support). (a) Khania removed: T over the 10 pairs of HT KN ZA GO TH (6 ring, 4 unlinked) with log distance and log smaller-site size as covariates; exact QAP, 120 relabellings. (b) Khania kept with its own indicator covariate; exact QAP, 720 relabellings. 4 measures x all/admin documents = 16 tests per arm. | Without KH: words T -0.28 (P 0.95), signs +0.79 (0.17), ligatures -0.67 (0.83), structure +1.14 (0.20); admin the same (P 0.13-0.88). With a KH covariate: words +0.08 (0.43), signs +1.34 (0.058), ligatures -0.41 (0.76), structure +1.54 (0.15); admin signs P 0.067, structure 0.12. None survives 16 tests. | Once Khania's isolation is modelled, ring-linked sites are no more alike than unlinked sites in words, ligature variants or entry structure. Signs only: ring pairs are slightly more alike (P 0.06), grade C. Would support: the same with new Zakros or Knossos texts. Would kill: P > 0.2 when the tiny sites (GO, TH) are dropped. |

| la31:LA-31.1a | Travel-time matrices for 29 Linear A find-sites (23 Cretan, Thera, Kea, Milos, Miletos, Samothrace, Troy). Walking: Tobler's hiking function on a 0.01 deg DEM (AWS Terrain Tiles, 8-neighbour least-cost). Sailing: square-sail polar (no sail closer than 70 deg to the wind, best on a broad reach; oars at 0.5-2 kn as fallback; gale > 27 kn = waiting) over daily ERA5 10 m winds (Open-Meteo archive, 200 points at 0.5 deg, 2014-2018), 16-direction least-time routes on a 0.05 deg sea grid, one voyage set per day = 1,826 x 29 x 28 = 1.48 M simulated voyages; walking legs to ports within 12 h + 2 h embarkation each end. Seasonal medians (etesian Jul-Aug 310 days, spring 455, autumn 305, winter 756) and monthly medians; calm-water rowing (2.5 kn) as a no-wind control. Matrices: data/la31/travel.json. | Plausible values: KN-MA walk 6.8 h, KN-HT 9.6 h, KN-KH 23.7 h. Etesian season is strongly directional off Crete: Kea -> Knossos 38 h vs Knossos -> Kea 137 h; Samothrace -> KN 66 h vs 320 h; Thera -> KN 21 h vs 46 h; inside Crete median asymmetry only 4 % (max 46 %). But symmetrised, every travel model ranks site pairs like straight-line km: Spearman rho with km = 0.994 (etesian), 0.999 (winter, calm), 0.963 (walking, Crete). | Method works; the symmetrised wind map carries almost no information beyond distance for this site set (A, method). Only the direction of travel differs between models. |
| la31:LA-31.1b | Site-pair sharing: W shared word types, S shared rarer syllabic sign types (outside the 40 commonest), L shared logogram types, E entry-structure similarity (numbers per word, fractions per number, logograms per word, words per line, word->number rate, word length), ALL = sum of z. Each as z against 1,000 document-label permutations stratified by support (tablet / nodule / roundel / vessel / object), so size and genre are held. Mantel (Spearman, 5,000 site permutations = rewired network) against closeness under km, calm rowing, walking (Crete), wind (six seasons), size; partial Mantel given km and given calm. Site sets: all 29, Crete 23, 12 biggest. | Crete 23: sharing falls with distance: ALL r +0.24 (P 0.023 km; 0.024-0.033 for every wind season, 0.027 calm, 0.029 walking); S +0.18-0.19 (P 0.04-0.05); L +0.21 (P 0.046-0.054). All 29: no distance decay (ALL +0.11, P 0.18; W -0.12). 12 biggest: none (ALL +0.23, P 0.14-0.17). Partial on km: wind adds nothing in any set or layer (best all29 S etesian|km +0.10; Crete ALL -0.05 to -0.06, P 0.66-0.71; W -0.17, P 0.94). Size: z falls with pair size (r -0.4 to -0.7): big archives are each other's least-sharing partners (endemism, la15). | Inside Crete, nearer sites share more signs and commodity signs than chance (B, P ~0.02-0.05, uncorrected over 5 layers). Which travel model is right cannot be told: wind, rowing, walking and km tie. |
| la31:LA-31.1c | Planted control: synthetic words that spread from an origin (chosen by size) to site j with probability exp(-T[origin -> j] / L) under etesian sailing time (or km / 5 km/h), replacing every document's words (word counts kept), same z + Mantel pipeline, 12 reps; L calibrated so planted cross-site sharing matches the real 110 shared word-site pairs (L = 4 h), and 4 x L. | At real sharing level (L 4 h): Mantel detection 0/12 for every predictor. At 4 x L (16 h): distance decay detected 0.50-0.67, but wind|km 0/12 whether the truth was wind or km, and raw r(wind) > r(km) in 1.00 (wind truth) and 0.83 (km truth). | The Mantel route cannot tell sailing time from distance at LA size even when wind is planted as the truth; real 'wind ties km' is therefore uninformative (calibrated null). |
| la31:LA-31.1 | Cycle verdict | Distance decay inside Crete (B); no travel model beats km; planted wind spread is undetectable as such. | Symmetric tests: dead end at this size; go directional (cycle 2). |
| la31:LA-31.2a | Directed gravity model (pseudo-likelihood): for every type t and site j, logit P(t at j) = a + b log(layer size_j) + c log(1 + n other holders) + e [n > 0] + d log(mean over holders i of exp(-T[i -> j] / L)), L in 4-256 h. Gain G = log-likelihood gain of the d term (max over L); Delta = G(T) - G(T transposed): > 0 means types sit where the wind would carry them. Layers W (903 types), S (99), L (176). Nulls: rewired network (site labels of T permuted, 100), planted downwind / upwind / symmetric spread (12 reps). | All gains are tiny (<= 1 log-likelihood unit; geography adds almost nothing once the number of holders is known). All 29, W: Delta -0.86 (upwind side fits better; rewired P 0.91); S +0.17 (P 0.09); L +0.50 (P 0.13). Crete 23: W -0.01 (P 0.54), S +0.10 (P 0.45), L +0.37 (P 0.23). Best model per layer: W etesian^T 0.97, S etesian 0.29, L km 0.76; etesian gain vs rewired P 0.47-0.85. | No downwind flow and no season preferred. Words, if anything, sit on the upwind side; signs and logograms lean downwind, none significant. |
| la31:LA-31.2b | Power of the downwind test vs corpus size: planted spread (etesian, L 64 h) with each document's word count x1, x4, x16 (16 reps each); AUC of Delta for downwind vs symmetric and vs upwind spread. | x1 (LA size): Delta down +0.32 / sym -0.16 / up -0.24; AUC down>sym 0.68, down>up 0.72. x4: +0.81 / -0.10 / -0.51; AUC 0.79, 0.89. x16: +1.76 / +0.81 / +0.03; AUC 0.74, 0.89 (symmetric spread also drifts positive at large size: the model is misspecified, so the AUC saturates). Cycle-2 planted (x1, 12 reps): downwind Delta > 0 in 0.83 (L 64 h) and 0.50 (L 256 h) vs symmetric 0.58 / 0.33, upwind 0.50 / 0.33. | The direction test separates downwind from upwind (AUC 0.72-0.89) but not from symmetric spread (0.68-0.79 at any size): the real W Delta (-0.86) lies below every planted downwind mean, i.e. it argues mildly against summer downwind word flow, but cannot exclude it. |
| la31:LA-31.2 | Cycle verdict | Asymmetric (etesian) sailing time does not explain which sites share words, signs or logograms better than its own transpose or km; the test has AUC ~0.7 power at LA size. | Downwind flow: not supported (C at best for a weak upwind lean of words; would kill: Delta > 0 on a 4x corpus). |
| la31:LA-31.3a | Monthly wind fingerprint: partial Mantel (given km) of each month's combined walk + sail time (median over 5 years of that month's days) against site-pair sharing z (W, S, L, E, ALL); the best month is scored against the site-permutation null of the MAXIMUM over 12 months (500), so month-picking is paid for. All 29 and 12 biggest sites. | All 29: every layer peaks in Jun-Sep (W best Sep, S Sep, L Sep, E Aug, ALL Jul; ALL r -0.05 in Apr to +0.05 in Jul-Sep) but max-null P 0.31-0.77. 12 biggest: no summer peak (best Feb, Aug, Aug, Jan, Feb; P 0.35-0.84). | No month explains sharing beyond km. |
| la31:LA-31.3b | Summer-minus-winter contrast (mean partial r Jun-Sep minus Nov-Mar), site-permutation null (1,000), and planted words spread under Jul-Aug vs Dec-Feb times (L 16 h, 10 reps each). | Real: ALL +0.059 (P 0.19), W +0.057 (P 0.23), S +0.022 (P 0.37), E +0.067 (P 0.18). Planted summer spread +0.005 +- 0.017 (> 0 in 0.50); planted winter spread +0.002 +- 0.014 (0.60). | The contrast cannot tell a summer process from a winter one even when planted (dead method at this size). The small real summer lean is noise-level. Killed. |
| la31:LA-31.3c | The harder leg: does sharing follow the slower direction of a pair (max of the two sailing-season times: contact limited by the return trip) or the faster (min)? Partial Mantel given km, 2,000 site permutations. | All 29: slower ALL +0.03 (P 0.42), faster +0.05 (P 0.27); per layer P >= 0.17. 12 biggest: faster-leg W +0.20 (P 0.097), slower W -0.21 (P 0.87); ALL faster +0.11 (P 0.29). | Neither leg explains sharing. Grade C at most: among big archives, words follow the easy leg (would support: P < 0.05 with Thera / Kea sharing data; would kill: faster-leg r <= 0 when HT is dropped). |
| la31:LA-31.3d | Linear B comparison: 7 sites (KN, KH, PY, Thebes, MY, TI, Midea; DAMOS words and signs), travel matrices rebuilt with the same machinery (data/la31/travel_lb.json); same support-stratified z (500 perms), Mantel (2,000), and the directed gain model (rewired 200). | Words: distance decay km r +0.45 (P 0.029); calm sea route | km +0.33 (P 0.056); wind | km +0.16 (P 0.26). Signs: calm | km +0.36 (P 0.041); wind | km -0.03. Directed: W gain etesian forward 1.28 vs transposed 1.21 (Delta +0.08, rewired P 0.31); km 0.97, calm 1.08. | Linear B words also fall off with distance and lean toward sea-route (rowing) times rather than km (C; 21 pairs). Wind adds nothing and shows no direction in Linear B either, so the Linear A null is not specific to Linear A. |
| la31:LA-31.3 | Cycle verdict | No month, season, leg or direction of wind beats straight-line distance in Linear A; Linear B behaves the same; the seasonal and directional tests have little or no power at these sizes (planted). | Winds carried the words: not supported (calibrated, low-power negative). |
| la31:LA-31.final | Arrow in the dark, 'the winds carried the words'. Travel-time matrices for 29 Linear A find-sites: walking by Tobler's function over a 0.01 deg DEM; square-sail sailing with an oar fallback over daily ERA5 winds 2014-2018 (1.48 M simulated voyages, by month and season, asymmetric); combined walk + sail routes; calm-water rowing as the no-wind control. Tested whether site-pair sharing of words, rarer signs, logograms and entry structure (z against support-stratified document permutations) follows sailing time better than km, walking or size (Mantel / partial Mantel, site-permutation = rewired-network null), and whether types sit downwind (directed gravity pseudo-likelihood, forward vs transposed times), by month, season and leg. Controls: planted wind / km / summer / winter / downwind / upwind / symmetric spread on the real geography; corpus x1-x16 power curve; Linear B (7 sites) with the same machinery. | (1) Symmetrised, every travel model ranks pairs like km (rho 0.96-0.999); the wind only adds direction (etesian Kea -> Knossos 38 h vs 137 h back; inside Crete median asymmetry 4 %). (2) Inside Crete nearer sites share more (ALL r +0.24, P 0.023; S, L P 0.04-0.05), but km, rowing, walking and every wind season tie; across all 29 sites and among the 12 big ones no distance decay (P 0.14-0.18). Partial on km: wind adds nothing (P >= 0.14). Planted wind spread at the real sharing level is not detected (0/12) and at 4x is detected as distance only (wind|km 0/12). (3) Direction: words Delta -0.86 (upwind side, P 0.91), signs +0.17 (P 0.09), logograms +0.50 (P 0.13); the test's power against symmetric spread is AUC 0.68 at LA size (0.79 at x4, 0.74 at x16). (4) No month or season (max-null P >= 0.31; summer-winter contrast cannot separate planted summer from winter spread), no leg (slower / faster P >= 0.10). (5) Linear B: words fall with distance (r +0.45, P 0.03) and lean to sea-route rowing time (calm|km +0.33 to +0.36, P 0.04-0.06); wind and direction add nothing there either. | NOT SUPPORTED (calibrated, low-power negative). Grade B: inside Crete, sign and logogram sharing decays with distance; grade A (method): symmetrised sailing time is distance in rank for this site set, so only direction can separate wind from geography, and the direction test does not separate downwind from symmetric spread even on a 16x corpus (AUC <= 0.79). Grade C: words follow the easier leg among big archives (P 0.097); LB sharing follows sea routes (P 0.04-0.06). Downwind word flow and seasonal sailing: not supported; absence is an upper bound. Not cracked. |

| la29:LA-29.1a | Land data: 27 sites (20 Cretan LA, Thera/Kea/Melos, LB PY/TH/MY/TI). Spatial structure: e-folding length of 5-km catchment means over central Crete 12-14 km. | 53 variables; 51 vary over the 10 Cretan commodity sites; M_eff (eigenvalue participation) 4. Examples: HT olive_5 1.00, cl_olive 0.49; Syme upland_5 0.77, rain 796 mm; Psykhro upland 1.00, rain 921 mm; Zakros cl_pasture 0.55; Khania cl_orchard 0.18. | Data step (A as data; values are modern proxies of ancient land, not ancient land use). |

Linear B weak control: KN, PY, TH, MY (4 archives, document-level GRA VIN OLE OLIV FIC LIV LANA OTHER). | Planted beta 0.5: detected 0-3/20; beta 1.0: 9-19/20 (right variable 12-19/20); beta 2.0: 20/20. LB: best cl_orchard_5 +0.003; 2/51 variables beat the field null (chance). | Power: a land effect of about x2.7 per SD on two major commodities would have been seen; a real land-driven mix (modern land cover) is found half the time at LA size. Control: 100 shuffles of site labels. | 38/71 signs at P < 0.05, 43 BH survivors; but shuffled site labels give 23.8 (95 % 34) and BH survivors in 84 % of shuffles. | NULL INVALID: Gaussian fields lack the skewed, one-site-extreme marginals of real land variables (e.g. CLC arable 0.05 at HT, 0 at most sites), so they are far too lenient. | la29:LA-29.final | Per-site land from open data (AWS Terrain Tiles DEM z11, WorldClim 2.1, CLC 2018 lattice via EEA REST, SoilGrids 2.0) in 2/5/10 km catchments: 53 variables (elevation, slope, coast distance, land share, arable/upland/olive/vine proxies, rain, summer drought, temperature, CLC olive/vine/arable/pasture/shrub, soil). (1) LOSO multinomial prediction of each site's commodity mix (GRA VIN OLE OLIV CYP LIV VIR *301 *304 *401) from one variable; (2) per-sign site frequency vs land as a decoder; (3) 10^4 random land indices selected on half the documents and tested on the other half. Nulls: 10^4 Gaussian random fields (10-40 km) and a rank-mapped spatially constrained permutation; size alone; shuffled site labels. Controls: planted land-driven commodities and signs; CLC land cover as a 'modern ledger'; Linear B KN/PY/TH/MY. | Commodity: best vine_5 +0.031 nats/entry (P max-of-fields 0.18); size alone -0.019; shuffled labels higher (median +0.059). Planted x2.7/SD found 9-19/20, modern ledger 5/10 at LA counts and 10/10 at 10x; LB fails (4 archives). Decoder: Gaussian null invalid (shuffles 24 vs 3.6 expected); rank null: all documents 9/71 signs (shuffled 2.2, 95 % 5), the libation-formula signs JA, WA, I, NA, *301 rising at mountain sanctuaries; administrative documents only 3/66 (shuffled 2.9), without HT 0/42; VIN/OLE/GRA anchors P 0.75-0.98. Random guessing: land survivors on held-out halves +0.0011 vs fields +0.0017 (commodity), sign r 0.552 vs 0.549; transfer to LB: 2/51 variables beat fields (chance), median worse than fields. | NOT CRACKED; NOT SUPPORTED (B, calibrated negative). Out-of-site prediction skill ~0 (no better than smooth random fields, size, or shuffled labels). No sign linked to land in administrative texts; no commodity class assigned. Upper bound: a land effect of about x2.7 per SD on a major commodity, or on a sign at 2 % frequency, would usually have been seen. Grade A (known in kind): the libation-formula signs are geographically upland because the sanctuaries are (genre, not produce). Grade A (method): Gaussian random-field nulls are far too lenient for skewed site-level land variables; use rank-mapped fields. Grade C: vine-suitable land (vine_5) as the weak best predictor of the mix (would support: P < 0.05 with new non-HT commodity tablets; would kill: skill <= 0 on new commodity documents from sites outside HT and KH). What a site recorded follows archive type (Khania CYP/*301/*401, Malia HIDE, HT lists), not hinterland. |

LA: shape z 0.3-0.8 (n 163-2,745); blind-row sound z 1.8 (3+ signs) to 3.95 (all), surviving the distributional control (3.65), LB-value consonant only 1.6. Planted dictation slips are detected down to 10 % of LA's pairs, copying slips down to 20-30 %; real LA is below both. Modern LA re-readings follow shape (z 2.6), so the shape metric sees real LA hands. | Not cracked. Planted: held-out +1.74 (sd 3.2), above the N1 95th percentile in 9/12; planted rates recovered exactly 47/155 (30 %); held-out gain on MIXED sections +0.02. | NO hidden-currency vector balances held-out Linear A totals: the plain sum (all rates 1) beats every fitted vector out of sample. Power is low: at Linear A size even a planted currency gives no held-out gain on mixed sections (only 8 types recur across tablets), so this is a weak negative for 'mixed totals are converted' (grade B for 'no evidence', C for 'no currency'). |
| la30:LA-30.1c | Same as 1b with the 'round' criterion (|S - T| <= max(0.5, 1 % of T)): scribal rounding of converted values allowed. Same nulls and planted control (24 + 24 + 12 reps). | Real held-out -2.75 (mixed -2.60) after train +2.7. N1 -3.46 (P 0.16), N2 +0.03 (P 1.0). Planted +1.81 (above N1 95th pct in 10/12), rates recovered 35/155, mixed-section held-out gain -0.08. | Same verdict: no rate vector transfers to held-out totals; allowing rounding only adds overfitting. Fails before a currency can be tested on mixed sections (planted control has no mixed-section power). |
| la30:LA-30.1d | Occam version of 1b (exact): after each train fit every rate and fraction value not needed for the train count is reset to 1 / its site value, then tested on the held-out half. Same nulls and planted control. | Real held-out -0.30 (mixed -0.23). N1 -0.85 (P 0.12), N2 random totals -0.22 (P real >= N2: 0.60). Planted currency: +3.88 (mixed +1.28), above the N1 95th percentile in 11/12. | CALIBRATED NEGATIVE (grade B): with the planted control now passing (also on mixed sections), real Linear A totals gain no more from a fitted currency than random totals do. A currency affecting several recurrent commodity types in most sections would have been seen; one confined to rare types (17 of 25 occur on one tablet only) would not. |
| la30:LA-30.1e | Occam version with the 'round' criterion (rounded conversions allowed). | Real -1.43 (mixed -1.28); N1 -1.00 (P real >= ... 0.84: real is no better than shuffled labels), N2 0.00. Planted +3.23 (mixed +0.81), above the N1 95th pct in 9/12. | Same calibrated negative: allowing rounding makes real totals fit worse out of sample than random totals. |
| la30:LA-30.1-verdict | Cycle summary | Plain sums are the best predictor of held-out KU-RO totals (exact and rounded); fitted currencies lose 1.7-2.8 held-out totals per half. Without the Occam step the planted control has no power on mixed sections; with it (1d) the planted currency is found (11/12, mixed +1.3) and the real data still give nothing (-0.3 vs random totals -0.2). | KILLED for KU-RO totals: the calibrated Occam test (1d) passes its planted control and finds nothing (grade B). Next: enlarge the constraint set (cycle 3: hidden totals anywhere on a tablet) and calibrate on Ur III silver accounts (cycle 2). |
| la30:LA-30.2a | Do Ur III prices form one fixed rate vector? Median price per good; share of lines within 1 % of the median; sections balanced by the median vector. | Barley 1/300 gin2 per sila3 (36 % of lines at the median), sesame oil 0.078 (4 %), lard 0.061, dates 0.0022, bitumen 0.0083, alkali 0.00083, wool (siki) 0.1 gin2/ma-na. Median vector balances 13/58 totals within 5 %, 21/58 within 10 % (6/58 within 2 %). | Even in a real silver-priced economy rates are not fixed: they scatter about +-20-50 % between texts. A fixed-vector search must therefore use a loose tolerance (10 %) to see Ur III at all. |
| la30:LA-30.2b | Same search as cycle 1 (random log-uniform rates 1e-4..1, 2x10^4 per split + 12 annealed chains x 300 steps), tolerance 10 %, fit on half the texts, test on the other 29; 6 splits x 16 replicates. Nulls: N1 commodity labels shuffled, N2 totals x log-uniform[1/2, 2]. 'fixed' = totals recomputed from the median prices (an exactly fixed currency) and its label-shuffled null. | Real: 7.39 of 29 held-out totals balanced (sd 1.06) vs N1 1.57 and N2 2.48 (P < 0.0005 both); fitted rates within 25 % of the written median price for 31 % of goods (N1 8 %, N2 16 %). Fixed currency: 26.7 of 29 (vs 1.80 shuffled), rates within 25 % for 84 %. Full fit (10^5 random + annealing): 35/58 balanced; barley 0.0028 (truth 0.0033), dates 0.0023 (0.0022), sesame oil 0.065 (0.078), lard 0.052 (0.061), bitumen 0.0099 (0.0083); small-share goods (reed 'gi', gypsum) are off by x5-15. | CONTROL PASSES: the method finds a real ancient currency from totals alone, beyond both nulls, and returns the bulk goods' prices to within about 20 %. At 58 sections with 12 recurring goods the search has power; Linear A has 32 sections with 8 recurring types (cycle 1 planted control covers that size). |
| la30:LA-30.2c | Linear A size: the same control on a random 32 of the 58 Ur III sections per replicate (16 reps; 16 held-out sections). | Real Ur III: 3.77 of 16 held-out totals balanced vs N1 0.48 and N2 1.24 (P < 0.0005 both); rates within 25 % for 26 % of goods (N1 5 %). Fixed currency: 12.3 of 16 (shuffled 0.69), rates 69 %. | CONTROL PASSES AT LINEAR A SIZE, even with real Ur III price scatter (+-20-50 %). So a currency of Ur III strength in 32 totals is visible; what Linear A lacks is recurrence of types (8 of 25 types on >= 2 tablets vs 12 of 12 here), which cycle 1's planted control (Occam) covers. |
| la30:LA-30.2-verdict | Cycle summary | The hidden-currency search recovers a real ancient currency (Ur III silver) from totals alone at 58 and at 32 sections, and recovers bulk-good prices within ~20 %; nulls are flat. Bronze Age price check, from these Ur III data only: barley 1 gin2 per 300 sila3, sesame oil ~23x barley by volume, dates ~0.7x barley, wool 10 ma-na per gin2. | Method grade A as a control; it gives Linear A cycle 1 its meaning: the Linear A null is a real absence at the power available, not a blind method. |
| la30:LA-30.3a | Score = inscriptions with >= 1 exact hit: a number equal to the rate-weighted sum of a run of 2-7 numbers right before it that mixes >= 2 types incl. a commodity. Rates on p/q <= 12. Fit on half the inscriptions (2x10^4 random + 12 annealed chains x 250 steps + Occam reset), held-out hits minus plain-sum hits; 6 splits x 4 reps. Nulls: N1 labels shuffled across all numbers (20), N2 numbers shuffled within each inscription (20). Planted: random rate vector; in 12 % of inscriptions with a mixed run one number replaced by the weighted run sum (10). | Plain sum already hits 15 inscriptions (N1 labels: 14, so these are chance coincidences of small numbers). Real held-out gain -2.13; N1 -3.35 (P 0.19); N2 -1.83 (P 0.52). Planted: -2.48, rates recovered 4/65, above the N1 95th pct in 2/10. Full fit 21 inscriptions (GRA 1/2, VIN 3, CYP 2/3, *304 5/3, AROM 10, *86 10/3). | UNCALIBRATED: the planted control fails (chance coincidences among 1,685 runs of small numbers swamp a planted currency), so no verdict on the real data from this setting. The full-fit rates are noise (they come with no held-out gain). |
| la30:LA-30.3b | Strict variant to cut chance coincidences: targets >= 10 and runs of 2-4 numbers only (277 mixed runs; plain sum hits 1 inscription). Same fit, Occam reset, nulls and planted control (planted targets < 10 not scored). | Real held-out +0.42 (sd 0.43, 4 reps) vs N1 -1.42 and N2 -0.48 (P 0.048 each, the floor for 20 reps). Planted -0.27, rates recovered 10/65, above the N1 95th pct in 4/10. Full fit 10 inscriptions, among them both sides of HT 9 (a/b) and of ZA 6 (a/b), plus HT 18, HT 116a, HT 123+124b, HT 125b, TY 2, ZA 20; rates GRA 2/5, OLE 9/5, NI 12, AROM 10, ... | The planted control still fails, so the small real excess cannot be read as a currency. Splits were by inscription side, and parallel sides (HT 9a/b, ZA 6a/b) repeat their lists, so a rate fitted on one side can 'predict' its twin: the likely cause of the P 0.05 edge. Grade C at most, demoted: NOT SUPPORTED. Would support: the excess surviving a split by whole tablet; would kill: it vanishing (expected). |
| la30:LA-30.3-verdict | Cycle summary | Opening the search to every run on every tablet multiplies chance coincidences faster than signal: the planted currency is not recovered at either setting. | INCONCLUSIVE (uncalibrated) as a test; no evidence for a hidden currency outside KU-RO totals. |
| la30:LA-30.final | KU-RO / PO-TO-KU-RO totals (32 sections, 15 mixing types; 25 types, 8 recurring across tablets) modelled as T = sum r[type] x q with free exchange rates (p/q <= 12) and free fraction values; fit on half the tablets, held-out totals counted against the plain sum; exact and rounded criteria; Occam reset of unneeded rates. Nulls: commodity labels shuffled, totals replaced by random numbers of the same size, identical search. Controls: planted currency at LA size; Ur III merchant accounts (CDLI; 58 sections, silver per line hidden, true rate = median written price), at 58 and at 32 sections. Extension: hidden totals anywhere on 212 tablets (1,685 / 277 mixed runs). Differs from la6 (pairwise ratios inside entries). | Ur III control: 7.4/29 held-out totals balanced vs 1.6 (labels shuffled) and 2.5 (random totals); 3.8/16 vs 0.5 and 1.2 at LA size; bulk prices recovered within ~20 % (barley 0.0028 vs 0.0033 gin2/sila3, oil 0.065 vs 0.078, dates 0.0023 vs 0.0022). Linear A: fitted currencies never beat the plain sum out of sample (Occam, exact: -0.30 vs random totals -0.22, P 0.6; rounded: -1.43 vs 0.00); planted LA-size currency found 11/12 (+3.9, mixed +1.3). Hidden-total extension: planted control fails (2/10, 4/10); a P 0.05 edge in the strict variant traces to parallel tablet sides (HT 9a/b, ZA 6a/b). D and B occur in 0/32 KU-RO sections. | KILLED (grade B, calibrated negative): no exchange-rate vector balances held-out Linear A totals beyond the nulls; the failed totals on HT 27a, 94a, 100 (322 vs 335, 111 vs 110, 93 vs 97) are not explained by converting VIR+X, *86, *305 or TI+A into a common unit. No implied relative values to compare with Bronze Age prices. Fraction values: nothing (D and B never enter a KU-RO section; the free-fraction fits do not transfer). Grade A (method): a totals-only search does recover a real ancient currency (Ur III silver) at Linear A size. Not cracked; no reading. |
| la35:LA-35.1a | THE NUMBER BENDS THE WORD (tools/la35_c1.py). 1,000 LA entries (word, optional logogram, number >= 1; 301 documents). Stem families = words sharing a stem with >= 2 different endings. 39 stem definitions (first 1/2/3 signs x ending = last sign / last two signs / remainder; plus 30 random per-word cut points) x 3 number classings (1 / 2 / >= 3; 1 vs >= 2; round multiple of 10 vs other) = 117 cells. Statistics: G (ending depends on number within families) and CMH (the SAME ending tracks the same class across >= 2 stems = an agreement morpheme). Nulls: numbers shuffled within (tablet, commodity) N1, (site, commodity) N2, 300 each; family-wise P = max-z over the 117 cells. | With totals words: G max-z 1.6 (N1 P_fw 0.70), 3.5 (N2 P_fw 0.023); CMH 3.3 (N1 0.056), 3.8 (N2 0.023), best cell = first sign / last sign; the cell is carried by final -RO before >= 3 (KU-RO, KI-RO, PO-TO-KU-RO: 48 of 53 RO entries are >= 3). Totals words removed (957 entries): G P_fw 0.61 (N1) / 0.44 (N2); CMH 0.17 / 0.16. | No ending tracks number in LA once the total words are removed. The only "agreement" signal is KU-RO: totals are large, which is accounting, not grammar. |
| la35:LA-35.1b | Controls for 1a. Negative: LA numbers shuffled over the whole corpus. Planted: final X before 1, Y before >= 2, in 15% or 30% of entries with >= 2 signs (3 seeds each; N1 null). Positive: LB count entries (2,720; no measure unit after the number), 6 tablet subsamples at LA size (~1,000 entries) and full, with and without TO-SO / TO-SA totals. | Shuffled LA: G P_fw 0.58 / 0.53; CMH 0.046 (N1, one of 4 tests) / 0.15. Planted 15%: CMH P_fw 0.007 in 3 of 3 (max-z 4.4-6.7); 30%: 3 of 3 (5.3-8.1). LB full: G and CMH P_fw 0.007 under both nulls (CMH max-z 10.1). LB at LA size, totals removed: N2 P_fw < 0.05 in 5 of 6 subsamples (G or CMH), N1 in 2 of 6 (0.007-0.046). Strongest LB endings (first two signs / last sign, 1 vs >= 2, full LB): -U before 1 (32:4), -NO, -WO before 1; -RA, -DE, -WE before >= 2 (-eus / -ewes, tripod/dual -de, neuter plural -a). | The test sees a planted rule on 15% of LA entries and sees Linear B's real number marking at LA size against the site null (5/6) and, half as often, against the strict within-tablet null (2/6). LA shows neither. Upper bound: an LA ending rule covering >= 15% of entries does not exist; an LB-sized effect is excluded only against the site null. |
| la35:LA-35.2a | Massive held-out rule search (tools/la35_c2.py). Rule = endings S1 -> number '1', endings S2 -> '>=2' (1,500 random rules of 1-3 endings per side + every single-ending rule) per split; 20 random half-splits by tablet. Train score = sum of within-stem-family (CMH) z for '1'; top 50 rules kept; held-out scores: M1 Spearman(train z, test z) over endings, M2 mean test score of the top 50 rules, M3 family-centred AUC of '1' from the train ending effect. Stems = first 2 signs (final-sign endings), first sign, and first 2 signs with the whole remainder as ending. Nulls: numbers shuffled within (tablet, commodity) N1 and (site, commodity) N2, 30 shuffles x 5 splits. | LA pre2_E1: M1 0.11, M2 -0.14, M3 0.42 (N1 null 0.09 / 0.46 / 0.55; P 0.55 / 0.84 / 0.90). pre2_R: 0.40 AUC, P 0.77-0.97. pre1_E1: M3 0.55 vs 0.51 (N1 P 0.06, N2 P 0.06 = smallest possible with 15 shuffles), carried by final RO before >= 2 (KU-RO, KI-RO totals). | No held-out ending rule in LA beyond the totals. |
| la35:LA-35.2b | Controls: planted rule (30% of entries with >= 2 signs get final X before 1, Y before >= 2; 3 seeds) and LB at LA size (1,000 entries, 4 tablet subsamples, same pipeline). | Planted: top rules name the planted X and Y in all 3 seeds (RO>1 59, NA>=2 44; RE>1 45, RA>=2 45; NE>1 55, TU>=2 39 of 60 top-3 slots); M3 0.71-0.74 vs N2 0.46-0.53 (P 0.0625, the floor) but vs N1 0.61-0.68 (P 0.06-0.44). LB at LA size: M1 0.13-0.31, M3 0.52-0.60, all inside both nulls (P 0.31-0.88). Top LB rules are real agreement-like endings (-U >1, -WE / -DE / -RA >= 2) but do not replicate on held-out tablets. | The held-out search finds a planted rule by name but its numeric score has little power against the within-tablet null; Linear B's real number marking is NOT detectable this way at LA size. LA's failure is therefore uninformative (no power), not evidence of absence. |
| la35:LA-35.3a | Bare form vs extended form (tools/la35_c3.py). Every word W (>= 2 signs) that also occurs as W+X (X = 1 or 2 signs): 37 LA pairs, totals words excluded. Statistics: mean log number after W+X minus after W (signed), share of '1' (one), cross-stem consistency per suffix (cons); also W+X vs W+Y substitution pairs (17). Nulls N1 (tablet, commodity), N2 (site, commodity), N3 (commodity), 2,000 each. Controls: planted suffix X on 30% of entries with n >= 2 (3 seeds), LB at LA size (6 subsamples) and full LB. | LA ext: signed -0.24 (N2 P_lo 0.07), one +0.19: the EXTENDED form carries MORE '1' entries (N1 P 0.065, N2 0.0045, N3 0.0025). Substitutions: nothing (P > 0.15). LB full (234 pairs): the extended form carries FEWER '1' (one -0.08, P_lo 0.002-0.016) and larger numbers (signed +0.22, N2/N3 P 0.02), as a plural should. LB at LA size: 1-2 of 6 subsamples significant. Planted suffix: not recovered by these statistics (P 0.3-0.7). | Lead (C): in LA, the longer member of a W / W+X pair goes with 1 more often, the reverse of Linear B. Statistics too weak to see a planted suffix, so needs a kill test (3b). |
| la35:LA-35.3b | Kill test for 3a (tools/la35_c4.py). Confound: longer words (personal names on HT lists of '1') go with 1 whatever their stem. Fake-pair control: for each real pair, W is paired with a random word V of the same length as W+X that does not start with W (any site, or same site); 5,000 fake pair sets. Also leave-one-pair-out, sign count, HT vs non-HT. Same for LB full and 6 LA-sized LB subsamples. | LA: real +0.188 vs fake +0.153 (P_hi 0.31) / same-site fake +0.201 (P 0.57). Share of '1' by word length: 1 sign 0.23, 2 signs 0.22, 3 signs 0.35, 4 signs 0.30. Pairs + 14 / - 8 / = 15; HT +0.11 (22 pairs, P vs fake 0.75), non-HT +0.26 (7). LB full: real -0.082 vs fake +0.019 (P_lo 0.004; same-site 0.004); pairs + 50 / - 77. LB at LA size (51-67 pairs): P_lo 0.001, 0.002, 0.004, 0.07, 0.12, 0.29 (3 of 6 at P < 0.01; same-site control alike). | KILLED: LA's "longer form before 1" is word length (3-sign words are 1-entries more often), not a suffix. Linear B passes the same control in full and in 3 of 6 LA-sized samples (about 50% power; LA has only 37 pairs, LB 51-67 at the same entry count): its extended forms go with larger numbers (plural/dual). LA shows nothing of the kind (upper bound below). |
| la35:LA-35.3c | Size of what LA could hide: bootstrap over the 37 LA pairs of (real minus fake-pair mean) share of '1'. | LA excess +0.033, 90% interval [-0.11, +0.18]. LB full excess -0.10. | LA's interval does not exclude an LB-sized plural effect (-0.10); it excludes only a stronger one (< -0.11, one-sided 95%). Too few LA stem pairs (37) to decide. |
| la35:LA-35.final | THE NUMBER BENDS THE WORD (la35; tools/la35_common.py, la35_c1-c4.py). Does a word's ending change with the number after it (1 / 2 / >= 3; round vs not), as singular, dual, plural or counting forms do? (1) Within-stem-family G and cross-stem CMH tests over 117 stem-definition x number-class cells (39 stem definitions, 30 of them random cut points), family-wise max-z, against numbers shuffled within (tablet, commodity) and (site, commodity). (2) Massive held-out rule search: 1,500 random ending -> number-class rules per split x 20 tablet splits. (3) Bare W vs extended W+X pairs and their kill test with length-matched fake pairs. Controls: Linear B count entries (no LB sound values used as input; LB is only a calibration corpus) at LA size and full; planted agreement rules; corpus-wide shuffled numbers. | LA, totals words removed: no ending tracks number (G P_fw 0.44-0.61; CMH 0.16-0.17). With totals, the only signal is final -RO of KU-RO / KI-RO / PO-TO-KU-RO before large numbers (P_fw 0.023). The held-out search finds nothing (M3 AUC 0.42 vs null 0.55). LA's W+X forms carry more '1' entries than W (P 0.003-0.065), but length-matched fake pairs do the same (P 0.31): word length, killed. Controls: planted rule on 15% of entries recovered 3/3 (P_fw 0.007); LB at LA size passes (1) against the site null in 5/6 subsamples and the within-tablet null in 2/6; LB passes (3) in full (P 0.004) and in 3/6 LA-sized subsamples; the held-out search (2) fails LB at LA size (no power). | No number-agreement morpheme in Linear A (B, calibrated negative for rules covering >= 15% of entries; LB-sized effects excluded only against the site null). The one ending that tracks number, -RO of the total words, is accounting (A, known). Grade C, would support / would kill: an LB-sized plural suffix (share of '1' 0.10 lower on W+X) cannot be excluded with 37 LA stem pairs (90% interval -0.11 to +0.18); new W / W+X pairs on non-HT tablets with the longer form before >= 2 would support it, a pooled excess >= 0 over 80+ pairs would kill it. The DA-KU-SE-NE (6, 1) / DA-KU-SE-NE-TI (45) pair cited in the literature is one of 37 pairs and moves nothing (leave-one-out range +0.17 to +0.22). |
| la37:LA-37.1a | tools/la37_common.py, la37_c1.py, la37_report1.py. Every pair of signs with >= 8 tokens (10 in full LB) in 2+ sign words scored blind for contextual interchangeability: T = rank average of (i) cosine of PPMI context vectors (left, right, 2-back, 2-ahead neighbours, word edge '#'), (ii) the Voynich v35 split-half interchangeability index (contexts from two document halves, cross-half cosine over self-cosine), (iii) word-type pairs differing only by a <-> b in one slot, normalised by each sign's swap degree. Statistic chosen once on Linear B (rank average beat each component) and then frozen. Nulls: frequency-matched pairs (percentile within sign-frequency bins) and a within-word sign shuffle (R 100-150, per-pair z, BH q, max-z FWER). Positive control: Linear B KN+PY (DAMOS), blind; scored post hoc with LB values (16 doublet pairs: a/a2, a/a3, ra/ra2, ra/ra3, ro/ro2, pu/pu2, ta/ta2, o/wo, labialised and palatalised pairs; same consonant; same vowel). | LB full (75 signs): AUC doublet 0.78, same C 0.78, same V 0.63; frequency-matched doublet AUC 0.82; top 20 pairs 70 % related vs 23.5 % base (a~a3, ra~ra3 at fm 1.00; ra~ra2 0.99, ro~ro2 0.98, pu~pu2 0.98, ta~ta2 0.94; o~wo 0.60, de~dwe 0.04 missed). LB at LA size (10 document draws, ~3,900 sign tokens, ~4.6 doublet pairs in the alphabet): doublet AUC 0.69, same C 0.67, same V 0.60; top 20 57.5 % related vs 24.9 %. | Grade A (method): a blind distributional detector puts Linear B doublets and same-consonant pairs at the top (2.3-3x base rate in the top 20), also at Linear A size, though weaker. |
| la37:LA-37.1b | Kill controls: the same LB corpora with signs shuffled inside each word (keeps word bags, removes order). | LB full shuffled: doublet AUC 0.69, top 20 55 % related; LA-size shuffled 0.58, top 20 42 %. Order adds signal (0.78 vs 0.69; 57.5 % vs 42 % at LA size) but about half of the related-pair signal is carried by word bags alone (inflected forms of one word share their other signs). | Order-specific signal exists, but the shuffle null is not a clean null for doublets: it keeps much of the true signal. |
| la37:LA-37.1c | Planted doublets in the real Linear A corpus: a new sign X* replaces host X (KA, SI, TA, NA, DA, RE) at rate 0.4 under 4 regimes: token free variation, by word type (lexical), by site group, word-initial only (complementary). | Token: planted pair is the top-scoring pair of X* 6/6, overall percentile 1.00, frequency-matched 1.00. Site group: 5/6 top, percentile 0.999. Lexical (by word type): 0/6 top, percentile 0.73. Word-initial only: 0/6, percentile 0.60. But the within-word-shuffle z flags 0/24 (q and FWER never reach 0.1). | The ranking recovers free-variation and writer-split doublets (12/12 near the top); it is blind to doublets fixed per word and to doublets in complementary distribution (upper bounds: < 3/6 each). The shuffle-z test has no power even for perfect planted doublets (it shuffles the evidence into its null), so it is dropped as the flag; cycle 2b uses half-split replication instead. |
| la37:LA-37.1 | Cycle verdict | Detector calibrated: works on LB (A), recovers free and writer-split planted doublets in LA-sized data; blind to lexical and positional doublets. The requested shuffle null kills true positives and cannot be the significance test. | METHOD GRADE A (ranking), significance test replaced. Next: the LA search with replication across document halves, la21 rows as the independent check, LB values as the outside check. |
| la37:LA-37.2a | tools/la37_c2b.py. Search correction by replication: a pair is flagged if its frozen score T is in the top 2 % of all 2,016 pairs (64 signs) in BOTH halves of a random document split, in >= 50 % of 20 splits. Chance: pairs replicating when one half's labels are permuted (0.5-1.2 per split). Controls: LB full; 10 LB draws at LA size; LA with signs shuffled inside words (2 runs); planted LA doublets at rate 0.4 (6 hosts x token / site-group / word-type). | LB full: 18 flagged, 78 % related (14/18: same C 9, same V 3, doublets a~a3, ra~ra3) vs base 23.5 %. LB at LA size: 2-6 flagged in 10/10 draws (mean 4.3), precision 0.74 vs base 0.25, but 0 true doublets: they are mostly same-consonant (vowel-alternating) pairs. Planted: token 6/6 (replication 0.85-1.0), site group 5/6 (0.5-0.9), word-type 0/6. LA shuffled: 0 and 1 flagged (KU~KI). LINEAR A: 0 flagged. Highest LA replication: A~DA 0.4, A~PA 0.4, KU~KI 0.4 (also 0.65 in the shuffled corpus: the KU-RO / KI-RO word pair, a word-bag effect), TI~TE 0.2. | Grade B (calibrated negative): no Linear A sign pair among the 64 commonest is interchangeable in the way planted free-variation or writer-split doublets are (upper bound: < 3/6 such doublets at rate >= 0.4 could hide). Linear A also lacks the 2-6 replicating same-consonant pairs every LA-sized LB draw shows (0 vs 10/10). Real LB doublets are themselves not flagged at LA size, so rare optional doublets of the LB kind cannot be excluded. |
| la37:LA-37.2b | tools/la37_c2.py. The same T on the full LA corpus: frequency-matched percentile, within-word-shuffle z (R 100; dropped as flag after LA-37.1c), 30 document bootstraps (share in top 5 %), HT vs non-HT replication. | 0 pairs pass q <= 0.1 with fm >= 0.95 (as predicted by the planted test, the shuffle null has no power). HT vs non-HT T correlation rho 0.37. Most stable top pairs (bootstrap >= 0.8): A~PA 0.97, A~DA 0.93, RE~ME 0.93, A~KU 0.90, KU~KI 0.87, SI~TI 0.80; all also >= 0.88 percentile in both HT and non-HT. | Stable but not replicated at the flag level: exploratory candidates only (grade C, see cycle 3). |
| la37:LA-37.2c | Blind la21 consonant rows (same-row probability averaged over la21 LA runs) vs T. Circularity control: T recomputed on 20 within-word-shuffled LA corpora. LB control: LB la21 rows vs LB T. | LA: Spearman(T, same-row) 0.32; top 30 pairs same-row 0.120 vs base 0.056 (P 0.0002); stable pairs 0.163 (P < 0.0001). Shuffled T: 0.09 +- 0.01. LB control: 0.39; top 30 0.352 vs 0.077. | Rows and interchangeability agree in LA at about 80 % of LB's strength, through word-internal order (shuffle 0.09). | la37:LA-37.4a | tools/la37_c4.py, la37_report4.py. A second, independent detector: if two signs are doublets, a class-bigram model that writes them as one class (P(s|prev) = P(class(s)|class(prev)) x P(s|class(s)), add-k) predicts held-out words as well as or better than the unmerged model. Gain of every single-pair merge in bits per 1,000 held-out sign tokens, 5-fold by document; frequency-matched percentile. Controls: LB full, 5 LB draws at LA size, planted token doublets in LA (rate 0.4, 6 hosts). | LB full: doublet AUC 0.79 raw (0.68 frequency-matched), same C 0.45 / 0.66, same V 0.50 / 0.59 (raw gains favour rare and *-signs). LB at LA size: doublet AUC 0.58-0.82 (mean 0.71), same C 0.57-0.63. Planted LA: percentile 0.977-1.000, rank 1-47 of 2,080 pairs. LA: merge gain agrees with the context score T (Spearman 0.54 on frequency-matched gains; LB 0.35). Outside check: LB-derived same-consonant pairs gain more (AUC 0.61, P 0.0001 under 10,000 label permutations); same vowel no (0.48, P 0.84). Cycle-2/3 candidates: KU~KI 0.96, SI~TI 0.95, TI~TE 0.94, RE~ME 0.93 frequency-matched percentile; A~DA 0.69, A~PA 0.61, A~KU 0.31, NA~NE 0.68. | Grade A (method): held-out merge gain detects LB doublets more specifically than context similarity does (doublet 0.79 vs same C 0.45 raw). Grade B (outside check): in LA it also follows LB-derived consonants. The word-final candidates SI~TI, TI~TE, RE~ME and KU~KI pass both detectors; the word-initial A~DA, A~PA, A~KU fail the second one and are demoted (they are a word-initial slot effect, not interchangeable signs). |
| la37:LA-37.4b | Massive random guessing: 20,000 random hypotheses of 4 merged pairs scored on document part A (trained on the other two parts); the top 200 re-tested on held-out part B against 200 random hypotheses (CLES = share of random >= best). Control: LB full. | LB: best 200 re-test -14.1 vs random -52.8 bits/1000 (CLES 0.022): selection transfers. LA: -25.4 vs -39.0 (CLES 0.29): weak transfer. LA survivors (pairs most often in re-test winners): TA~TO 12, NA~TA 10, NA~DA 9, SI~TI 8, DA~E 7. | Grade B (calibrated negative at hypothesis level): random merge sets that help on one third of the LA tablets help only weakly on another third, unlike LB. No merge set is claimed. SI~TI recurs in the survivors (C, below). |

Grade C: SI~TI, TI~TE, RE~ME as alternating word-final signs (KU~KI is the KU-RO/KI-RO pair). |
| la37:LA-37.final | FIND THE DOUBLETS (la37; tools/la37_common.py, la37_c1.py, la37_c2.py, la37_c2b.py, la37_c3.py, la37_c3b.py, la37_c4.py, la37_report1.py, la37_report4.py; logs loops/la37_cycle1-4.txt). Every pair of the 64 commonest LA signs (>= 8 tokens in 2+ sign words; 2,016 pairs) scored blind for interchangeability: T = rank average of PPMI context cosine, the Voynich v35 split-half index, and degree-normalised one-slot swaps; a second detector scores held-out class-bigram gain when the pair is merged (plus 20,000 random 4-pair merge hypotheses with held-out re-test). Search correction by replication in both halves of 20 random document splits. Nulls: frequency-matched pairs; within-word sign shuffle; label permutation. Controls: Linear B KN+PY blind (16 known doublet pairs, same C, same V), LB at LA size (10 draws) and length-matched (10 draws), planted LA doublets (token free variation at 0.15/0.25/0.4, site-group, word-type, word-initial; 6 hosts). Independent check: blind la21 rows. Outside check only: LB-derived values. Web check: earlier distributional LA work compares LA with LB sign values (Packard; SigLA); none searches all pairs blind for LA doublets with LB and planted calibration. | Controls: LB doublets and same-consonant pairs rank at the top (AUC 0.78 / 0.78; top 20 70 % related vs 23.5 %); at LA size 0.69 and 57.5 %; merge gain picks doublets specifically (0.79 vs same C 0.45). Replication flags 2-8 pairs in 20/20 LA-sized LB draws (precision 0.74; mostly same-consonant vowel alternations, 0 true doublets). Planted free-variation doublets found 6/6 (0.4), 5/6 (0.25), 4/6 (0.15); site-split 5/6; doublets fixed per word 0/6 and word-initial-only 0/6 (blind spots). The within-word shuffle null kills planted doublets too (0/24), so it was dropped as the test. LINEAR A: 0 replicated pairs. Near candidates: A~DA, A~PA, KU~KI (0.4), TI~TE (0.2). Rows: T follows the blind la21 rows (Spearman 0.32 vs LB 0.39; shuffled 0.09) but rows are context-derived, so this is consistency only. LB-derived values: same C AUC 0.67 (P 0.0001), same V 0.555 (P 0.005), equal to LB's own at LA size. Writer/position split test: fails its LB control (LB doublets show no split), so uninformative; minimal pairs of the candidates sit at word edges and never share a document (except KU-RO/KI-RO). Merge gain confirms SI~TI, TI~TE, RE~ME, KU~KI (>= 0.93 percentile) and demotes A~DA, A~PA, A~KU (0.31-0.69). Random merge sets transfer weakly in LA (CLES 0.29) vs LB (0.02). | NOT CRACKED. Grade A (method): blind distributional doublet detection works on Linear B and on planted LA doublets. Grade B (calibrated negative): Linear A has no free-variation or writer-split doublet among its 64 commonest signs (< 3/6 at use rate >= 0.25 could hide), and fewer freely alternating pairs than any LB-sized sample (0 vs 2-8, 20/20). Grade B (outside check): LA interchangeability follows LB-derived consonants as strongly as LB's does at the same size. Grade C: SI~TI, TI~TE, RE~ME alternate word-finally (would support: both forms of one stem on one tablet or in one hand; would kill: the same pairs alternating medially as often). Killed / demoted: A~DA, A~PA, A~KU as doublets (initial-slot effect, fail the merge test). Rare optional doublets of the LB kind (a2, a3, ra2) are below detection at LA size even in LB. |
